Also verified: `replace` guard, custom `equivgen()` naming, the trivial `groups(1)` case, and that a `groups()` request exceeding the node count still surfaces Stata's own clean r(2000) error (no extra validation needed - `cluster generate` already handles it). Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwhierarchy.do` (not a new file - it already had real coverage for the default/`type()`/`context()`/`linkage()`/`dismat()`/`disnet()` paths from unit 20's own unblocking work). No `unw_core.do`/Mata core changes - pure `.ado`/doc addition built entirely on Stata's own native `cluster` suite. Certified at **L2**: the extended test plus `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` (the workflow's own upstream stages) and `nwcomponents` (the pattern this unit's output shape was explicitly modeled on) - all clean, both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 36: nwnclique/nwnclan (maximal n-clique and n-clan enumeration) - new commands

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwnclique`/`nwnclan` — new commands implementing maximal n-clique (Luce 1950) and n-clan (Mokken 1979) enumeration, reusing the existing `BronKerbosch()`/`Brute_dist()` infrastructure directly rather than new search algorithms | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Continues closing the "Cliques/k-plexes (cohesive subgroups beyond k-core)" Pending item (cliques and k-plexes done in units 29/34; k-components/lambda sets/factions/Moody-White blocks remain open). An n-clique relaxes an ordinary clique's "every pair directly tied" requirement to "every pair within geodesic distance `n` of each other in the network as a whole" - n=1 is exactly a clique, so `nwnclique` requires n>=2 and points to `nwclique` for that case, the same pattern already established for `nwkplex`'s own k=1 guard. The key implementation insight, confirmed empirically rather than just assumed: an n-clique is simply an ordinary clique of the "distance <= n" graph - so `NWdef::calculate_nclique()` builds that thresholded adjacency matrix (via `calculate_distances(0, "brute")`, the same distance routine `nwgeodesic` itself already uses) and hands it directly to the *unmodified* `BronKerbosch()` function `nwclique.ado`'s own `calculate_cliques()` already uses - no new backtracking algorithm needed at all, unlike `nwkplex`'s own generalization (which needed a genuinely different candidate-validity check). Missing/unreachable-pair distances need no special-casing, since a Mata comparison against a missing value is always false. `nwnclan` adds the n-clan refinement (Mokken 1979): an n-clique only guarantees each pair's shortest path in the *whole* network is within `n` - that path may run through a node that is not itself an n-clique member, a known weakness of the plain concept (Alba 1973). `calculate_nclan_filtered()` takes the already-enumerated maximal n-cliques and keeps only the ones whose *induced* subgraph (built from the network's true, unthresholded adjacency, restricted to just that candidate's own members) has every pair within `n` steps using only within-group ties - checked via a new `nclan_diameter_ok()` helper that reuses `Brute_dist()` directly on the induced submatrix (its own missing-diagonal/missing-unreachable-pair convention makes a single `hasmissing()`-after-zeroing-the-diagonal check plus a `max()` bound sufficient). This matches the standard, established literature treatment of n-clans: a *filtered subset* of the maximal n-cliques, not an independently re-maximized search - a maximal n-clique that fails the check is simply not reported as a clan, never replaced by some smaller clan-qualifying subset of itself. **A genuinely interesting, non-obvious finding from building this**: constructing a small, hand-computable network where a *full command-level* `nwnclique` call reports a maximal n-clique that `nwnclan` then excludes turned out to be considerably harder than expected, for a provable structural reason (documented in full, with the short proof sketch, in `cscripts/test_nwnclan.do`'s own header comment) - every node lying on a shortest bridging path between two others is automatically within `n` of every *other* node on that same path too (sub-path distances never exceed the full path's own length), so Bron-Kerbosch's own maximality search always ends up including the whole bridging path once it includes any of it, and once included, that same path *is* the within-group route the clan check needs - so it always passes. Genuine divergence needs a node whose short whole-network shortcut is blocked from clique membership by an unrelated, independently-required member - a real but more elaborate multi-branch condition not reproducible with simple hand-built bridge graphs. Verified `nclan_diameter_ok()` directly and rigorously anyway, at the Mata level, via a deliberately hand-constructed divergent case (a 6-node path plus a 2-tie hub bridging the two endpoints) that bypasses the maximality search specifically because that search is the part which keeps resolving the divergence away - confirmed both the failing (hub excluded) and passing (hub included) sides of the same check. Full command-level tests use networks where n-clique and n-clan legitimately coincide (still exercising the complete real pipeline, cross-checked directly against `nwnclique`'s own output on the same network, not just against a hand count). Also verified via a hand-derived 5-node path (n=2: exactly {A,B,C}/{B,C,D}/{C,D,E}, matching `nwkplex`'s own unrelated k=2 result on the identical network - confirmed coincidental, not a sign the two concepts collapse together in general), K4 (trivially one whole-network n-clique/n-clan at any n), a 6-node path at n=5 (the entire path qualifies as one n-clique, a basic n-monotonicity sanity check), directed-network auto-symmetrization, `generate()`/`replace`, `netlist` support, and invalid `n()`/`minsize()`. New `cscripts/test_nwnclique.do` (28 assertions) and `cscripts/test_nwnclan.do` (9 assertions, 2 of them the isolated Mata-level divergence check) plus production-mode copies - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both topical/alphabetical indexes; cross-referenced from `nwclique.ado`'s and `nwkplex.ado`'s own "See also" sections (both regenerated). This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core, but an isolated addition - `BronKerbosch()`/`Brute_dist()` themselves are reused completely unmodified, nothing pre-existing calls the new functions) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Certified per the tiered strategy at **L2**: both new tests plus `nwclique`/`nwkplex` (the closest sibling algorithms) and `nwgeodesic` (shares the `calculate_distances()` routine `calculate_nclique()` now also calls) - all clean, both modes, no unexpected failures. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 37: nwkcomponents (maximal k-component enumeration) - new command, new vertex-connectivity/max-flow Mata infrastructure

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwkcomponents` — new command implementing maximal k-component enumeration (Kanevsky 1993; the single-level case of Moody & White's (2003) cohesive blocking), backed by genuinely new Mata infrastructure (`bfs_augment()`/`maxflow_vertex_split()`/`vertex_connectivity()`/`min_vertex_cutset()`/`KComponents()`) - the package's first max-flow/vertex-connectivity computation | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the last remaining, most structurally demanding item of the "Cliques/k-plexes (cohesive subgroups beyond k-core)" Pending item family (cliques/k-plexes/n-cliques/n-clans done in units 29/34/36; lambda sets, factions, and the full multi-level Moody-White hierarchy remain open, now on a solid foundation). A k-component is a subgraph with vertex connectivity >= `k` - `k`=1 is exactly an ordinary connected component (`nwcomponents`, which already implements that case far more cheaply via plain reachability), so `nwkcomponents` requires `k`>=1 and points to `nwcomponents` for `k`=1, the same "hand off the trivial case to the cheaper existing command" pattern already established by `nwkplex` (k=1) and `nwnclique`/`nwnclan` (n=1). Unlike the clique-family commands (which all reuse the pre-existing `BronKerbosch()` backtracking), k-components required building genuinely new infrastructure from scratch: `maxflow_vertex_split()` implements the standard node-splitting reduction (Even 1979) of vertex-connectivity to ordinary max-flow (each node split into an "in" and "out" half joined by a capacity-1 edge, so routing flow through a node costs exactly one unit of vertex-cut budget), solved via a from-scratch Edmonds-Karp BFS-augmenting-path implementation (`bfs_augment()`) since Mata has no built-in flow/graph primitives at all; `vertex_connectivity()` applies Menger's theorem (the graph's own connectivity is the minimum, over every non-adjacent pair, of the minimum separating vertex set between them) via a deliberately simple O(n^2)-max-flow-calls brute force over every non-adjacent pair rather than the smaller reference-vertex subset a more optimized algorithm (Even's own) would use - the same hand-verifiability-over-asymptotic-elegance trade-off already established for `BronKerbosch()`/`is_valid_kplex()` - with a `K_n` (complete graph) special case handled separately (connectivity = n-1 by definition, since no non-adjacent pair exists for the Menger's-theorem loop to run over at all); `min_vertex_cutset()` extracts an *actual* minimum cutset (not just its size) via a residual-graph BFS after the winning pair's own max-flow is exhausted; `KComponents()` is the recursive cut-and-recurse decomposition (compute connectivity; if it already meets the target, report the whole set and stop; otherwise find a minimum cutset, remove it, split the remainder into connected components, and recurse into each `{component + cutset}` piece, re-adding the cutset to *every* resulting branch - matching Moody-White's own convention that cutpoints remain shared members of whatever cohesive sub-blocks their removal reveals, not assigned to just one side). **Proved, not just tested, a critical correctness property**: the recursive cutting could in principle infinite-loop if `min_vertex_cutset()` ever returned an empty cutset while its own node count still exceeded the target (nothing to remove, so the "remaining" set stays identical and recursion never shrinks) - `min_vertex_cutset()` only returns empty when its input is a complete graph (no non-adjacent pair exists to search over), and a short algebraic argument (documented directly in `KComponents()`'s own header comment in `unw_core.do`) shows this can never coincide with a node set that both passes the function's own size-pruning guard and fails its connectivity check, since a complete s-node graph passing the size guard (s >= k+1) automatically has connectivity s-1 >= k, taking the "already qualifies" branch instead of ever reaching the cutting logic - ruling the dangerous case out by construction, not by adding a runtime guard against it. Verified the new max-flow/connectivity primitives in isolation first, extensively, before ever wiring them into the recursive algorithm: `vertex_connectivity()`/`maxflow_vertex_split()` against a 4-node path (expect 1), a 4-cycle (expect 2), K4 (expect 3, the complete-graph special case), a disconnected graph (expect 0), K2 (expect 1), and a star (expect 1) - 6 structurally distinct hand-derived cases, all exact; `min_vertex_cutset()` against a star (expect just the center) and a path (expect one of its two valid middle nodes). Only then verified the full `calculate_kcomponents()` against two hand-derived multi-node networks worked through by hand in full: two triangles joined by a single bridge *edge* (expect the two triangles as separate, non-overlapping 2-components, since the bridge nodes' own intermediate `{C,D}` piece never reaches connectivity 2 and is correctly dropped) and a "bowtie" of two triangles sharing a single cut *node* (expect the two triangles as genuinely overlapping 2-components, sharing the cutpoint) - deliberately chosen as complementary cases (non-overlapping vs. overlapping cutset outcomes) covering both ways a k-component decomposition can resolve. Also verified: `k`=1 on a connected graph reports the whole graph as one component (cross-checked directly against `nwcomponents`' own count on the same network); `k` too large (exceeding any substructure's actual connectivity) returns a clean, empty result rather than erroring or hanging; a genuinely disconnected network splits into independent components at `k`=1 exactly like `nwcomponents` does; K4 qualifies up to `k`=3 but not `k`=4; directed-network auto-symmetrization; `generate()`/`replace`; `netlist` support; and invalid `k()`. Follows the same output shape as the rest of the cohesive-subgroup family (`nwclique`/`nwkplex`/`nwnclique`/`nwnclan`): a per-node "largest qualifying k-component size" summary variable plus the full overlapping membership matrix in `r(kcomp_matrix)` and count in `r(kcomponents)` - no `minsize()` option, unlike its siblings, since a k-component's own minimum possible size (`k`+1) already rules out the kind of trivial-small-set degeneracy `minsize()` exists to filter elsewhere. New `cscripts/test_nwkcomponents.do` (38 assertions) plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both topical/alphabetical indexes; cross-referenced from `nwcomponents.ado`'s and all four sibling cohesive-subgroup commands' own "See also" sections (all five regenerated). While regenerating `nwcomponents.sthlp`, hit the package's own known `nw_helpwriter` line-copy fragility for a **fourth** time (see that Pending row, now updated) - this time on `nwcomponents.ado`'s own pre-existing Mata body content (not this unit's own new text, unlike the two self-inflicted instances found fixing `nwhierarchy` in unit 35), confirmed via the same "crash occurs well after the doc header's own closing `***/`" pattern already established for `nwplot.ado`/`nwimport.ado` - the doc content itself (including this unit's own new `nwkcomponents` cross-reference) copied completely and correctly; only the automatic certification stamp was skipped and added by hand, since `nwcomponents`'s own test genuinely passes. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) with entirely new, previously-nonexistent capability (max-flow/vertex-connectivity - nothing pre-existing calls any of the five new functions) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Certified per the tiered strategy at **L2**: the new test plus `nwcomponents` (the k=1 sibling this command explicitly defers to) and the other four cohesive-subgroup commands (`nwclique`/`nwkplex`/`nwnclique`/`nwnclan`) - all clean, both modes, no unexpected failures. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 38: two-mode/temporal architecture initiative begins - fixed a genuine mode-persistence data-loss bug

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| First unit of a large user-requested architectural initiative (two-mode status and, eventually, temporal status as first-class, compositional network metadata rather than living in a parallel `nw2*` command family): fixed a genuine, previously-undiscovered data-loss bug where a two-mode network's actual per-node mode partition was silently destroyed on every `nwsave`/`nwuse` round-trip; populated the long-dead `description_mode1`/`description_mode2` fields | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal fix, no user-facing syntax change - `.sthlp` updates land with the broader two-mode documentation pass later in this initiative) | A thorough audit (full findings kept for the rest of this initiative, not re-derived per unit) found that two-mode metadata is **already substantially real, first-class `NWdef` state** - `is2mode`/`modes` (a per-node "1"/"2" string array)/`get_nodes_mode1()`/`get_nodes_mode2()`/`description_mode1`/`description_mode2` all already exist, populated by `nwset`'s existing `bipartite` option and the `nw2fromedge`/`nw2set` commands - contrary to what the class's own header comment claimed ("TODO: modes is not handled yet" - stale, now corrected in place). The real gap this unit closes: **that metadata was never actually being persisted**. Confirmed directly via a save/reload round-trip probe before writing any fix: a network built as 2 mode-1 + 3 mode-2 nodes came back as 5 mode-1 + 0 mode-2 nodes after `nwsave`/`nwuse` - `nwsave`'s own edgelist-export step (`nwtoedge ... ignore2mode`) discards mode information by design (it only captures ties), and nothing else ever wrote the `modes' array out as real saved data at all; only the bare is-two-mode yes/no flag (`_nw_twomode`) survived. Fixed with two new `NWdef` methods, `get_modes_labeled_string()`/`set_modes_from_labeled_string()` (`unw_core.do`), serializing mode membership as "label=mode,label=mode,..." pairs keyed by each node's own label rather than bare position - confirmed necessary, not just cautious, by directly observing during testing that `nwuse`'s own reload path (`nwfromedge`, rebuilding the network fresh from the saved edgelist) does **not** reproduce the original node order, so a positional round-trip would have silently assigned the wrong mode to the wrong node. Wired through new `nw_name.ado` options (`newmodes()`/`newmode1desc()`/`newmode2desc()`) and returns (`r(modes)`/`r(nodes1)`/`r(nodes2)`/`r(mode1desc)`/`r(mode2desc)`), and new `nwsave.ado`/`nwuse.ado` columns (`_nw_modes`/`_nw_mode1desc`/`_nw_mode2desc`) - the latter two backward-compatible by construction: `nwuse` only reads them if present (`capture confirm variable`), so a `.nwdta` file saved before this fix (simulated directly in the test by dropping the new columns from an otherwise-normal saved file) still loads cleanly, falling back to `get_modes()`'s own pre-existing lazy default (all nodes "1") rather than erroring or guessing at data that was genuinely never saved - no regression versus the network's own prior (already-broken) behaviour, since that data was unrecoverable regardless. Also fixed a second, related, previously-invisible gap while in this code: `description_mode1`/`description_mode2` (human-readable mode names, e.g. "person"/"organisation" - already displayed by `nwsummarize`) had getters but no way to actually set them from outside the class at all, so every two-mode network's own mode-name display was silently blank; now settable via the same `nw_name.ado` options. New `cscripts/test_nw_twomode_metadata.do` (31 assertions - basic round-trip with per-label verification, a plain one-mode network confirmed unaffected, the legacy-file backward-compatibility case, and multiple two-mode networks in one save call staying independent) plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Certified at **L2**: the new test plus `nwsave`/`nwuse`'s own existing tests, `nwsummarize` (reads the mode-description fields this unit populates), and the full `nw2*` family (`nw2degree`/`nw2project`/`nw2set`/`nw2fromedge`/`nw2toedge`/`nw2clustering`) - all clean, both modes (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure, confirmed via the exact failure location, unrelated to this fix). No `unw_core.do` structural changes beyond the two new, purely additive serialization methods - nothing pre-existing calls them, so no broader blast radius. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 39: nwset gains a `twomode` edgelist option - two-mode declaration reachable from nwset itself

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwset ..., twomode` - declares a two-mode network directly from an edgelist of id variables (the literal syntax the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative asked for); documented the pre-existing but previously-undocumented `bipartite` option alongside it | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Second unit of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative (see unit 38's own row and `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s tracking section for the full initiative). The exact requested shape (`nwset person organisation, twomode`) did not exist inside `nwset.ado` itself - only as the separate `nw2fromedge` command. Rather than reimplementing that logic a second time, `nwset`'s new `twomode` branch is a thin, early dispatch that delegates straight to `nw2fromedge` (unchanged, still directly callable) - matching exactly how `nwset`'s own pre-existing `edgelist` option already reaches `nwfromedge` for the one-mode case. Deliberately kept as a genuinely distinct option from `bipartite` (not folded into it) rather than combined, since the two options declare input shapes that cannot be told apart from a bare `varlist` alone: `bipartite` takes a Mata matrix or a *wide* affiliation-matrix `varlist` (each variable = one mode-1 node, each observation = one mode-2 node); `twomode` takes an edgelist of two (or three, for a valued network) id variables, one row per tie - combining both options is rejected with an explicit, immediate error rather than guessed at. The value variable is a third *positional* variable, matching the established convention `nwfromedge`/`nw2fromedge` already use for the one-mode case, deliberately **not** a new `weight()` option - the user's own suggested example syntax used `weight()`, but the existing package convention was judged the better fit here (per the user's own explicit authorization to deviate from the illustrative syntax while preserving the substantive principles), avoiding a second, inconsistent way to say the same thing. `twomode` also surfaced (via `nw2fromedge`, unchanged) that mode descriptions are already auto-derived from the two variable names used (e.g. `person`/`institution`) - confirmed working, not something this unit needed to add. Filled a genuine, adjacent documentation gap while in this file: `bipartite` had **zero** doc-header coverage at all before this unit despite being a long-standing, working option - now documented alongside `twomode` in a new "4. Declare a two-mode network" section (also fixing a pre-existing doc-header section-numbering bug found in the process - two unrelated sections were both labelled "2."). New `cscripts/test_nwset_twomode.do` (18 assertions - basic edgelist declaration with mode-count/description checks, valued two-mode, the `twomode`+`bipartite` conflict error, wrong-variable-count error, and two regression guards confirming ordinary one-mode `nwset` calls and the pre-existing `bipartite` matrix form are both completely unaffected) plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Certified at **L2**: the new test plus `nwset`'s own pre-existing test (confirmed unaffected) and the `nw2fromedge`/`nw2project`/`nw2set` family this now delegates to/sits alongside - all clean, both modes. No `unw_core.do` changes - pure `.ado` addition. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 40: nwdegree gains two-mode awareness - first migrated Category-A command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwdegree` on a two-mode network now automatically redirects to `nw2degree` (the correct native bipartite degree formula) instead of silently computing a meaningless one-mode degree on the raw bipartite adjacency matrix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal behaviour change surfaced via a runtime note, not new syntax - `.sthlp` updates land with the broader two-mode documentation pass later in this initiative) | Third unit of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative (see unit 38/39's own rows and `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s tracking section) - the first migrated "Category A" command (a command with a natural, correct two-mode definition that an ordinary `nw*` call should use automatically, per the initiative's Part 3 classification). Confirmed via direct inspection before this unit that `nwdegree` had **zero** two-mode awareness at all: it silently ran the ordinary one-mode degree formula on a bipartite network's own raw (structurally block-zeroed) adjacency matrix, producing a plausible-looking but meaningless number with no error or note whatsoever - a genuine, previously-undiscovered gap, not a hypothetical one. Fixed by reusing the exact, already-shipped, already-proven redirect pattern from `nwclustering.ado` (flagged as the initiative's own "model example" in `docs/NETWORK_TYPE_MATRIX.md`) rather than inventing a new mechanism: inside `nwdegree.ado`'s existing per-network netlist loop, immediately after `nw_syntax` (which already exports an `is2mode` local to every caller - confirmed reusable infrastructure, nothing new needed there), a new branch checks `is2mode` and, if true, calls `nw2degree` on that one network instead and `continue`s the loop - so a mixed netlist (`nwdegree onemode twomodenet`) correctly dispatches each network to its own correct formula within a single call. This is explicitly **not** automatic projection (nothing here collapses the two-mode network down to one mode) - it is selecting the correct **native** two-mode formula, the literal Category-A behaviour the initiative's Part 3 calls for, and is architecturally distinct from a Category-C command (no native bipartite definition), which must instead require an explicit `projection()`-style request. Used `{txt}` (not `{err}`) styling for the redirect note, deliberately deviating from `nwclustering.ado`'s own precedent, which uses `{err}` for what is normal, expected, non-error behaviour - judged inconsistent with the user's own explicit instruction not to call ordinary automatic-correct-behaviour a "warning"; `nwclustering.ado` itself was left unchanged (out of scope for this unit) but this stylistic correction should be applied there too in a future pass. `nwdegree`-specific options with no bipartite equivalent (`alpha()`/`isolates`/`standardize`/`in()`/`out()`/`outputoff`) are not silently dropped: a second note explicitly lists any such option that was present but ignored, rather than pretending they were honoured. New `cscripts/test_nwdegree_twomode.do` (basic redirect producing `nw2degree`-shaped output rather than one-mode output; a direct value cross-check confirming the redirect path and a bare `nw2degree` call on the identical network produce byte-identical results, not merely "some plausible number"; the ignored-options note; a plain one-mode network confirmed completely unaffected; and a mixed one-mode+two-mode netlist call confirmed to dispatch each network correctly within the same call) plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly against the compiled `.mlib`. Certified at **L2**: the new test plus `nwdegree`'s own full pre-existing test suite (confirmed zero regression), `nw2degree`'s own test (the redirect target), and `nwclustering`'s own test (the sibling command this pattern was copied from) - all clean, both modes. No `unw_core.do` changes - pure `.ado` addition, reusing existing `is2mode`/`nw_syntax` infrastructure. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 41: nw2fromedge node-mode assignment bug - fixed a real data-corruption bug, closed the get_density() "gap" that never actually existed

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed `nw2fromedge` (the two-mode edgelist ingester behind `nwset ..., twomode`, unit 39) silently assigning the wrong mode to nodes whenever the two edgelist variables' labels don't sort into two clean alphabetical blocks - a genuine, previously-undiscovered data-corruption bug, not a display or cosmetic issue | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal fix, no syntax change) | Fourth unit of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative - started as the next queued item on `docs/ROADMAP.md` (re-auditing `NWdef::get_density()`, flagged by unit 38's own audit as having "the wrong, one-mode denominator" on bipartite data), and immediately surfaced something much more serious. Direct hand-verification (this initiative's own standing discipline - never trust an audit finding or a memory summary without re-deriving it against real data) built a 3-person/2-institution two-mode network and computed density by hand: 3 ties out of a possible 3×2=6 gives density 0.5, but the network returned 0.333. Tracing the discrepancy (not guessing) found the real cause was **not** in `get_density()` at all - `get_density()`'s formula (`sum(*e)/sum(*e!=.)`) is, in fact, already exactly correct for bipartite networks, confirmed by two independent hand-computable cases afterward (a fully-connected 3×2 network giving exactly 1.0, a partially-connected one giving exactly 0.5) - because `clean_matrix_2mode()` already masks same-mode cells to Mata missing (`.`), and Mata's `sum()` (confirmed via a direct probe: `sum((1,0,.\1,.,0\.,1,1))` returns `4`, not missing) already skips missing values, so the existing formula was silently doing the right thing all along. The actual bug: `nw2fromedge.ado`'s own node-mode assignment logic assumed "the first N1 nodes (by node *index*) belong to group 1, the rest to group 2" - but nodes are numbered by `nwfromedge` (which `nw2fromedge` delegates to for the underlying edgelist-to-matrix conversion) by sorting the *combined* label set of *both* edgelist variables together, so group-1 and group-2 labels land at interleaved node indices whenever they don't happen to sort into two separial alphabetical blocks - not a rare edge case, just any dataset where a mode-2 label alphabetically precedes a mode-1 label (confirmed directly: "LiU"/"Oxford" institutions and "Peter"/"Thomas"/"Tim" persons - "LiU"/"Oxford" both sort before "Peter" alphabetically, so the old code assigned mode 1 to `{LiU, Oxford, Peter}` and mode 2 to `{Thomas, Tim}`, scrambling institutions and persons together rather than keeping them separate). This silently corrupted every downstream two-mode computation for any affected dataset - not just density, but which nodes `nw2degree`/`nw2clustering`/`nw2project`/`nwplot`'s mode-based coloring, and unit 40's own `nwdegree` redirect all treat as mode 1 vs mode 2. Fixed by recording group 1's actual distinct label set (as Mata state, which survives the `preserve`/`restore` needed to reach the label-count) before calling `nwfromedge`, then, once the network's own node order is known, assigning each node's mode by direct label lookup rather than position - a new standalone Mata function `modes_from_labels()` (`unw_core.do`, alongside the existing `first_index_match()` helper it calls) rather than an inline `.ado`-level loop, after confirming empirically that Stata's one-line interactive `mata:` command form does not reliably parse a `for`-loop containing a nested `if`/assignment (a `{...}`-braced compound body triggered "invalid expression"; even an unbraced nested `if` triggered "unexpected end of line") - a real Mata interactive-mode parsing limitation worth remembering, not a logic bug, confirmed via isolated minimal repro before working around it. Also handled the case where both edgelist variables are numeric: `nwfromedge` builds those nodes' final labels as `"n" + the number` (not the bare number), so the label set collected for matching purposes must apply that exact same transform, or the lookup would silently fail to match anything and every node would fall back to the default mode - confirmed via the pre-existing (already broken in a different way, see below) `cscripts/test_nw2fromedge.do`'s own fully-numeric test case. Fixing this surfaced a downstream test-baseline issue, not a new bug: `cscripts/test_nw2project.do`'s five `stat()`-formula assertions (`min`/`max`/`mean`/`sum`/`minmax`) were hardcoded to match `nw2fromedge.ado`'s own documented worked example (Peter/Thomas sharing LiU/Oxford) - but the test called `nw2project ..., project(2)`, which, under the old mode-assignment bug, accidentally computed the *persons* projection (because the bug had already swapped `mode1`⇄`mode2` for that exact dataset) even though `project(2)` nominally means "project onto mode 2". With the bug fixed, `project(2)` now correctly means what it says (institutions), so the test's own `project()` argument was corrected to `project(1)` (persons) to keep testing the documented worked example's actual values, unchanged - re-verified directly against the fixed code, all five values (1/7/14/3.5/5) match exactly; a sixth `inst_test` assertion was flipped from `project(1)` to `project(2)` for the same reason, now correctly testing the institution-level projection its own name always claimed to test. New `cscripts/test_nw2fromedge.do` gained direct per-node mode-membership assertions (not just mode *counts*, which the old, buggy code could satisfy by accident) confirming the fix on both the interleaved-alphabetical-order case and the both-numeric-variables case. Certified at **L2/L3** (justified by blast radius: `unw_core.do` + the two-mode ingestion path underlying `nwset ..., twomode`, `nw2project`, `nw2degree`, `nw2clustering`, `nwdegree`'s redirect, `nwsummarize`, and `nwsave`/`nwuse` persistence) - full sweep of `test_nw2clustering`/`test_nw2degree`/`test_nw2fromedge`/`test_nw2project`/`test_nw2set`/`test_nw2toedge`/`test_nw_twomode_metadata`/`test_nwdegree_twomode`/`test_nwset_twomode`/`test_nwsummarize`/`test_nwuse`/`test_nwsave`/`test_nwdegree`/`test_nwclustering`/`test_nwset`/`test_nwplot`, both dev mode and production mode against a freshly rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). Also discovered and closed, as a directly-adjacent housekeeping fix rather than scope creep, a real version-control gap: `nw2fromedge.ado` itself, plus its sibling `nw2clustering.ado`/`nw2set.ado`/`nw2toedge.ado` and their `cscripts/` tests, were sitting on disk fully working and already relied upon by several previously-committed harmonisation units (24, 38, 39, 40) but had never actually been committed to git (in `nw2fromedge.ado`'s case, the committed version was a completely different, pre-modernization implementation from a much older commit; the three siblings were entirely untracked) - all four `.ado` files and four test files committed alongside this fix, so the repository's tracked state now actually matches what has been tested and shipped. A broader such gap remains (several other already-shipped commands - `nwbridges`/`nwappend`/`nwshared`/`nwsimmelian`/`nwnode`/`nwnoderename`/`nwplotjs`/`nwpreserve`/`nwrestore` and their tests - are also untracked) - out of scope for this unit (unrelated to the two-mode/temporal initiative), flagged in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s Pending section as its own housekeeping item rather than fixed silently here. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 42: nw2project gains count/binary/jaccard/cosine + provenance metadata - two more real pre-existing bugs found and fixed along the way

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nw2project`'s `stat()` gains four new shared-neighbor-structure options (`count`/`binary`/`jaccard`/`cosine`), available on both valued and unvalued source networks; projected networks now record their own provenance (source network, mode, stat used), persisted through `nwsave`/`nwuse` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fifth unit of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative (Part 4 of the user's own specification: "support binary/shared-affiliation/weighted/jaccard/cosine projections eventually; projected network should get provenance metadata where sensible"). The four new `stat()` options reuse the exact same shared-neighbor traversal `nw2project_edges()` already builds (one extra `degvec` accumulator recording each selected node's far-level/affiliation degree, populated in the same pass) rather than a second pass over the network - `jaccard` = shared/(deg_i+deg_j-shared), `cosine` = shared/sqrt(deg_i*deg_j), `count`/`binary` reuse the pre-existing `sharedcount` matrix directly. All four verified against hand-computed values on two cases: full neighbor overlap (all four options land at their natural extremes: count=2, binary=1, jaccard=1, cosine=1) and a partial-overlap case (deg 3 vs deg 2, 2 shared: jaccard=2/3, cosine=2/√6≈0.8165) - both computed by hand before writing the assertions, not reverse-engineered from the code's own output. Provenance: a new `NWdef` field (`provenance`, string scalar) with `get_provenance()`/`set_provenance()`, wired through `nw_name.ado`'s `newprovenance()` option/`r(provenance)` return (mirroring `newmodes()`'s own unit-38 pattern exactly) and `nwsave.ado`/`nwuse.ado`'s `_nw_provenance` column (backward-compatible via the same `capture confirm variable` graceful-legacy-file pattern already used for `_nw_modes`); `nwsummarize` displays it when non-blank. `nw2project.ado` captures the source network's own name and mode description *before* `netobj` is reassigned to the newly-created projected network (it would otherwise be gone the instant the pointer is repointed), building a note like "projected from network structnet, mode 1 (ego), stat=minmax". Verifying the save/reload round-trip (not just the immediate in-session value, since the two are backed by genuinely different code paths) surfaced two further real, pre-existing bugs, neither related to provenance itself, that had to be fixed before the round-trip could even be tested: (1) `NWdef::create_by_name_sparse()` (used by `nw2project` to build the projected network) calls `zap()` internally, which unconditionally blanks every field including `name` - and nothing in `nw2project.ado` ever re-set it afterward, unlike `nwset.ado`'s own use of the dense sibling `create_by_name()`, which immediately calls `set_name()` right after for exactly this reason. The projected network's *container-level* name list (`nw.nws.names`) still correctly listed it, so every explicit-netname call worked fine and this went unnoticed - but the NWdef object's own internal `name` field stayed blank, and any *bare*/"current network" code path that reads it directly (`pcurrent->get_name()`) broke: confirmed via a direct probe that `nwsave`'s own internal bare `nwload` call crashed with "subscript invalid" trying to evaluate `pdefs[0]` after `get_index_of_current()` silently found no match against the blank name. Fixed with one added line (`` `netobj'->set_name("`name'") `` immediately after `create_by_name_sparse()`, mirroring `nwset.ado`'s pattern). (2) Separately, `nwuse.ado`'s own per-network reload loop called `` nwname `_nw_netname', ... `` - `` `_nw_netname' `` is a LOCAL-macro lookup for a local literally named `_nw_netname`, which is never defined anywhere in that loop (`_nw_netname` is the *Stata variable* holding each row's saved network name; the correct local, already computed one line earlier for exactly this purpose, is `` `n' ``). An undefined local silently expands to empty text rather than erroring, so this call had *always* been invoking `nwname`/`nwsym` bare - operating on whatever network happened to be "current" at that instant, not explicitly the network this loop iteration was reloading. Found via `set trace on` while tracing why provenance wasn't surviving a round-trip; confirmed this same bug silently affected every other `newXXX()` field this same call passes (`new2mode`/`newselfloop`/`newtitle`/`newvalued`/`newmodes`/`newmode1desc`/`newmode2desc`) too, though it happened to go unnoticed for those in existing tests because "current" already matched the intended network by coincidence in every previously-tested scenario. Fixed by replacing both occurrences (`nwname`/`nwsym`) with the correct `` `n' ``. A third, smaller issue surfaced in the same investigation: the provenance note's own first draft wrote the source network's name inside literal apostrophes ("network 'mynet'"), which corrupted the string once it passed through a further round of macro re-expansion (`nwname` forwarding its full `` `0' `` to `nw_name`) - not a bug in existing code, but a mistake in this unit's own first attempt, fixed by dropping the literal apostrophes from the note's format before it ever shipped. New test coverage added directly to `cscripts/test_nw2project.do` (kept in the same file rather than a new one, since it extends the same command): all four new `stat()` options on both the full-overlap and partial-overlap cases; provenance set immediately after projection; provenance surviving a real `nwsave`/`nwuse` round-trip end to end; a plain one-mode network's provenance confirmed to stay blank and its own save/reload confirmed unaffected (this also exercises the `nwuse.ado` local-macro bugfix on an ordinary, non-projected network). Certified at **L2/L3** (justified by blast radius: `nw2project`, `unw_core.do`, and the `nwsave`/`nwuse`/`nw_name` persistence chain shared by every other metadata field routed through it) - full sweep of `test_nw2clustering`/`test_nw2degree`/`test_nw2fromedge`/`test_nw2project`/`test_nw2set`/`test_nw2toedge`/`test_nw_twomode_metadata`/`test_nwdegree_twomode`/`test_nwset_twomode`/`test_nwsummarize`/`test_nwuse`/`test_nwsave`/`test_nwdegree`/`test_nwclustering`/`test_nwset`/`test_nwplot`, both dev mode and production mode against a freshly rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). `nw2project.sthlp` regenerated cleanly via `nw_helpwriter` (doc header updated with the four new `stat()` options and a provenance note). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 43: temporal metadata groundwork - nwset time()/interval()/eventtime(), new nwattime command, three real bugs found along the way

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwset` gains `time()`/`interval()`/`eventtime()` options declaring snapshot/interval/event temporal metadata on an edgelist network; new `nwattime` command produces an ordinary static graph view of the ties active at a given timepoint; `nwsummarize`/`nwname` display/return the new metadata | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Sixth unit of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative - Part II of the user's own specification ("temporal information should also become part of the network metadata, but only as groundwork for future temporal-network functionality... do NOT build a full temporal-network modelling system yet"). New `NWdef` fields (`unw_core.do`): `istemporal`/`temporaltype` plus, per the three distinguished semantics, `timevar`/`edgetime` (snapshot - one timepoint per tie), `startvar`/`endvar`/`edgestart`/`edgeend` (interval - `start<=t<end`, the documented convention), `eventtimevar`/`eventlist` (event - timestamped relational events stored as raw sender/receiver/eventtime triplets, deliberately **never** folded into the ordinary `edge` matrix as persistent ties, matching the user's own explicit instruction not to pretend an event stream is an ordinary graph). Two new standalone Mata helpers populate per-edge time values by resolving each row's own node LABELS to indices (`build_edge_value_matrix()` for snapshot/interval, `build_eventlist()` for event) - not by row/node position, for the same reason unit 41's `nw2fromedge` mode-assignment fix already established (nodes are numbered by sorting the combined label set of both edgelist variables together, so a positional assumption is wrong). `nwattime` implements the requested "temporal network → select edges active at t → static graph view → ordinary nw algorithm" conceptual model via three further standalone Mata slicing functions (`nwattime_slice_snapshot`/`_interval`/`_event`) returning the same (i,j,weight) triplet shape `nw2project`'s own `set_edge_from_triplets()` consumer already expects; the resulting network is completely ordinary and was directly confirmed usable by an unmodified, unrelated command (`nwdegree`) with zero special-casing. Event slicing is the one place an event network is allowed to become a persistent graph at all, and only because the exact instant was explicitly requested via `at()` - matching the specification's own "acceptable for static commands to reject event networks without explicit window" language: this command **is** the explicit request. `nwattime` on a non-temporal network is rejected with a clear, explicit error (new `nw_syntax.ado` exports, `istemporal`/`temporaltype`, mirroring the existing `is2mode` export exactly, reusable by future temporal-aware command migrations). Composability with `twomode`/`bipartite` is explicitly **not** attempted in this pass (a genuine, acknowledged gap, not silently mishandled - combining them now produces a clear, immediate error naming the limitation, checked in both the `twomode` and the temporal dispatch branches after the first version of this check only guarded one direction and let `nwset ego alter, time(t1) twomode` through unblocked, caught by the new test's own mutual-exclusivity assertion). Found and fixed **four** real bugs while building and verifying this, none hypothetical: (1) a genuine Mata parsing limitation (not a logic bug) - Stata's one-line interactive `mata:` command form does not reliably parse a `for`-loop with a braced multi-statement body (confirmed via isolated minimal repro, same limitation unit 41 already found for a different function) - worked around by moving the label-to-index resolution into the standalone `build_eventlist()`/`nwattime_slice_event()` Mata functions instead of an inline loop; (2) a Mata class-reference ordering bug in this unit's own first attempt - the three `nwattime_slice_*()` functions were initially placed *before* `NWdef`'s own class declaration in `unw_core.do` (early in the file, alongside `modes_from_labels()`/`build_edge_value_matrix()`, which don't need the class type at all), causing "class nw_def undefined" - fixed by relocating them to immediately after the class declaration closes, the same position `nw2project.ado`'s own external `nw2project_edges()` implicitly relies on being reachable by (different file, later compile); (3) a genuine, previously-latent correctness bug, confirmed via a direct probe (`class{real scalar flag}; t=cls(); if(t.flag) printf("truthy")` prints "truthy" for a never-set field) - an uninitialized Mata `real scalar` class field defaults to missing (`.`), not 0, and Mata's `if()` treats `.` as truthy, so this unit's first `is_temporal_boolean()`/`is_temporal()` implementation (`if (istemporal) return("true")`) would have reported **every** ordinary, never-declared-temporal network in the entire package as temporal - caught before shipping only because `nwattime`'s own non-temporal rejection test unexpectedly passed through instead of erroring; fixed by switching to the already-established, already-correct `is2mode`-style explicit `istemporal == 1` check (`is_2mode()` already uses exactly this pattern, confirmed by reading it, not assumed) - a real defensive-check convention worth applying to any *future* boolean-flag field added to `NWdef`, not just this one; (4) `nwset.ado`'s own new temporal dispatch block left a stray nonzero `_rc` behind after a **successful** `time()`/`eventtime()` call, discovered via a genuinely confusing symptom (the exact same `nwset` call reported `_rc==0` under `set trace on` but `_rc==111` without it) - root cause was an unconditional `capture mata: mata drop `__tval2'` cleanup line legitimately failing (`__tval2'` is only created on the `interval()` branch) and `capture` swallowing the resulting error without anything afterward resetting `_rc` before `exit`; fixed by giving `__tval2'` a harmless placeholder value unconditionally so the final cleanup drop no longer needs `capture` at all. `nwsummarize` and `nw_name.ado`/`nwname` (`r(temporal)`/`r(temporaltype)`/`r(timevar)`/`r(startvar)`/`r(endvar)`/`r(eventtimevar)`) both extended to surface the new metadata, closing the "extend `nwsummarize` for temporal once it exists" item `docs/ROADMAP.md` had been tracking since unit 38. New `cscripts/test_nwset_temporal.do` (snapshot/interval/event declaration with per-edge value correctness checked by direct label-resolved Mata lookups against hand-computable input data; mutual exclusivity between `time`/`interval`/`eventtime`; the `twomode`+temporal combination error, checked from *both* dispatch directions; wrong-variable-count error; a plain non-temporal network's own regression guard, including the specific missing-value-truthy trap this unit found) and `cscripts/test_nwattime.do` (snapshot exact-match slicing cross-checked against an unmodified `nwdegree` call on the result; interval boundary cases matching the documented `start<=t<end` convention exactly, including an open-ended/missing-end tie confirmed still active far in the future; event exact-timestamp slicing with a same-instant duplicate-event dedup check; the non-temporal-network rejection; provenance correctness on the resulting static view) - both pass cleanly, plus production-mode copies against a freshly rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib`. Certified at **L2/L3** (justified by blast radius: `unw_core.do`, `nwset`, `nw_syntax` - reused package-wide - `nw_name`, `nwsummarize`, and a new command) - full sweep of `test_nw2clustering`/`test_nw2degree`/`test_nw2fromedge`/`test_nw2project`/`test_nw2set`/`test_nw2toedge`/`test_nw_twomode_metadata`/`test_nwdegree_twomode`/`test_nwset_twomode`/`test_nwsummarize`/`test_nwuse`/`test_nwsave`/`test_nwdegree`/`test_nwclustering`/`test_nwset`/`test_nwplot`/`test_nwset_temporal`/`test_nwattime`, both dev mode and production mode - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). `nwset.ado`'s doc header gained a new "5. Declare a temporal network" section (syntax forms, the three semantics, composability limitation documented explicitly, not silently implied); `nwattime.sthlp` written from scratch; both regenerated cleanly via `nw_helpwriter` with a "last certified" stamp. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 44: nwplot/nwdegree bare r(99) after a second nwplot/nwdegree call - a real, reported regression, root-caused and fixed

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed `nwplot` silently leaving `_degree`/`_outdegree`/`_indegree`/`_isolates` stranded in the Stata dataset after its own internal degree/isolates computation (used by the `mdsclassical` layout, the default for any network with more than 50 nodes), which then collided with `nwdegree`'s own "variable already exists" guard on the very next call - reported by the user as "nwplot and nwdegree fail with a bare `r(99)` after creating a second network", explicitly not accepted as a diagnosis and traced to its actual source per their own instruction | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal fix, no user-facing syntax change; error message clarity improved, not documented behaviour) | Not part of the two-mode/temporal architecture initiative - a standalone, user-reported regression triaged and fixed on its own. **Root cause** (confirmed via direct, minimal probes at every step, never assumed): `nwplot.ado`'s own internal degree/isolates computation (`mdsclassical` layout block) generates temporary `_degree`/`_outdegree`/`_indegree`/`_isolates` variables via `nwdegree`, then tries to clean them up with a single compound `capture drop _isolates _degree _outdegree _indegree` command. Stata's plain `drop varlist` is all-or-nothing over its *entire* list: if even one named variable does not exist, the whole command fails and drops **nothing**, not even the variables that do exist - confirmed with a minimal, direct probe (`capture drop a b c d` with only `a`/`b` existing left both `a` and `b` undropped, `_rc==111`). Exactly one of `_degree` (undirected) or `_outdegree`/`_indegree` (directed) is ever actually created by `nwdegree` - the other name(s) never exist - so this compound drop silently failed via `capture` on **every single call**, for every network, regardless of directedness, leaving `_isolates` and whichever of `_degree`/`_outdegree`/`_indegree` *was* created stranded in the Stata dataset (an ordinary, session-wide dataset shared across all networks, not scoped to any one network) after `nwplot` returned. The very next command that tried to generate one of those same default variable names - `nwplot`'s own next invocation on *any* network, or a plain user `nwdegree` call - then hit `nwdegree.ado`'s own "variable already exists" guard (a direct `err 99`), which itself raised a bare, unexplained `r(99)` because its own diagnostic `di` line was silently swallowed by the enclosing `qui foreach netname_temp in `netname' { ... }` block (missing `noi`) - both bugs fixed together. **Crucially, this reproduces identically on a single network's own second `nwplot`/`nwdegree` call, with no second network ever created** (confirmed directly, before touching any code) - the "after creating a second network" framing in the original report was a coincidence of that specific reproduction sequence (a second `nwplot` call happened to be interleaved with a second `nwrandom` call), not evidence of a registry/sparse-backend/current-network/metadata-leakage bug. Direct, exhaustive investigation of the network registry, per-network metadata (directed/valued/two-mode/temporal), sparse backend, and named-network resolution (`nw_syntax`/`nwname` on `random`/`random_1`/`random_2` throughout) found **all of it already correct** - confirmed via extensive probes, not assumed clean; none of it needed any change. Two fixes, both narrowly scoped: (1) `nwplot.ado`'s two compound-drop lines split into one `capture drop` per variable, so a missing name is silently skipped rather than aborting the whole cleanup; (2) `nwdegree.ado`'s own "already exists" diagnostic marked `noi` so it actually reaches the user instead of being swallowed by the enclosing `qui`, plus a small adjacent typo fix (the existence-check loop referenced the never-defined local `` `_isolates' `` - plural - instead of the real `` `_isolate' `` - singular - set two lines above, meaning the isolates target variable's own existence was never actually checked at all). A broader codebase search for the same compound-drop antipattern found two more instances (`nwmovie.ado`/`nwmoviexy.ado`, `capture drop _frame_x _frame_y`) - not fixed here, out of scope for this bug (a matched coordinate pair always created/dropped together, not "exactly one of several mutually-exclusive names" like the `_degree`/`_outdegree`/`_indegree` case that guarantees the failure) - noted in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s Pending section rather than silently left unrecorded. New `cscripts/test_nwplot_multinet_regression.do`: the exact reported reproduction sequence end to end; the single-network reproduction (no second network) proving this was never a multi-network bug; directed and undirected networks both exercised above the `mdsclassical` threshold (>50 nodes); a direct check that `nwplot` never leaves `_degree`/`_outdegree`/`_indegree`/`_isolates` stranded (the actual root-cause invariant, checked directly rather than only inferred from a later command's success); three differently-sized networks with `nwdegree`/`nwplot` on each by explicit name; repeated switching (`A → B → A → C → B → A`); mixed network types (ordinary/directed/two-mode) coexisting; and a check that the "already exists" guard's own diagnostic message is not silently swallowed when it genuinely does fire (the exact defect this same unit fixed). Certified at **L2/L3** (justified by blast radius: `nwplot`, `nwdegree`, and every command that internally calls either) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode against `lib/lnwcommands.mlib`) - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure, unrelated). No `unw_core.do`/Mata changes - pure `.ado`-level fixes, no mlib rebuild needed. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 45: nwdegree directed-network indegree/outdegree silently swapped - a real, severe, previously-undiscovered correctness bug; two adjacent isolates bugs on directed networks also fixed

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed `nwdegree`'s default (no explicit `generate()`) output on a **directed** network silently storing outdegree values into the variable named `_indegree` and indegree values into the variable named `_outdegree` - the two were swapped for every single default call, package-wide, since this file's creation; fixed the two adjacent `isolates` bugs already flagged in this queue (a hard crash without `generate()`, and separately a silent data-corruption bug with a full explicit `generate()`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal correctness fix, no user-facing syntax change; doc header's own worked example already implied the fixed behaviour, was just never actually delivering it) | Found while returning to this queue's own pending item ("`nwdegree.ado`'s own `isolates` option... crashes on any directed network") - investigating it surfaced something much more severe. **Root cause**: the default word-order construction for a directed network's output names (`"_indegree _outdegree"`, word 1 = indegree, word 2 = outdegree) did not match the extraction convention used everywhere else in the same file (`local _outdegree : word 1 of ...`, `local _indegree : word 2 of ...` - word 1 = outdegree, word 2 = indegree, matching this file's own doc header: "the next example saves the out- and indegree centrality in the variables myout and myin"). Confirmed directly, empirically, before touching any code: a hand-built 4-node directed star (A→B, A→C, A→D - node A's true outdegree is 3, indegree 0) came back with `_indegree[1]==3`, `_outdegree[1]==0` - exactly swapped. The centralization `r()`-results (`r(indg_central)`/`r(outdg_central)`) were never affected, since those are computed straight from the underlying Mata vectors and never routed through these Stata variable names at all - this is exactly why the bug went undetected: nothing in `cscripts/test_nwdegree.do` had ever checked the actual per-node `_indegree`/`_outdegree` variable values on a directed network, only the (correct) centralization scalars. Fixed by reordering the default construction to out-then-in (`"_outdegree _indegree"`, and `"_outstrength _instrength"` for the valued case), matching the extraction and the documented convention, rather than changing the extraction to match the inconsistent construction. **Two further, adjacent bugs in the same block**, both involving the `isolates` option: (1) `isolates` without an explicit `generate()` used to completely *replace* the output-name list with the single word `"_isolates"` regardless of directedness - on a directed network this left `_indegree` empty and `_outdegree` aliased to `"_isolates"`, so a later `mata: st_store(..., "`_indegree'", ...)` (not wrapped in `capture`) crashed hard passing `st_store()` an empty variable-name string - the crash this queue's own pending item described, now fixed by *appending* `_isolates` to the normal degree-name list instead of replacing it. (2) `` `_isolate' `` (the local naming the isolates target variable) always read word 1 of the name list regardless of how many degree-name words actually preceded it - correct only by accident when isolates was the *only* thing requested; broken for this file's own documented worked example (`generate(myout myin mysiolate) isolates` on a directed network) - `` `_isolate' `` would have resolved to `"myout"` (word 1, the OUTdegree name), so `capture generate `_isolate' = .` silently no-op'd (myout already existed as a real degree variable) and the isolates block's own `replace `_isolate' = ...` then silently **overwrote the user's real outdegree centrality values in "myout" with a 0/1 isolate indicator**, while `"mysiolate"` itself was never created at all - a genuine silent-data-corruption bug, confirmed directly before fixing (built the exact documented example, observed `myout` come back as a degree-values column, not the 0/1 flag it should have been). Fixed by taking the isolate name from the word position immediately after however many degree-name words actually precede it (word 3 for directed, word 2 for undirected), and by generalizing the "append `_isolates` if not enough words were given" rule to apply whenever the (default-constructed *or* user-supplied) name list is short by exactly one slot - not just the empty-`generate()` case - so a caller supplying `generate(myisolate)` alongside `isolates` on an undirected network (an existing, already-shipped test line) also gets a genuine, separate isolates variable instead of the single given name being aliased to both the degree computation and the isolate flag. That existing test line needed `replace` added, since its own default-appended `"_isolates"` name now collides (correctly, as intended) with an identically-defaulted name from the immediately preceding test call in the same file - not a regression, the same "don't silently overwrite" protection every other `nwdegree` call already has. **Also discovered, while investigating**: this exact same root cause (the `mdsclassical`-layout block's degree/isolates computation) explains a second, previously-unresolved Pending item - "calling bare `nwplot` and then `nwplot ..., layout(,lgc)` on the same live network can fail with r(99)", flagged in harmonisation unit 33 as "not reliably reproducible" - `layout(,lgc)` passes through the same `mdsclassical` block `nwplot`'s own unit-44 fix (compound-`capture drop` bug) already repaired, and this was re-verified clean across 12 alternating plain/`lgc` calls on a 200-node network plus 8 separate seeds before removing that Pending entry too, not assumed fixed by inference alone. New assertions added directly to `cscripts/test_nwdegree.do`: exact per-node `_indegree`/`_outdegree` values on the file's own existing directed-network matrix (previously only centralization scalars were checked); `isolates` without `generate()` on a directed star network (no crash, correct all-zero isolates) and on a network with one genuine isolate (correctly flagged); the full documented `generate(myout myin mysiolate) isolates` example, checking `myout` holds real outdegree values (not corrupted) and `mysiolate` exists and holds the real isolate indicator. New assertions added to `cscripts/test_nwplot.do`: 12 alternating plain/`layout(,lgc)` calls on a 200-node network, all `_rc==0`. Certified at **L2** (`nwdegree`'s own test, `nwplot`'s own test as the other consumer of this exact code path, `nwclustering`/`nw2degree` as commands that redirect into `nwdegree`) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode) - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). `nwdegree.sthlp` regenerated (doc text itself unchanged - the fix makes the already-documented behaviour actually correct, nothing new to document). No `unw_core.do`/Mata changes - pure `.ado`-level fix. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 46: nwgen's 16 dead variable-producing shortcuts - 13 implemented for real, 3 given clear errors; a pervasive nwclustering stale-`_rc` bug found and fixed along the way

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwgen VAR = fcn(netname)` - 13 of 16 previously dead variable-producing shortcut keywords (`degree(`/`outdegree(`/`indegree(`/`isolates(`/`components(`/`lgc(`/`clustering(`/`closeness(`/`farness(`/`nearness(`/`between(`/`evcent(`/`context(`) now genuinely work, dispatching to their already-existing, already-tested dedicated command; the remaining 3 (`addnodes(`/`collapse(`/`subset(`) raise a clear, immediate error instead of silently doing nothing; fixed a pervasive `nwclustering` stale-`_rc` bug found while verifying `clustering(`'s own dispatch | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Returned to this queue's own longest-standing flagged item: `nwgenerate.ado`'s `nwgenopt` vocabulary recognized 16 *variable*-producing keywords (a separate family from the *network*-producing shortcuts `large(`/`duplicate(`/etc. above, which already had real dispatch branches) but had **no dispatch branch implementing any of them** at all - a call like `nwgen x = isolates(net)` silently fell through the entire if-chain doing nothing, leaving `x` never created. This had already caused two real, previously-undiscovered bugs inside the package itself (`nwplot.ado`'s own `mdsclassical` layout used exactly three of these internally before being fixed, harmonisation unit 33, to route around the gap by calling `nwdegree`/`nwcomponents` directly instead). 13 of the 16 map cleanly, with no semantic ambiguity, to an already-tested command's own `generate()` option - implemented for real, each checked against a hand-computable 4-node directed star (A→B, A→C, A→D) and its undirected counterpart, not just "does it run without erroring": `degree(`/`outdegree(`/`indegree(` dispatch to `nwdegree` (directed networks need two of `nwdegree`'s own output slots - `degree(` itself, being directed-ambiguous, is defined as total degree, out+in summed, the same convention igraph's own default `degree()` uses for a directed graph); `isolates(` also dispatches to `nwdegree`, correctly reserving the right number of slots per directedness (relies directly on harmonisation unit 45's own fix, done earlier this session, without which this would have inherited that exact crash); `components(`/`lgc(` dispatch to `nwcomponents`; `clustering(` to `nwclustering`; `closeness(`/`farness(`/`nearness(` to `nwcloseness` (which always generates its own fixed 3-word closeness/farness/nearness set - each shortcut places the caller's variable name in the matching word position, the other two falling into throwaway tempvars); `between(` to `nwbetween` (confirmed via direct probe: `nwbetween` auto-symmetrizes by default, so a directed star's own betweenness came back identical to its undirected counterpart - not a bug, `nwbetween`'s own documented default, initially mis-predicted by hand before verifying empirically and correcting the test); `evcent(` to `nwevcent`; `context(` to `nwcontext` (its required `attribute()` option arrives via the same option-passthrough convention every other shortcut in this file already uses, e.g. `nwgen x = context(net), attribute(myvar)`). The remaining 3 do **not** naturally reduce to "one value per node" the way the other 13 do - `nwaddnodes` mutates a network's own node set, `nwsubset` produces a new *network* (not a variable), and `nwcollapse`'s own semantics need separate design work - each now raises a clear, immediate, explanatory error naming the real command to use directly, rather than guessing at a wrong mapping. **Found and fixed a real, separate, pervasive bug while verifying `clustering(`'s own dispatch**: a plain `nwclustering` call (no `symmetrize`) returned a stale nonzero `_rc` (`482`) on *every single call*, despite completing correctly and printing its own results with no error message at all - the same "`capture`'d cleanup on a conditionally-created temp object, nothing afterward resetting `_rc`" bug class already found and fixed repeatedly elsewhere this session (`nwattime`'s own predecessor investigation, `nwset`'s temporal dispatch). Root cause: `nwclustering.ado`'s own end-of-program cleanup unconditionally ran `capture nwdrop `symnet'` - but `symnet` (a `tempname`) is only ever actually created when `symmetrize` is given, which is *not* the common case; on every other call the `nwdrop` legitimately failed ("network not found"), and Stata's own `_return restore` (a low-level command for restoring held `r()`-class results, not an ordinary command) does not itself reset `_rc` the way a normal successful command would, so the stale code silently leaked out as `nwclustering`'s own return value. Fixed by only attempting the drop when `symnet` was actually created, guarded by the same condition that created it. Also found (via the same probe) that `nwclustering` returns `myclust==.` (missing, not 0) for any degree-1 node, since no triangle is possible through a single tie - confirmed to be the pre-existing, unrelated, already-correct mathematical convention (clustering coefficient is genuinely undefined below degree 2), not something this unit's fix touched or needed to touch. New assertions added directly to `cscripts/test_nwgenerate.do`: all 13 working shortcuts on both a directed and an undirected hand-built star network, with exact per-node value checks (not just "does it run"); `replace` behaviour for the one shortcut (`degree(` on a directed network) whose final combine-into-target step is a plain `gen` rather than a `generate()`-aware dispatch, and so needed its own explicit already-exists guard - found and fixed in the same pass (an earlier draft of this fix checked a dedicated `` `replace' `` local that this file's own comma-parsing scheme never actually populates - confirmed via direct probe that it is always empty regardless of what the caller passes - fixed to check the raw option text in `` `fcn_opt' `` instead, matching this file's own already-established `strpos()` convention used elsewhere in the same program); the 3 explicit-error keywords, confirming both the correct error code and that no partial/stray variable gets left behind. New assertions added to `cscripts/test_nwclustering.do`: a plain (no `symmetrize`) call's `_rc` checked directly (not just that the command "worked"), plus a `symmetrize`-requiring call confirmed still correct. Certified at **L2** (`nwgenerate`'s own test, `nwclustering`'s own test, plus `nwdegree`/`nwcomponents`/`nwbetween`/`nwevcent`/`nwcontext`/`nwcloseness` as the dispatch targets) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode) - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). `nwgenerate.ado`'s doc header gained a new section documenting all 13 working shortcuts plus the 3 explicit-error ones (previously undocumented entirely, despite being listed in the option vocabulary); `nwgenerate.sthlp`/`nwclustering.sthlp` regenerated cleanly via `nw_helpwriter` with fresh "last certified" stamps. No `unw_core.do`/Mata changes - pure `.ado`-level fixes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 47: nwgen's 8 dead network-producing shortcuts - 5 restored for real, 1 kept as a documented error, 2 found genuinely blocked by separate pre-existing bugs; two more isolated bugs fixed in nwsmall/nwpref along the way

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwgen NEWNET = fcn(...)` - 5 of `nwgenerate.ado`'s 8 previously-dead network-producing shortcut keywords (`dyadprob(`/`pref(`/`ring(`/`small(`/`transpose(`) now genuinely work, dispatching to their already-existing, already-tested command; `path(` kept as a deliberate, better-explained error; `lattice(`/`homophily(` found to be blocked by separate, pre-existing bugs in `nwlattice.ado`/`nwhomophily.ado` themselves (new Pending rows, not fixed here); fixed two further isolated bugs in `nwsmall.ado`/`nwpref.ado` discovered while restoring their own shortcuts; recalibrated one stale, pre-existing `nwpref` test assertion that had been silently calibrated against the already-fixed (unit 45) `nwdegree` indegree/outdegree swap bug | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Direct continuation of unit 46 - `nwgenerate.ado`'s *other*, older family of dead shortcuts (network-producing, `nwgenopt`'s first 14 keywords) already had a clear "not currently implemented" error for 8 of them (`dyadprob`/`homophily`/`lattice`/`path`/`pref`/`ring`/`small`/`transpose` - an earlier harmonisation-phase fix, preventing the silent-no-op symptom the *variable*-producing family had before unit 46), but none had actually been restored to working order. 5 map cleanly onto an already-tested command's own output-naming option and are now implemented for real: `dyadprob(` → `nwdyadprob` (its own `weightnet` argument is optional, unlike every other netname-based shortcut here, so no pre-validation `nw_syntax` call is forced on it); `pref(`/`ring(`/`small(` → `nwpref`/`nwring`/`nwsmall` (node-count-based, matching the already-working `random(` shortcut's own pattern); `transpose(` → `nwtranspose` (uses `generate()`, not `name()`, unlike every other shortcut here - `nwtranspose`'s own default, no-`generate()` behaviour mutates the source network *in place*, which would not fit this shortcut's "produces a new network under `newnetname`" contract at all, so `generate()` is always supplied unconditionally, confirmed directly to leave the source network untouched). `path(` is kept as a deliberate error (unchanged from before, message improved): `nwpath` can produce zero, one, or several output networks (one per shortest path found, since several can tie for shortest), and its own `name()` option is dead code (grep confirms it is declared in the syntax line but never referenced anywhere in the program body) - neither shape fits `nwgen`'s own "exactly one network per call" contract, so forcing a guess (e.g. always take path 1) would silently discard the other paths found. **Found `lattice(`/`homophily(` to be genuinely blocked by separate, pre-existing bugs in their own target commands**, not a dispatch problem: confirmed by calling each directly, bypassing this shortcut entirely - both fail identically either way. `nwlattice.ado` calls a completely missing command, `nwvalidvars` (`nwvalidvars.ado` does not exist anywhere in the repo - "command nwvalidvars is unrecognized" on every call). `nwhomophily.ado` crashes with "__000001 not found" (a Mata tempname/matrix-name mismatch, not yet root-caused beyond confirming it) on every call, including its own doc header's worked example. Neither fixed here - both are substantial, separate investigations (writing a whole missing command from scratch with unclear original semantics; root-causing an unrelated internal Mata bug) genuinely out of scope for "restore the shortcut dispatch layer" - both recorded as new Pending rows rather than silently left undiscovered or falsely claimed working. **Found and fixed two further, isolated, pre-existing bugs while restoring `pref(`/`small(`**, confirmed via a direct probe calling each target command standalone (bypassing the shortcut) before fixing, exactly reproducing the failure either way: (1) `nwsmall.ado`'s own syntax line was simply missing `name(string)` entirely, despite its own body already referencing `` `name' `` (defaulting to the hardcoded "small" every time, since the local could never actually be set by a caller) - added, matching its sibling `nwring.ado`'s own already-correct declaration. (2) `nwpref.ado`'s final `nwset` call referenced `` `prefname' ``, a local never set anywhere in the file (a plain typo for `` `name' ``, the local its own syntax line actually declares and every sibling generator already uses correctly in the identical position) - any caller's `name()` was silently discarded, the network always landing under `nwset`'s own generic default name instead. Fixing `nwpref.ado`'s naming bug had no effect on its own random-number generation (confirmed directly: the pre-existing, since-recalibrated test assertion mismatch below reproduces identically with the *original*, unfixed `nwpref.ado`, ruling out today's fix as the cause). **Recalibrated one stale assertion in the pre-existing (untracked, never-committed until this unit) `cscripts/test_nwpref.do`**: `sum _indegree; assert r(max)==2` had been silently calibrated against `nwdegree.ado`'s own pre-existing indegree/outdegree swap bug (fixed earlier this session, harmonisation unit 45) - the variable named "_indegree" used to actually hold OUTdegree values (bounded, small, since every new node forms exactly `m=2` outgoing ties, hence `max==2` looked plausible); with that bug fixed, "_indegree" now correctly holds real preferential-attachment indegree (unbounded, rich-get-richer dynamics - one heavily-preferred node can accumulate far more than `m` incoming ties), confirmed with the identical seed (123) directly against both the fixed and a temporarily-reverted copy of the current `nwdegree.ado` before recalibrating, not assumed. New assertions added to `cscripts/test_nwgenerate.do` for all 5 restored shortcuts plus `path(`'s own continued, better-explained error; new targeted regression assertions added directly to `cscripts/test_nwsmall.do`/`cscripts/test_nwpref.do` confirming `name()` now actually works (neither file's own pre-existing assertions had ever exercised it). Certified at **L2** (`nwgenerate`'s own test, `nwsmall`'s/`nwpref`'s/`nwring`'s/`nwdyadprob`'s/`nwtranspose`'s own tests, `nwdegree`'s own test as the sibling command whose earlier fix this unit's own recalibration depends on) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode) - all clean (the one `test_nwuse.do` failure is the pre-existing, already-baselined `nwwebuse` dead-URL environmental failure). `nwgenerate.ado`'s doc header updated (`path(`'s entry now explains why it is not implemented, rather than implying it might be); `nwgenerate.sthlp`/`nwpref.sthlp` regenerated cleanly via `nw_helpwriter`; `nwsmall.sthlp` hit the already-known, already-documented "too few quotes" `nw_helpwriter` crash (this unit's own new bugfix comment contains a backtick-quote pattern, landing safely after the doc header itself had already been fully and correctly copied - confirmed by inspecting the resulting file directly) - "last certified" stamp added by hand, matching the established precedent for this exact situation (`nwplot.ado`/`nwcomponents.ado`, earlier units). No `unw_core.do`/Mata changes - pure `.ado`-level fixes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 48: `nwfromedge.ado` rewired to sparse-native construction (sparse-backend migration item 1 of 2) - six real bugs found and fixed along the way, none of them pre-existing/dormant, all newly exposed by removing the dense construction path's implicit safety net

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwfromedge.ado`'s own network-construction step rewired from the old dense `make_matrix()` (a plain `J(nodes,nodes,0)` allocation, filled cell-by-cell from the same ego/alter/value triplets already sitting in Mata, then handed to `nwset`'s own dense-only `mat()` ingestion) to direct sparse-native construction (`create_by_name_sparse()` + `set_edge_from_triplets()`), mirroring the direct-construction pattern `nw2project.ado`/`nwattime.ado` already use; `make_matrix()` deleted entirely (confirmed no other live callers except the already-deprecated `deprecated/nwfromedge2.ado`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal architecture change; no user-facing syntax change, `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md` updated) | `nwset`'s own `edgelist` option is a thin wrapper dispatching straight to `nwfromedge` (`qui if "`edgelist'" != "" { qui nwfromedge `varlist', ... ; exit }`), so it inherits this rewiring for free - not a separate migration.
