This closes the single largest remaining O(N²) allocation on the package's ordinary, most-common usage path (turning a plain edgelist into a network). **Six real, previously-nonexistent bugs surfaced while hand-verifying the rewiring** (string/numeric nodes, valued/directed, `labs()`, auto-symmetrize-detection, zero-weight ties), each confirmed via direct probe/`set trace on` before fixing, never assumed: (1) a fresh Stata session's global `nw` (the single `NWs` Mata variable anchoring all network state) is never initialized until something calls `nws_create()` - `nwset.ado` already guards this for itself, but `nwfromedge.ado`, which (unlike `nw2project.ado`/`nwattime.ado`, always run against an already-existing source network) can legitimately be the very first network-creating call in a session, did not, and hit `type mismatch: transmorphic found where struct expected` on `.add()` - fixed with the same guard `nwset.ado` already uses. (2) `get_nodenames_from_string()` returns a column vector; `create_by_name_sparse()` requires a row vector - `nwset.ado`'s own call site already transposes it (`(...)'`), the new call site initially didn't - fixed to match. (3) `nw_syntax`'s own c_local exports (`directed`, `labs`, etc.) silently clobbered `nwfromedge.ado`'s own identically-named option-parsing locals, since both share the same program scope - the auto-symmetrize-detection block always saw the network's own post-construction `directed` state instead of the user's option, confirmed via `set trace on` showing `directed` flip from empty to `"true"` immediately after the `nw_syntax` call - fixed using `nw_syntax`'s own `other()` namespacing option (`other(other)` → `` `othernetobj' `` etc.), the exact convention `nwrecode.ado` already uses for this same reason (its own code comment: "the same othernetname/othernodes/otherid/otherdirected naming convention"). (4) `check_symmetry()`/`symmetrize()` (in `unw_core.do`) read/wrote the dense `edge` field directly without calling `ensure_dense_built()` first, unlike every `get_matrix*()` accessor - harmless while every network was always constructed dense-first, but silently wrong (operating on an empty/stale `edge`, `check_symmetry()` trivially returning true on two empty 0x0 matrices) once a network could be built sparse-natively and never touch `edge` at all - fixed by adding `ensure_dense_built()` to both, matching the rest of the class's own established convention. (5) `isselfloop` has no `zap()`-time default (a fresh `NWdef` leaves it at Mata's missing-scalar default, not `0`), so `ensure_dense_built()`'s own diagonal-exclusion (`if (isselfloop == 0) _diag(e,.)`) never fired unless `set_selfloop()` was called explicitly - `nwset.ado`'s dense path always does this (`set_selfloop("`selfloop'" == "selfloop")`, defaulting false); the new direct-construction call site didn't, silently including the diagonal in `get_density()` and any other diagonal-excluding stat (caught by the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwfromedge.do`'s own density assertion, off by exactly the diagonal-inclusion amount: `.222...` reported vs `.333...` expected on a 3-node directed network) - fixed by calling `set_selfloop(0)` explicitly, matching `nwset.ado`'s own default. (6) `set_edge_from_triplets()` (in `unw_core.do`) stored every `(ego, alter, weight)` triplet verbatim, including `weight == 0` rows - every dense `edge` matrix treats `0` as "no tie" for free (an unset `J(n,n,0)` cell already reads as `0`, and `get_arcs_count()`/`get_edges_count()` both explicitly exclude `*e==0` cells), but a sparse index has no such implicit background value, so an explicit zero-weight triplet silently became a *real* stored tie - caught by the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwcomponents.do` (routed through `nwset`'s `edgelist` dispatch): a value-0 edgelist row was keeping an otherwise-isolated node connected, `r(components)==1` instead of the asserted `2` - fixed by filtering `weight != 0` triplets inside `set_edge_from_triplets()` itself, so `nw2project.ado`/`nwattime.ado` (which call it directly) get the same fix for free rather than needing a per-caller patch. **A seventh bug, adjacent but distinct**, surfaced fixing bug (3)'s regression test: `nwsym.ado`'s own `r(is_symmetric)` is documented (its own help text: `"true"` or `"false"`) but was actually stored via `st_numscalar` as `0`/`1` - a genuinely pre-existing bug, not caused by this migration, but only surfaced now because fixing it to match its own documented contract (needed so `nwfromedge.ado`'s auto-symmetrize check, which already correctly compared against the string form and was therefore silently broken from day one, would actually work) broke two *other* existing callers that had come to depend on the buggy numeric form: `nwgenerate.ado`'s network-arithmetic path (`nwgen mynet2 = 2 * mynet1` started failing with `true not found, r(111)` - a bare `if `r(is_symmetric)' == 1` treating the string as a variable reference) and `nwimport.ado`'s own directedness-inference block (same pattern, plus an unrelated adjacent quote typo, `` "`d" `` for `` "`d'" ``, fixed in the same edit since it sits on the very next line touched). All three call sites now agree on the documented string convention. New/updated regression tests: `cscripts/test_nwfromedge.do` gained two new cases (a reciprocal edgelist auto-detected as undirected without the `undirected` option, checked both via `nw_syntax`'s `directed` export and directly via `check_symmetry()`; a zero-weight triplet leaving its node's `degree()` at exactly `0`); `cscripts/test_nwsym.do` gained a direct `nwsym, check` test asserting `r(is_symmetric)` is the string `"true"`/`"false"` for both a symmetric and an asymmetric matrix. All of `test_nwfromedge.do`'s pre-existing assertions (density, arcs, `labs()`, string node names with spaces, numeric-ID auto-naming) re-verified passing unchanged. Certified at **L4** (justified: this touches `unw_core.do`'s own `set_edge_from_triplets()`/`check_symmetry()`/`symmetrize()` - existing, shared functions, not new isolated additions - plus `nwsym.ado`'s own r()-result contract, consumed by three separate callers) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode against `lib/lnwcommands.mlib`) - clean except the pre-existing, already-baselined dead-external-URL/missing-local-data failures (`nwdegree`/`nwexport`/`nwplot`/`nwplotmatrix`/`nwshared`/`nwsimmelian`/`nwuse`/`nwvalue`, all `r(601)`; `nwimport`'s own separate UCINET-fetch test, `r(6750)` - unrelated to the fix just made to that same file's directedness-inference block) and two untracked, unrelated in-progress scratch files (`cscripts/test_nwaddnodes.do`/`test_nwcollapse.do`, paired with pre-existing uncommitted, unfinished rewrites of `nwaddnodes.ado`/`nwcollapse.ado` from earlier, separate work - not part of this unit, deliberately left untouched rather than silently reverted or fixed out of scope). `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md` updated (Commit 8 section, corrected a stale note that had `nwneighbor.ado` as not-yet-migrated when direct inspection confirmed it already was). No `.sthlp` changes needed (no user-facing syntax/behaviour change to document beyond the bug fixes themselves, which restore already-documented behaviour rather than changing it). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 49: distance family migrated to sparse BFS/Dijkstra (sparse-backend migration item 2 of 2, migration complete) - a real regression caught and fixed mid-migration by re-running the function's own already-passing probes, not assumed still correct after a "safe-looking" follow-up change

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `calculate_distances()`'s two branches now dispatch to new sparse-native `calculate_distances_bfs()` (unweighted all-pairs via forward BFS over `neighbors()`) and `calculate_distances_dijkstra(alpha)` (weighted all-pairs, linear-scan extract-min over a `neighbors()`/`edge_weight()`-cached adjacency list), replacing `Brute_dist()`/`Dijkstra_dist()` respectively. `calculate_distance_pair(ego,alter)` replaced with a single early-exit BFS. `calculate_distances_without()` replaced with an `edgelist()`-driven loop calling a new `bfs_dist_excluding(source,target)` helper per existing edge, instead of mutating the dense `edge` field through the `get_matrix()` pointer. Feeds `nwgeodesic` (direct), `nwbridges` (direct), `nwpath` (direct), and `nwcloseness`/`nwreach` (both dispatch to `nwgeodesic` internally) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal architecture change; no user-facing syntax/behaviour change, `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`/`docs/ROADMAP.md` updated) | Completes the sparse-backend migration (see `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`) - the distance family was its last remaining item, having blocked true sparse scalability for the 5 commands above. Complexity: `calculate_distances_bfs()`/`calculate_distance_pair()`/`calculate_distances_without()` go from the old dense path's O(n) matrix-power iterations (each O(n³), so O(n⁴) worst case for all-pairs, or O(n⁴) worst case again for the old per-pair loop) to O(n·(V+E)) or better - a real asymptotic win on genuinely sparse networks, not just a storage-format swap. `calculate_distances_dijkstra()` keeps an O(n³) extract-min bottleneck (the same linear-scan-over-unsettled-nodes tradeoff already established and documented for `calculate_betweenness_weighted()` earlier this session - Mata has no built-in priority queue/decrease-key) but its edge-relaxation step is now O(degree) via `neighbors()`/`edge_weight()` instead of an O(n) dense row scan, and - like all four migrated functions - it never builds the dense `edge` matrix. **Verification methodology**: rather than trust a from-scratch reimplementation, every expected value was first captured from the *prior* dense implementation via direct Mata probe (undirected/directed, valued/unvalued, disconnected components, isolates, `alpha` at 0/0.5/1/1.5, a directed 4-cycle, a hand-verified self-cycle case) before writing a single line of the new sparse code, then the new implementation was run against the identical probes and confirmed byte-identical - a dense-vs-sparse regression in the literal sense, not an abstract correctness argument. Two genuinely subtle cases were checked empirically rather than assumed: (1) the old dense `calculate_distances_without()` zeroed **both** `edge[i,j]` and `edge[j,i]` before recomputing distance(i,j) - probed directly with a hand-built asymmetric case (a genuinely distinct reverse arc *and* an alternate route) to confirm the reverse-arc zeroing never actually changes the result, before committing to a forward-only exclusion in the sparse replacement; (2) the old dense `calculate_distance_pair(i,i)` (self-pair) returns the length of the shortest **cycle** back to `i` via the squared-matrix diagonal, not trivially `0`, whenever no direct self-loop edge exists - confirmed via probe (`2` on a 2-cycle) before designing the BFS replacement to seed from the source's own out-neighbors rather than pre-marking the source visited, which reproduces the exact same behaviour without a special case. **A real bug caught mid-migration, via the established discipline of re-running already-passing probes after every change rather than trusting a "safe-looking" follow-up**: an early draft of `bfs_dist_excluding()`, written to also handle a genuine self-loop edge (`source==target`, which `calculate_distances_without()` can and does call it with, via `edgelist()` enumerating a real self-loop entry) by seeding from `source`'s own neighbors without pre-marking `source` itself visited, silently broke the *ordinary* (non-self-loop) case: on an undirected network, removing edge `(i,j)` doesn't remove `i` from node `j`'s own symmetric neighbor list, so a path could loop back through an intermediate node and "reach `i` again" through the very edge meant to be excluded - caught immediately because the same probes that had already verified the function (an undirected 5-node path graph, where removing any single edge must always disconnect it - a tree has no alternate route) were re-run after this change and now failed. Fixed by branching explicitly: pre-mark `source` visited (blocking any return path to it) in the ordinary `source != target` case, and skip that pre-mark only for the genuine self-loop `source == target` case - then verified against the *actual* prior dense implementation on a hand-built self-loop network (extracted from git history at the pre-migration commit and run directly in a separate probe, not just reasoned about), byte-identical both before and after this fix. New permanent regression test `cscripts/test_sparse_distances.do`: all cases above (undirected/directed/valued/unvalued/disconnected/isolate/self-cycle/asymmetric-reverse-arc/self-loop), plus an explicit `edge_dense_built == 0` check after every one of the four migrated functions on a purely sparse-natively-constructed network. `lib/benchmark_sparse.do` gained a distance-family section: the BFS-based family benchmarked at 5,000 nodes/25,000 edges (completes in under two minutes, `edge_dense_built` confirmed 0 throughout - the old dense matrix-power approach would have been impractical at this scale), the weighted-Dijkstra family benchmarked separately at a smaller 300-node tier appropriate to its still-O(n³) extract-min step, with the benchmark's own docstring explaining why the two families warrant different tier sizes rather than implying uniform scalability. `Brute_dist()`/`Dijkstra_dist()`/`Dijkstra()` themselves are left unchanged (still used by `nclan_diameter_ok()`'s own bounded, small-induced-subgraph work - not part of this migration, no scalability concern there). Certified at **L4** (justified: touches `unw_core.do`'s own `calculate_distances()`/`calculate_distance_pair()`/`calculate_distances_without()` - existing, widely-relied-on functions, not new isolated additions, feeding 5 downstream commands) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode against `lib/lnwcommands.mlib`) run twice (once after the initial migration, once again after the self-loop bugfix) - both times clean except the same pre-existing dead-external-URL/missing-local-data failures and the same two untracked, unrelated in-progress scratch files already noted in unit 48's own row, confirming this migration introduced no new regressions anywhere in the package. `nwgeodesic`'s own pre-existing test (already covering `alpha` 0/0.5/1/1.5, symmetrization, eccentricity/radius/diameter, hand-computable path-graph and K4 cases) and `nwbridges`'/`nwpath`'s own pre-existing tests (bridge counts, path length/reachability, `paths_matrix`) all re-verified passing unchanged - no new `.ado`-level test assertions were needed there since the existing coverage already exercises the migrated Mata functions end to end with real, hand-computable expected values. No `.sthlp` changes needed (no user-facing syntax/behaviour change). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 50: nwplot default color scheme fix - nodes and edges rendered identically by default; a significant test-methodology gap found along the way (not fixed here, recorded as Pending)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwplot` now defaults `scheme()` to `s1network` (one of the package's own three purpose-built network schemes, already listed in `_pkg_ado.txt` but never actually used unless a caller passed `scheme()` explicitly) instead of silently falling through to whatever graph scheme happens to be ambient (`c(scheme)`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | User-reported: "I made one plot and both nodes and edges were all blue." **Root-caused, not assumed**: nwplot's own default node-fill/edge-line color resolution (`_getcolorstyle`) uses `"scheme p<n>"`/`"scheme p<n>line"` literals, which resolve against whatever scheme is active at render time; nwplot's own `scheme(string)` option was left empty by default rather than pointing at any specific scheme, so it silently inherited `c(scheme)` (Stata's own ordinary default, `stcolor`). Confirmed directly - not inferred - by rendering a minimal two-point scatter+line graph with `mfcolor("scheme p1")`/`lcolor("scheme p1line")` under `stcolor` and inspecting the actual exported PNG: both render as the identical blue. Reproduced the exact reported symptom on a real `nwplot` call before fixing (`nwrandom 12 / nwplot` under `stcolor` - uniform blue nodes and edges, before-image saved and compared pixel-for-pixel against the after-image), and confirmed the fix visually too (red nodes, grey edges under the new default `s1network`) - not just "no error thrown." Fix: default `scheme` to `s1network` when the caller doesn't specify one; `s1network`'s own definition (`scheme-s1network.scheme`) deliberately gives `p1`/`p1line` different colors, unlike an ordinary statistical-graph scheme where a single series' marker and connecting line usually *should* match. Added `return local scheme` (nwplot had exactly one existing `return local` before this - `export` - so this is a small, precedented addition) purely so the fix is regression-testable without pixel comparison in the ordinary suite. Doc header updated (three schemes now correctly described, `s1network` noted as the default); `nwplot.sthlp` regenerated via `nw_helpwriter` (hit the already-known, already-documented "too few quotes" crash on `nwplot.ado`'s own Mata layout code, same precedent as harmonisation units 33/37/47 - doc-header copy completes correctly before the crash, confirmed by inspecting the diff directly; "last certified" stamp added by hand). New assertions in `cscripts/test_nwplot.do` asserting `r(scheme)` is `"s1network"` by default and reflects an explicit override. **Found, not fixed here, a significant test-methodology gap while placing this new test**: `cscripts/test_nwplot.do`'s own `nwuse florentine, nwclear` call (line 96, not wrapped in `capture`) fails with the already-known dead-URL `r(601)` - but since a plain `do file.do` (the invocation this session's own regression sweeps actually use, checking each log for `r([0-9]`) aborts the *entire remaining script* on any uncaptured error, none of the ~250 lines after that call - including the file's own Plot A-G SVG-export block - have actually been executing at all, this session or (based on when that content was added) quite possibly since it was written. This is not unique to `nwplot`: the same pattern (an uncaptured `nwuse`/`nwwebuse florentine` or `nwimport`'s own UCINET fetch, not positioned as the literal last statement) likely affects some or all of the other files already on this document's own "known dead-URL failure" list (`nwdegree`/`nwexport`/`nwplotmatrix`/`nwshared`/`nwsimmelian`/`nwuse`/`nwvalue`/`nwimport`) - not audited file-by-file here, since doing so productively is entangled with actually fixing the underlying dead URL (item 3 of the pending feature-request queue, `webnwuse`→`nwwebuse`/Box.com redirect, explicitly deferred by the user pending a way to resolve individual Box file links). Recorded as its own Pending row below rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover. This unit's own new regression test was placed *before* the `nwuse florentine` line specifically to avoid falling into the same trap. Certified at **L1-L2** (`nwplot`'s own test, `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` as nwplot's only two internal callers - both use `generate()` for coordinates only, never nwplot's own color rendering, so unaffected; no `unw_core.do` change) - full sweep of `cscripts/test_nwplot.do`/`test_nwplotmatrix.do` in both dev and production mode, clean except the same pre-existing dead-URL failure. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 51: nwplotjs removed entirely; nwplot's SVG export gated to Stata 16+

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwplotjs.ado`/`.sthlp` (and the stale, never-loaded `nwplotjs.do` duplicate defining the same program) deleted entirely, per explicit request - native `nwplot` SVG export (harmonisation unit 33) now covers the package's export needs without a separate sigma.js-based interactive renderer. `nwplot`'s own `export()` gained an explicit Stata-version check: SVG export requires Stata 16+, checked directly against the export filename's extension rather than left to fail with whatever generic error an older Stata would produce | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Confirmed via grep before deleting: `nwplot.ado`/`nwplotmatrix.ado` only ever referenced `nwplotjs` in their own doc-header "See also" lines (no functional/programmatic dependency - `nwmovie.ado`/`nwmoviexy.ado` are `nwplot`'s only two internal callers, and they use `generate()` for coordinates only), so removal is a clean, isolated deletion. Updated every reference: `nwplot.ado`/`nwplotmatrix.ado` doc headers and their `.sthlp` files (both regenerated by hand rather than via `nw_helpwriter`, a one-line "See also" change not worth another full regenerate-and-restamp cycle), `_pkg_ado.txt`/`_pkg_hlp.txt`/`nwcommands-ado.pkg` (removed the now-nonexistent files' package-list entries), `cscripts/test_nwplotjs.do` deleted (it was untracked, and - per this document's own now-removed Pending row - every actual `nwplotjs` call inside it was already commented out, so it tested nothing regardless). `docs/ROADMAP.md`/`docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md`/`LEGACY_FILES.md` updated to stop describing `nwplotjs` as a live, shipped command (all three had accurately described it as such as of unit 33's own audit, which is exactly when this reversal happened) rather than silently left stale; `LEGACY_FILES.md` additionally notes that `linkurious/` (the sigma.js fork `nwplotjs.ado` loaded its JavaScript from, previously documented there as "not archived - a live runtime dependency") was checked at removal time and no longer exists anywhere in the repository - not investigated further since it predates this unit and is moot either way now. **SVG version check**: added because `nwplotjs`'s removal makes `nwplot`'s own native SVG export the package's only vector/interactive-adjacent output path, and the user's own instruction specifically flagged that SVG export needs Stata 16+ (trusted as the author's own stated constraint rather than second-guessed against Stata's own generic `graph export` help text, which does not itself state a version-introduced cutoff for any given format). Implemented as an explicit `c(stata_version) < 16` check against the export filename's extension (case-insensitive `.svg` match via `substr(lower(...), -4, .)`), raising a clear, actionable error (`error 9`) instead of leaving it to whatever a real older Stata would do. Verified directly: the guard's own string/version logic checked in isolation (`"myplot.SVG"`/`"myplot.svg"` both match, `"myplot.png"` doesn't, and on this environment's actual Stata 18 the full condition correctly evaluates false so the guard never fires) and a real `nwplot ..., export(foo.svg)` call confirmed still succeeding (`_rc==0`) after the change - cannot literally trigger the blocked branch itself without an actual sub-16 Stata install, but the surrounding `di`/`error` idiom is the same one used throughout the rest of this codebase. **Found, not fixed here**: verifying this change against `cscripts/test_nwplot.do`'s own existing SVG-export assertions (Plot A-G) was not possible - they sit after that file's own `nwuse florentine` call, the same test-abort gap unit 50 already logged as Pending, so verification here used a standalone probe instead of the file's own test suite. Certified at **L1** (self-contained `.ado`/`.sthlp`/packaging-file change, no `unw_core.do` change, no other command's own dispatch touches `nwplotjs`) - `cscripts/test_nwplot.do`/`test_nwplotmatrix.do` re-run in both modes, clean except the same pre-existing dead-URL failure already on this document's own baseline list. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 52: `_nwsyntax`/`_nwsyntax_other` consolidated away entirely; `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` found completely broken and fixed - real, previously-undiscovered bugs, not just a naming cleanup

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Answers a direct user question ("I am confused about nw_syntax and _nwsyntax - are they the same? Can we consolidate?"): they were not independent implementations. `_nwsyntax.ado` was already a pure, deprecated wrapper around `nw_syntax` (its own header: "DEPRECATED => use nw_syntax instead"), re-exporting only 4 of `nw_syntax`'s locals (`netobj`/`id`/`netname`/`networks`). `_nwsyntax_other.ado` was a separate, independent, and - confirmed directly, not assumed - completely non-functional legacy reimplementation of `nw_syntax`'s own `other()` netlist-resolution feature, predating it. Both deleted entirely; every remaining live caller migrated to `nw_syntax` directly | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal architecture change/bugfix, no user-facing syntax change) | **`_nwsyntax` migration** (11 files: `_nwsetobs`, `_nwnodeid`, `nwsimilar`, `nwissymmetric`, `nwcloseness`, `nwconstraint`, `nwergm`, `_nwnodelab`, `nwdissimilar`, `nwmovie`, `nwmoviexy` - a further 6 files, `nwutility`/`nwhierarchy`/`nwtomatafast`/`nwdropnodes`/`nwkeepnodes`/`nwrecode`, turned out to already be migrated by earlier, uncommitted work found sitting in the tree). Each checked individually for the exact collision risk this session already found once for `nwfromedge.ado` (harmonisation unit 48) and `nwreplacemat`/`nwdropnodes`/`nwkeepnodes` (unit 30, per that unit's own certified row above): `nw_syntax`'s own bare `c_local` exports (`directed`/`valued`/`nodes`/`labs`/etc.) silently clobber a caller's identically-named option-parsing local when called without `other()`. Found and handled one genuine instance here too: `nwdissimilar.ado` declares its own `labs(passthru)` option, genuinely consumed later in the same program (passed through to `nwset`'s own `mat()` call) - calling `nw_syntax` directly (as the other 10 files safely could) would have silently overwritten the user's `labs()` value with the network's own node-label list before it was ever read. Fixed with `nw_syntax `netname', other(other)` plus a single `local netname "`othernetname'"'` (the only other-prefixed export this file actually needed), rather than either leaving the deprecated wrapper in place or introducing the same silent-clobber bug found and fixed twice already this session. **`_nwsyntax_other` removal - a real, severe bug fix, not just cleanup**: confirmed via direct empirical testing (not static analysis - the prior Pending entry for this exact item, open since harmonisation units 9-13, explicitly said "confirmed broken by static analysis... not empirically run end to end since movie/animation export needs ImageMagick, not installed in this environment" - ImageMagick **is** installed in this environment, confirmed via `which convert`/`which magick`, so this was tested for real) that `_nwsyntax_other` crashes unconditionally on every single call: `forvalues i = 1/$nwtotal` (`$nwtotal`, a legacy pre-2016 global, is never set anywhere in the current `NWsdef`-based architecture, so this expands to `forvalues i = 1/` - "invalid syntax", r(198) - before even reaching a second, independent broken reference further down the same function, `nw_mata`id'`, an equally nonexistent legacy per-network Mata global). Since `nwmovie.ado`/`nwmoviexy.ado` are `_nwsyntax_other`'s only two remaining live callers, and both call it unconditionally as part of their own main "make movie" pipeline (not behind any optional feature), **both commands could never successfully complete a single call** - confirmed directly with a minimal 2-network repro before touching any code. Fixed by replacing all 3 call sites in each file with `nw_syntax ..., other(other)` (using `nw_syntax`'s own already-correct empty/current-network resolution, which `_nwsyntax_other`'s broken reimplementation of the same feature never reached) plus a small, directly-inlined replication of `_nwsyntax_other`'s own `exactly()` netlist-padding behaviour (repeat the last resolved name to fill out a short list - e.g. one `edgesize()` network specified, applied to every movie frame - which `nw_syntax` has no built-in equivalent for). `_nwsyntax_other`'s own self-loop-removal side effect (also unconditionally hitting the same dead `nw_mata`id'` reference, so equally already-broken) was deliberately **dropped rather than reimplemented blind**: `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` have no existing test coverage and their own final step needs ImageMagick and shells out to open the result in the OS default viewer (the same class of side effect that has kept `nwplotjs` untested all along, see that item's own now-removed Pending row), so a from-scratch reimplementation of a feature that was silently dead code either way could not be verified here without much larger scope - recorded as its own Pending item below rather than silently dropped. **A second, wholly unrelated, pre-existing bug found and fixed while verifying the first**: `nwmovie.ado` (not `nwmoviexy.ado`, which does not have this typo) had `local nxy2' = "..."` - a stray apostrophe makes this define a local literally named `nxy2'` instead of `nxy2`, so the very next line's `` if "`nxy2'" != "" `` (a genuine, correctly-formed macro reference to the *never-set* local `nxy2`) crashed with "type mismatch", r(109), on every call that didn't supply the optional `nodexys()` varlist - the common case. Fixed the obvious way (removed the stray quote). **Verification**: with both bugs fixed, `nwmovie net1 net2` and `nwmoviexy net1 net2` (a 5-node random network duplicated into a second network of the same size) both completed end to end in this environment, producing a real, playable `movie.gif` - confirmed by direct inspection of the output file (81KB, valid GIF), not merely "`_rc==0`". Not committed as an automated `cscripts/` test for the same OS-side-effect reason `_nwsyntax_other`'s self-loop removal wasn't reimplemented (Safari-opening `shell open` call, ImageMagick availability varies by environment) - verified via a standalone, non-committed probe instead, and the `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` gap recorded explicitly in Pending below rather than left implicit. `_nwsyntax.ado`/`.sthlp`/`_nwsyntax_other.ado` deleted (both tracked; removed via `git rm` semantics), their `_pkg_ado.txt`/`_pkg_hlp.txt`/`nwcommands-ado.pkg` entries removed, and the long-open unit-9-13 Pending row tracking this exact item removed (resolved, not silently dropped - this row's own history is preserved in this unit's own write-up instead). `nwmoviexy.ado` brought under version control for the first time (previously untracked despite being actively relied upon, matching the same "version-control gap" pattern already documented and fixed for other files earlier this session - see `docs/ROADMAP.md`). `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own unit-33-era claim that `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` were "a separate, already-working raster/GIF pipeline, currently blocked in this environment only by missing ImageMagick" corrected - it was wrong on both counts (not working at all; ImageMagick was never actually missing). Certified at **L3-L4** (justified: `_nwsyntax`/`_nwsyntax_other` were both widely-referenced utility commands, not isolated additions, and this fix touches 13 `.ado` files across several unrelated command families) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, both dev mode and production mode) - clean except the same pre-existing dead-URL/environmental failures and two untracked, unrelated in-progress scratch files already noted in prior units' own rows, confirming no regressions anywhere else in the package from removing two long-standing utility commands. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 53: root-folder cleanup - 27 genuinely dead files removed, each individually verified rather than bulk-deleted

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Per explicit request ("delete old files not used anymore... keep the subfolders"), removed 27 genuinely dead files from the repo root: 16 untracked scratch/temp artifacts (dev scripts, log files, orphaned scratch data, two files with a stray Private-Use-Area Unicode character appended to an otherwise-ordinary name) plus 11 already-tracked files that existed as uncommitted, pending `git status` deletions from earlier work | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (`LEGACY_FILES.md`, its own designated living document for exactly this) | Every file checked individually before removal - none bulk-deleted on pattern-match alone, since a large pile of pre-existing uncommitted deletions already sat in the working tree with no record of why, and blindly finalizing all of them risked silently converting a real, fixable bug into permanent data loss. Confirmed this risk is real, not hypothetical: `nwvalidvars.ado`/`.sthlp` were among the pending deletions, and `nwvalidvars` is exactly the missing command already tracked in this document's own Pending table as breaking `nwlattice.ado` - but a direct diff against `git show HEAD:nwvalidvars.ado` confirmed it is byte-identical to the already-archived `deprecated/nwvalidvars.ado`, meaning the deletion merely finalizes an archival decision already made and documented elsewhere, not an accidental loss - restoring it to the root would have partially undone that decision rather than fixed anything, so the root-level deletion was finalized and the real `nwlattice.ado` fix stays exactly where it already was, in its own Pending row. Same byte-identical-to-`deprecated/` verification applied to `nwsave_old.ado`/`nwsort.ado`/`.sthlp` before finalizing those too. Root-level `compressed_example.txt`/`glasgow.dta`/`klas12b.dta` confirmed redundant against proper copies already under `data/` before removal (and `nwimport.ado`'s own apparent reference to `compressed_example.txt` turned out to be dead code - a misspelled local, `` `anyting' `` instead of `` `anything' ``, that never did anything). `kapmine.dat`'s only apparent reference (in `netexample.sthlp`) turned out to be a coincidental filename match inside a worked example that fetches from an external URL, not the local file. `test.do`'s only caller, `nw_deployfile.ado`, is itself unreferenced author-only release tooling, not a shipped package command; `test.dta`'s only "reference" (in `cscripts/test_nwbalance.do`) is a code comment describing an already-fixed bug, not a real dependency. Explicitly left untouched, per the same care: `demo/`'s own pending deletions and `data/~$excel_example.xlsx` (both inside subfolders this pass was not scoped to touch - "keep the subfolders" taken literally), and `nwcommands-dlg.pkg`/`nwcommands-ext.pkg`/`nwcommands-ext1.pkg`/`nwcommands-hlp.pkg` (root-level, but package-distribution manifests `nwinstall.ado` references by name - finalizing their deletion without first understanding the release/regeneration process behind them risked silently breaking `nwinstall`'s own distribution mechanism; recorded as its own Pending item below rather than guessed at). Full detail on every single file's own disposition and evidence in `LEGACY_FILES.md`'s new "Root-folder cleanup" section. Certified at **L4** (a repo-wide file removal, however individually low-risk each one was, warrants the full sweep rather than assuming zero blast radius) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, dev mode - no `.ado`/`unw_core.do` logic was touched at all, so no separate production-mode rebuild/re-run was needed) - clean, identical to the baseline already established in prior units, confirming none of the 27 removed files were silently load-bearing anywhere in the suite. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 54: help-file cross-reference audit - every `{help X}` link checked against the actual command surface, ~20 genuine broken/dangling references found and fixed, two runnable tutorial examples repaired and re-verified

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Scripted a repo-wide scan of every `{help(b) X}` cross-reference in every `.sthlp` file against the actual set of live commands/topic markers, filtering out Stata's own base-command help topics (`varname`/`if`/`egen`/etc., which correctly resolve outside this package and are not bugs). Fixed every genuine hit rather than the many false positives a cruder check (e.g. raw brace-counting, tried and abandoned first - SMCL's own directive syntax has too many legitimately unpaired `{tag}` singletons for that to be a useful signal) would have produced | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (this unit's own subject) | Distinct root causes, each verified individually rather than bulk-replaced: (1) **plain typos** with an unambiguous fix - `nwbridges.ado`/`.sthlp` self-referenced its own anchor as `nwbriges##bridge_type` (missing a 'd'); `nwsave.ado`/`.sthlp` linked `nwwebwuse` (transposed letters) instead of `nwwebuse`; `nwdegree.ado`/`.sthlp` linked the truncated `nwcluster` instead of `nwclustering`; five files (`nwtabulate`/`nwtab2`/`nwtab3` `.ado`+`.sthlp`) linked `netexamle` instead of `netexample`. (2) **references to commands this same harmonisation phase itself removed or archived** - `nworder.sthlp`'s own `{help nwsort}` (`nwsort.ado` archived to `deprecated/`, unit 53) and `nwset.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own `{help nwvalidvars}` (same unit) delinked to plain prose rather than left dangling; `nw_intro.sthlp`'s command-equivalence table, `nwunab.sthlp`'s own description, and `nw_programming.sthlp` (three separate spots, including two full runnable tutorial code examples) all still pointed at `_nwsyntax` after its own removal (unit 52) - fixed to `nw_syntax` throughout, since it is the actual, current, correct command being described in every one of these cases, not merely a dead link to patch over. (3) **aspirational references to commands that were seemingly planned but never actually built** - `nwcollapse.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own `{help nwmerge}` and `nw_datasync.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own sole "See also" entry `{help nwattributes}` (node/edge attribute storage is a documented, still-open roadmap gap - see `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own `nwergm` prerequisites list) - removed rather than invented a plausible-but-wrong replacement; `nw_datasync`'s now-empty "See also" section removed entirely rather than left as a bare, content-free title. (4) **concepts documented under a different, existing filename** - `nodename`/`nodelab` (used in `_nwnodeid.sthlp`, `_nwnodelab.sthlp`, `nw2toedge`/`nwtoedge`/`nwtabulate` `.ado`+`.sthlp`) are actually covered as subsections of `nodeid.sthlp`, not standalone files of their own - redirected to `{help nodeid:nodelab}`/`{help nodeid}` rather than left broken or given a newly-invented anchor that doesn't exist; `nw_tomata.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own `{help netobj:Mata network object}` and `nwtomata.sthlp`'s own `{help nwinternal:advanced network programming}` both redirected to `nw_programming.sthlp`'s own already-existing `{marker modernprogramming}` section, the correct current home for that content (its predecessor, the "Internal design (legacy, pre-2016)" section, was confirmed a poor fit before choosing the modern section instead - describes the superseded global/dense-matrix architecture, not `netobj`/`NWdef`). (5) **a `{help}` wrapper mistakenly applied to an ordinary Stata variable name**, not a command - `nwdrop.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own `{help _nwinclude}` (a real, actively-used internal variable, confirmed via grep across `nwdrop`/`nwsave`/`nwuse`/`nwload`/`unw_defs`, but not a command with its own help page) - changed to plain `{bf:_nwinclude}`. **`nw_programming.sthlp`'s two tutorial examples were not just relinked but actually fixed and re-verified end to end**: both `myindegree` and `myinverse` (worked examples teaching new package-extenders how to write custom network-aware commands) called the now-deleted `_nwsyntax` in real, runnable code blocks, not just prose - updated to `nw_syntax` (confirmed to export the same `netname' local under the same name, so a straight substitution is correct) and both examples run as literally printed in the help file, producing hand-verified correct output (`myindegree` on a 3-node directed star, indegree 0/1/2 as expected; `myinverse` on the same network, correctly producing the boolean tie-complement) - not merely "no error thrown." Found and fixed one further, unrelated, adjacent bug in the same `myinverse` example while re-verifying it: its own `mata: mata drop indegree inverse` referenced `indegree`, a copy-paste leftover from the *other* example that was never created in this one - corrected to `mata: mata drop net inverse`, the two Mata matrices this example actually creates. `nwbridges.ado`/`nwtab3.sthlp` brought under version control for the first time (previously untracked despite being live, matching the same gap already documented and fixed for other files this session). Full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, dev mode - every change here is doc-header/`.sthlp`-only content, no live program logic touched, so no separate production-mode rebuild/re-run was needed) - clean, identical to the established baseline, confirming zero functional impact from any of the ~20 fixes. Certified at **L1** (pure documentation/cross-reference content, no `.ado` program-body logic changed anywhere - even the two tutorial code-block fixes live inside `.sthlp`/doc-header text, not the package's own executable commands). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 55 (scoping only, no changes made): help-file network-type restructuring blocked on finishing the underlying audit first

User-requested (feature-list item 11): stop stating "supported network types" inline in each command's own help file; instead reference a shared matrix, with a separate help topic covering two-mode/valued support in general, linked from the general help suite. Investigated before implementing, rather than executing partially: `docs/NETWORK_TYPE_MATRIX.md` (the matrix the request refers to) is explicitly, in its own text, an incomplete, in-progress document - "This table is filled in incrementally... does not yet cover all ~127 public commands" and its own "Not yet audited against this matrix" section lists roughly 90 of the package's ~127 commands still unclassified (the entire data-management/lifecycle group, the generator group, the visualisation group, import/export, and several analysis commands). Only ~25-37 commands currently have a real, evidence-backed row.

**Why this blocks implementation rather than just slowing it down**: 25 `.sthlp` files currently carry their own, individually-written "Supported network types" section (confirmed via grep). Converting the *existing* matrix into a Stata help topic and removing those 25 sections in favor of linking to it would be a **net loss of documentation** for those 25 commands specifically, and would still leave the other ~90 commands with no network-type documentation at all, matrix-based or otherwise, when many of them already state their own type support inline in a form the matrix hasn't caught up to yet. Doing this properly requires finishing the underlying audit (the ~90 remaining rows) first, or at minimum every command whose own inline section would be removed - not a small addition to file for a "help text refactor," a substantial evidence-gathering task in its own right (each row in the existing matrix required reading the actual `.ado` code and quoting the specific line backing the classification, per the matrix's own stated methodology).

Not attempted as a partial implementation (e.g., converting only the ~25 already-audited rows and leaving the rest as-is) because that would produce an inconsistent, confusing hybrid - some commands linking to a shared matrix page, others still showing their own inline section, with no clear pattern for a reader to anticipate which is which, and no guarantee the ~25 "done" ones stay in sync with their own `.ado` files without the same regeneration discipline the rest of the help system uses. Recorded as its own Pending item below with a concrete, staged path rather than left as a vague restatement of the original request.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 56 (scoping only, no changes made): `nw2x` command removal - the requested architecture is already mostly in place

User-requested (feature-list item 2): "Remove all `nw2x` commands if they are not needed. The idea is to embed the whole two mode logic directly into the networks and have the normal commands adapt to the kind of network they are dealing with." Investigated the actual current state before proposing any removal, since a wrong assumption here could mean deleting genuinely load-bearing code. Finding: **the requested architecture already exists for 2 of the 3 real `nw2x` analysis commands** (`nw2set`/`nw2toedge`/`nw2fromedge` are data-declaration/conversion commands, a different category with no "one-mode equivalent" to redirect from or to, and are not what this request is about). `nwdegree.ado` and `nwclustering.ado` both already check `is2mode` and transparently redirect to `nw2degree`/`nw2clustering` respectively, printing a clear note, rather than silently computing a meaningless one-mode statistic on bipartite data (confirmed directly reading both files - this was itself fixed earlier this session/prior units, see `docs/NETWORK_TYPE_MATRIX.md`'s own `nwdegree`/`nwclustering` rows). This is precisely "the normal commands adapt to the kind of network they are dealing with" already working as designed - the `nw2degree.ado`/`nw2clustering.ado` files themselves cannot be deleted, since the redirect mechanism is a literal Stata command call to them; removing the files would break `nwdegree`/`nwclustering` on any two-mode input, the opposite of what's requested. `nw2project` is a different case entirely: projection (collapsing a two-mode network into a one-mode network) has no one-mode input to redirect *from* - there is no "normal `nwproject`" for a bipartite-specific `nw2project` to be a variant of, so it necessarily stays a standalone command regardless of how this item is resolved.

**Judgment call made (not left open)**: checked whether `nw2degree`/`nw2clustering` are genuinely redundant with their redirect callers before deciding whether to de-list them from the general help index. They are not - `nw2degree` (`generate()`/`replace`/`silent`) and `nw2clustering` (`measure()`/`level()`/`generate()`) both have their own, narrower, two-mode-appropriate option surfaces, distinct from `nwdegree`'s rich one-mode-oriented options (`alpha()`/`isolates`/`standardize`/`in()`/`out()`, none of which are meaningful on bipartite data) and `nwclustering`'s own `symmetrize` (meaningless once already redirected to bipartite-native logic) - `nw2clustering`'s own `level()` option in particular has no `nwclustering` equivalent at all. **Decision: keep `nw2degree`/`nw2clustering` as separately documented, separately discoverable commands** - a user who already knows their network is two-mode and wants the two-mode-specific option set directly (skipping the auto-detection note) is genuinely better served calling them directly, and hiding them from the help index would reduce discoverability with no compensating benefit, since the underlying `.ado` files must stay either way (the redirect mechanism is a literal command call to them). No code or documentation changes needed for this item - the requested architecture and this specific judgment call are both already correctly resolved as-is.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 57 (scoping only, no changes made): dialog (`.dlg`) UI audit not started - real scale confirmed, full audit deferred

User-requested (feature-list item 9): a proper `.dlg` dialog UI for all implemented features, installed via `nwinstall` (per the user's own note, "I had started doing that, but it might be old code by now"). Not investigated further within this session's remaining scope: which of the 57 files are current/working vs. stale relative to each command's own present-day option set (the user's own stated uncertainty - "might be old code by now" - needs verifying file-by-file, not assumed either way), whether every implemented command that should have a dialog actually has one, and whether the overall UI design reflects the package's current architecture and conventions as the user asked. Recorded as its own Pending item below with a concrete first step, rather than either skipped silently or attempted as a rushed, unverified pass across 57 files.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 58: Stage 1 roadmap remainder closed out - `nwlattice`/`nwhomophily` (and their `nwgen` shortcuts) fixed; `nwset`'s own documented `vars()` option found silently missing and restored; a real, unresolved homophily-direction bug found and recorded

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwlattice.ado` and `nwhomophily.ado` (and their `nwgen lattice(`/`homophily(` shortcuts, dispatch-ready since unit 47 but blocked on these same underlying bugs) both fixed and given permanent regression tests for the first time - previously zero coverage, previously unable to complete a single call | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (both already had `.sthlp` files; `nwrecode.ado`'s own long-open Stage 1 roadmap item was separately found to already have one too, from unit 13 - `docs/ROADMAP.md` simply never checked it off) | **`nwlattice`'s real root cause was not what the standing Pending item said**: the original diagnosis (open since harmonisation unit 47) was "calls a completely missing command, `nwvalidvars`" - reading `nwvalidvars.ado`'s actual body (recovered from `deprecated/`, where an earlier, separate cleanup pass had archived it without checking whether anything still depended on it) found it genuinely simple, self-contained, and correct - no legacy-architecture dependency at all, unlike `_nwsyntax_other`, which really was broken. Restored `nwvalidvars.ado`/`.sthlp` from `deprecated/` to the active root. This surfaced the *actual*, previously-undiscovered bug once `nwlattice` got past that: `nwset.ado`'s own `vars()` option - documented in its own doc header ("can be set wit option `vars()`") since long before this session - had been silently dropped from its `syntax` line entirely, with no other live caller depending on it to have ever surfaced the gap (`nwlattice.ado` is the only one). Restored `vars(string)` to `nwset.ado`'s construction path, validating the entry count against the network's own node count and calling the already-existing `NWdef::set_nodesvar()` method (no `unw_core.do` change needed - the method already existed, just had no working caller). Verified end to end: `nwlattice 3 3, undirected` now produces the correct 9-node/12-edge grid (hand-computable: 2 horizontal + 2 vertical neighbors per interior arrangement), a 4x4 toroidal lattice (`xwrap ywrap`) correctly gives every node degree 4 (32 edges), and `nwload` correctly materializes the requested `vars()` variable names. **`nwhomophily`'s crash** was a simple redundant cleanup bug: `mata: mata drop `` `__temp' `` `` at the very end of the program, when the identical variable was already dropped inside the loop directly above it (every iteration cleans up after itself) - confirmed via `set trace on` that the command's own actual logic (building the homophily-weighted tie-probability matrix, generating the network via `nwdyadprob`) always completed successfully first; only this final, unreachable-by-design line ever failed. Also removed a leftover debug `di` line printing both tempnames' literal macro text on every loop iteration (visible in the wild as a stray "M __000000  __000001" line in otherwise-clean output). **A real, separate, unresolved bug found while writing `nwhomophily`'s own regression test, not silently asserted as fixed**: attempting a statistical sanity check (does more homophily actually mean more within-group ties?) found `homophily(2)` and `homophily(-2)` produce byte-identical output networks under an identical seed. Traced concretely, not left as a vague suspicion: `nwhomophily.ado`'s own weight construction (`exp(homophily * same-group indicator)`) is correctly, oppositely signed for +2 vs -2 (hand-verified); the actual tie selection happens in `nwdyadprob.ado`'s `density()`-conditioned branch, which hands the weight matrix to the third-party `gsample` package for weighted sampling without replacement (`gsample `` `ties' `` [aweight=_tempdyad], generate(link) wor`) - confirmed `gsample` is genuinely installed and does implement real weight-sensitive sampling (its own PPS-consistency check correctly errors on an artificially extreme weight in an isolated probe), yet the two oppositely-weighted homophily calls still select the identical dyad set. Not further root-caused within this unit's own scope (would require tracing `gsample`'s own internals or `nwdyadprob`'s exact weight hand-off in more depth than the crash fix this unit was chartered for needed) - recorded as its own Pending item below rather than silently accepted or asserted correct in the new test file, which checks only structural properties (density hit exactly, correct node count, directed/undirected respected, multi-attribute accepted, `xvars` suppression) that are genuinely confirmed, not the statistical effect that isn't. New `cscripts/test_nwlattice.do`/`test_nwhomophily.do` (both zero coverage before); `cscripts/test_nwset.do` gained direct `vars()` coverage (correct variable names, and a wrong-count error case). Also confirmed `nwgen mylattice = lattice(3 3), undirected` and the `homophily(` shortcut both now dispatch correctly, closing the loop on unit 47's own restoration work for these two specifically. Certified at **L2** (`nwlattice`'s/`nwhomophily`'s own new tests, `nwset`'s own test as the file whose construction path changed, `nwgenerate`'s own test as the shortcut-dispatch layer) - full sweep of the entire `cscripts/` suite (110 files, dev mode - no `unw_core.do` change, `set_nodesvar()` already existed) - clean except the same pre-existing dead-URL/environmental failures and two untracked, unrelated in-progress scratch files already noted in prior units. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 59: full multi-level Moody-White cohesive-blocking hierarchy - new `nwcohesion` command, plus a real `keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()` sparse-architecture gap found and fixed along the way

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `nwcohesion.ado` command + `NWdef::calculate_cohesion_hierarchy()`/standalone `CohesionHierarchy()` Mata function in `unw_core.do`, implementing the full recursive Moody & White (2003) cohesive-blocking hierarchy that `nwkcomponents.ado`'s own doc header already flagged as "a natural, separate follow-on" once its single-level `KComponents()`/`vertex_connectivity()` primitives existed | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (new `nwcohesion.sthlp` doc header, `nwkcomponents.sthlp`/`nwcomponents.sthlp` cross-references) | **Design**: rather than reuse `KComponents()`'s own single-target-k contract directly, `CohesionHierarchy(origadj, nodeset)` recurses on ITS OWN actual computed connectivity kappa at every node set it visits (via `vertex_connectivity()`), records `(kappa, nodeset)` unconditionally as one row of the hierarchy, and only then searches for `kappa+1`-components within it via the existing `KComponents()` call - each child block can therefore end up at a level strictly higher than `kappa+1` (a well-documented real property of structural cohesion: levels can skip), which the recursion embraces rather than assumes away. **Termination proof** (see the function's own header comment for the full argument): every child returned by `KComponents(origadj, nodeset, kappa+1)` has connectivity >= kappa+1 by that function's own contract, strictly greater than `nodeset`'s own just-computed kappa - so a child can never be identical to `nodeset`, meaning each recursive call strictly increases the target level, bounded above by n-1; a set with zero qualifying children needs no special case, since `KComponents` returning 0 rows means the enclosing `for` loop simply doesn't iterate. **Verified against four hand-computable cases** (each checked via an isolated Mata probe against `calculate_cohesion_hierarchy()` directly, before writing `nwcohesion.ado` or its test): (1) two triangles joined by a single bridge edge - whole graph kappa=1 (a genuine cut vertex, NOT 0 - 0 would mean already-disconnected, an initial hand-verification error corrected once the probe's actual output was inspected rather than assumed), two triangle children at kappa=2 each, matching `test_nwkcomponents.do`'s own identical network exactly in structure; (2) a lone triangle - one block, kappa=2, too small (3 nodes < kappa+2=4) to ever split further; (3) a fully disconnected 4-node graph (zero edges) - one block, kappa=0, `KComponents` at k=1 finds nothing to split into; (4) two genuinely disconnected triangles (no bridge at all) - top block kappa=0 (disconnected by definition), two kappa=2 triangle children found via `KComponents`'s own ordinary "connected components" behavior at k=1. `nwcohesion.ado` follows `nwkcomponents.ado`'s exact syntax/display/netlist conventions (`generate()`/`replace`/`silent`, multi-network suffix handling), returning `r(blocks)`, `r(cohesion_matrix)` (blocks-by-nodes 0/1 membership, one row per block at ANY level - not just one requested `k`) and `r(cohesion_levels)` (a parallel column vector of each row's own connectivity level); the per-node `generate()` variable (default `_cohesion`) stores each node's OWN highest level across every block containing it, always well-defined (unlike `nwkcomponents`' `_kcompnum`, missing for nodes not qualifying at the one requested k) since every node belongs to at least the top-level block. New `cscripts/test_nwcohesion.do` covers all four hand-verified cases above plus directed-network auto-symmetrization, `generate()`/`replace`, netlist (multiple networks per call), and `silent` - dev mode clean; production-mode copy (`lib/cscripts_prod/test_nwcohesion.do`) also verified clean against the rebuilt `lnwcommands.mlib`. Full `cscripts/` dev-mode sweep: 102 pass (101 pre-existing + this new file), same 11 pre-existing baseline fails (2 untracked scratch, 9 dead-URL/environmental) - no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this command's own new test plus the Mata-core change's production-mode re-verification; not L3/L4 since no other command calls the new Mata methods yet). |
| `NWdef::extract_subgraph(rowvector k)` - a new Mata method returning a fresh, UNREGISTERED `NWdef` instance holding the induced subgraph on the nodes selected by `k`, leaving the calling network completely untouched (unlike `keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()`, which mutate in place, or `NWsdef::duplicate()`, which registers a new named network in the global store) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (header comment in `unw_core.do`; not yet given its own `.sthlp` since it has no direct Stata-facing syntax - it's an internal primitive, same status as `KComponents()`/`vertex_connectivity()` themselves) | Built as the shared induced-subgraph primitive originally scoped for BOTH this unit's Moody-White hierarchy work and Stage 3's still-open "ego-network extraction as a true induced subgraph" item - turned out NOT to be needed by `nwcohesion` itself, since `KComponents()` already operates directly on 0/1 node-indicator vectors over a single shared adjacency matrix without ever needing a real separate copy; kept as a standalone, independently-verified primitive for the still-open Stage 3 item and any other future recursive/subgraph-copying algorithm. Verified via an isolated Mata probe (a 4-node network, `k=(1,1,0,1)` dropping node 3): confirmed the ORIGINAL network's own node count and adjacency matrix are byte-identical before and after the call (no mutation), the returned subgraph has the correct reduced node count, and its adjacency matrix is exactly the correct induced subgraph (node1-node2 tied as in the original, node1-node4/node2-node4 correctly NOT tied since neither was tied to node 4 originally). Implementation copies every field `NWsdef::duplicate()` itself copies (name/selfloop/directed/valued/2mode/edge/nodenames/modes/nodesvar/label/caption) onto a fresh instance, then calls `keep_nodes()` on the COPY - deliberately calls `set_selfloop()` before `set_edge()` (the reverse of `duplicate()`'s own order), since `set_edge()`'s own diagonal-handling logic reads `is_selfloop_boolean()` at the moment it runs; `duplicate()`'s own opposite order is a latent, pre-existing correctness question for constructing a copy of a WITH-self-loops network (out of scope to fix here - `duplicate()` itself was not touched). |
| `NWdef::keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()` never called `ensure_dense_built()` before reading the `edge` field directly - a real, previously-undiscovered sparse-architecture gap, unlike essentially every other dense-touching `NWdef` method (e.g. `check_symmetry()`), which already guards this way | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ (covered indirectly - `extract_subgraph()`'s own probe and `nwcohesion`'s tests all exercise `keep_nodes()` on networks built via the ordinary dense `nwset` path, where `edge_dense_built` is already `True` and the guard is a cheap no-op; no test specifically exercises a genuinely sparse-native network, i.e. one built via `set_edge_from_triplets()`, through `keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()`/`extract_subgraph()` - see this document's own general validation/architecture-adoption item once that stage begins) | Partial (fix is in place and provably correct for the dense case exercised; the sparse-native case is unverified, not merely "assumed fine") | ✅ (inline comment on the fix itself) | Found while building `extract_subgraph()` (which calls `keep_nodes()` internally): for a sparse-native network (`edge_dense_built==False`, i.e. one built via `set_edge_from_triplets()` and never yet materialized into a dense matrix), `keep_nodes()`'s own `select(edge,k)` calls would have silently operated on an empty/never-populated `edge` field, producing a wrong (likely all-zero) result with no error at all - `nwdropnodes.ado`/`nwkeepnodes.ado`, the two existing live callers, were never caught by this because every test exercising them happens to build its network via the ordinary dense `nwset`/`nwfromedge` path first. Fixed by adding the same `ensure_dense_built()` call every other dense-reading method already makes, as the very first line of `keep_nodes()`'s own body (before it, not inside the `if (cols(k)==cols(nodes))` guard, so it always runs). This is exactly the class of "architecture adoption gap" the user's own requested end-of-work general validation stage is meant to surface - recorded here as a concrete, already-found example of what that audit should look for elsewhere, and as a still-open item (sparse-native coverage for this specific fix) in its own right. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 60: `nwaltergen diversity()` - Blau's index of ego-network composition, closing out Stage 3's last "blocked on subgraph extraction" item (which, on inspection, was never actually blocked on that at all)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `diversity(alter.srcvar)` stat for `nwaltergen`/`nwgen`, plus a new shared `BlauIndex()` Mata helper called from both `calculate_alterstat()` and `calculate_alterstat_hop()` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s Stage 3 list carried a long-standing item claiming ego-network size/density/composition/effective-size were all "blocked" pending a true induced-subgraph-extraction primitive. Checking each of the four against the actual current codebase (prompted directly by having just built `NWdef::extract_subgraph()` for `nwcohesion`, unit 59, and wanting to know whether anything else could now reuse it) found three of the four already fully done by earlier, undocumented-against-this-specific-line session work: size/density via `nwego` (unit 25, direct sparse-accessor computation, no subgraph needed) and effective-size via `nwburt` (unit 17's re-audit, direct formula). Only composition/diversity was genuinely missing - and, once designed, turned out not to need subgraph extraction either: Blau's (1977) heterogeneity index (`1 - sum(p_k^2)` over the proportions of each category among ego's alters) is a pure per-node attribute aggregation over a neighbor list, exactly the shape `calculate_alterstat()` already existed to compute - so it was added as a new `stat` branch there (and independently, by design, in `calculate_alterstat_hop()` too, matching that function's own established "kept in lockstep by construction, not by delegation" convention) rather than as a new subgraph-consuming command. `BlauIndex(vals)` itself is a small, pure-math standalone Mata function (distinct from the two aggregation loops' own deliberate independence) computing the index via `uniqrows()` category counting. Missing-value handling matches every other `calculate_alterstat()` stat (dropped before aggregating); zero-alter egos return missing (matching `mean`/`min`/`max`/`sd`'s convention, not `sum`/`count`'s 0-default) - a single-alter ego correctly returns exactly 0 (one category, p=1, no heterogeneity), not missing. Verified via an isolated Mata probe before any `.ado`/test changes: a 5-node star with alter categories split 2/1/1 gives Blau=.625 (hand-computed: `1-(.5^2+.25^2+.25^2)`), an all-identical-category star gives exactly 0, and a 2-node isolate pair gives missing for both nodes. `nwaltergen.ado`'s dispatch regex extended to accept `diversity` alongside the existing `mean\|sum\|min\|max\|sd\|count`; `nwgen.ado`'s own shortcut pre-check regex updated identically so `nwgen x = diversity(alter.y)` dispatches correctly, matching every other stat's existing shortcut support. New `cscripts/test_nwaltergen.do` coverage: the hand-verified 2/1/1-split star (exact value + all-single-alter nodes at exactly 0), an all-identical-category network (exactly 0), a zero-alter isolate pair (missing for both), and `nwgen`'s own dispatch equivalence against direct `nwaltergen` - dev mode clean; production-mode copy also verified clean against the rebuilt `lnwcommands.mlib`. Full `cscripts/` sweep (102 files, both modes): same 11 pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this stat's own new tests plus the Mata-core change's production-mode re-verification). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 61: `nwqap predict()` - fitted dyad-level values captured at the one point they're genuinely meaningful, closing out Stage 4's `predict` item

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `predict(newnetname)` option for `nwqap`, plus a new standalone `transformOutOfLong()` Mata function (the exact inverse of the pre-existing `transformIntoLong()`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `docs/CERTIFICATION.md`'s own Pending table had previously recorded, correctly, that no native postestimation `predict` can work after `nwqap` returns: `nwqap`'s own `ereturn post` (posting QAP-permutation-based `e(V)`, not a classical covariance) happens against the ORIGINAL node-level dataset, but `type()`'s own actual estimation (`quietly `type' `formula'`, run once on the real observed data - not one of the null-model permutation draws) happens earlier, against a transient dyad-level long-format dataset built inside a `preserve` block that gets `restore`d away before `ereturn post` ever runs - so `e(sample)`/the current dataset never correspond to what `type()` actually fit against by the time a user could call `predict`. Rather than trying to force Stata's native postestimation machinery to survive across that `restore` (a much larger, riskier change to this file's whole data-management structure), `predict(newnetname)` instead calls `type()`'s own native `predict` immediately after that one real regression run, DURING the exact window where the dyad-level dataset is still live and the fit is genuinely current - capturing whatever statistic that regression command reports by default (`Pr(y=1)` for `logit`/`probit`/`cloglog`, the fitted mean for `regress`, etc. - deliberately not hardcoded per-command, so any `type()` the user chooses is supported automatically). The captured dyad-level column is reshaped back into an n x n matrix via the new `transformOutOfLong()` (mirroring `transformIntoLong()`'s own column-major indexing convention exactly, proven correct by construction as its direct inverse) and materialized as a new valued network via `nwset` - which already provides non-destructive name-collision handling (auto-rename with a warning) for free, so no new collision-handling code was needed. The diagonal (always missing pre-estimation, since every network/attribute IV column has its own diagonal explicitly set missing before the regression runs - excluding self-ties from dyadic estimation) is forced to a clean 0 in the resulting network rather than left holding a raw missing value. Certified via `cscripts/test_nwqap.do`'s extended coverage: reusing the file's own existing `wdv = 2*iv1` exact-linear-relationship network (coefficient exactly 2, ~0 intercept) under `type(regress)` gives an EXACTLY checkable result - the fitted network must reproduce the observed `wdv` network almost exactly (max absolute difference < 1e-6), not merely "look plausible", since a perfect noiseless linear fit has no residual error to obscure a construction bug; a separate `type(logit)` case checks only sanity bounds (all fitted probabilities in [0,1], since a real MLE fit isn't hand-derivable the way a perfect linear relationship is); the diagonal-forced-to-0 property is checked directly; and calling `predict()` twice with the same name confirms the expected auto-rename-on-collision behavior. Both modes (dev + production, the latter re-verified against no Mata-core change at all - `transformOutOfLong()` lives in `nwqap.ado` itself, alongside the pre-existing `transformIntoLong()`/`permute_net()`, not in `unw_core.do`). Full `cscripts/` sweep (102 files, both modes): same 11 pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this option's own new tests; no other command depends on `predict()` or `transformOutOfLong()`). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 62: `nwcug condition(census)` - dyad-census/reciprocity-conditioned CUG draws, closing out Stage 4's last CUG item

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `condition(density\|census)` option for `nwcug` (default `density`, unchanged behavior) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The Pending table previously described dyad-census/reciprocity-conditioned CUG generation as "not yet built". Checking `nwrandom.ado` directly (rather than assuming the prior note was still accurate) found its `census()` conditioning - `dyadcensusGenerator(nodes, mutual, asym)`, placing an exact requested mutual/asymmetric dyad count at randomly-chosen dyad positions - already fully implemented and working; the only genuinely missing piece was `nwcug` itself never offering a way to use it instead of `density()`. Added `condition(density\|census)` (validated via the existing `_opts_oneof` idiom, matching `tail()`'s own pattern in the same file): `condition(census)` computes the observed network's dyad census via the pre-existing `nwdyads` command (`r(_100)`/`r(_010)`, mutual/asymmetric counts) once, then passes those exact counts to `nwrandom, census()` for every draw instead of `nwrandom, density()`. Requires a directed network - checked explicitly with a clear error, since mutual/asymmetric/null dyad types are meaningless for undirected ties (every dyad is simply tied or not). Certified via a strong, structure-independent argument rather than an arbitrary `stat()`'s null-distribution shape: since `dyadcensusGenerator()` places the EXACT requested mutual/asym counts on every single draw (only their positions are randomized), `nwdyads`' own `reciprocity` statistic is deterministically IDENTICAL across every draw when used as `stat()` - a hand-built 4-node directed network with a known dyad census (1 mutual, 2 asymmetric, 3 null dyads, reciprocity exactly 1/3) gives `sd_null` indistinguishable from 0 (floating-point noise only, ~5.6e-17) and `mean_null` exactly matching the observed value, `p_greater`/`p_less` both exactly 1 (every draw ties the observed value under both directions) - a far stronger correctness argument than checking one arbitrary statistic's plausibility. Also certified: the undirected-network guard rejects cleanly, an invalid `condition()` value is rejected via the same `_opts_oneof` mechanism as `tail()`, and `condition(density)` (both the default and passed explicitly) reproduces the file's pre-existing, unmodified density-conditioning behavior exactly - no regression from adding the option. `cscripts/test_nwcug.do` extended accordingly; both modes clean (no `unw_core.do` change - pure `.ado`-level wiring of two already-existing commands). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this option's own new tests; built entirely from two already-certified pre-existing primitives, `nwrandom`'s `census()` and `nwdyads`). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 63: `nwtoedge comparevars()` - ego/alter comparison columns, closing out Stage 5's last two items (which turned out to be one item)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `comparevars(varlist)`/`comparemode(string)` options for `nwtoedge` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s Stage 5 list carried two separate open items - "dyadic dataset export (general, not just dyad-census)" and "ego/alter comparison variables" - that turned out, on inspection, to be nearly the same gap. `nwtoedge` already existed and already did general dyadic dataset export (an edgelist with `_ego`/`_alter`/tie-value columns per network, plus `egovars()`/`altervars()` for raw sender/receiver attribute columns) - this was simply never checked off. The one genuinely missing piece was a *comparison* between ego's and alter's values (not just the two raw values side by side) - added as `comparevars(varlist)`/`comparemode(string)`, reusing `nwexpand`'s own already-certified `same`/`dist`/`absdist`/`distinv`/`absdistinv`/`sender`/`receiver` vocabulary internally rather than reimplementing any of it: each compare variable is expanded into its own dyadic network via `nwexpand` (auto-named `mode_varname`, matching `nwexpand`'s own default naming) and simply appended to the same network list `nwtoedge` already merges - so the entire rest of the pipeline (directed/`full` detection, tie extraction, the `(ego,alter)` merge) needed zero changes, since it already treats every entry in the list identically regardless of origin. **A real, previously-unforeseen bug found and fixed before any test passed**: the first working version omitted `nwexpand`'s own `network()` option, so the comparison network fell back to `nwexpand`'s generic `n1`..`nK` node labels instead of the real network's actual labels - silently breaking the `merge m:n (ego alter)` step, which then treated the comparison network's dyads as an entirely different node set and produced the UNION of both label sets (32 rows instead of the correct 16 for a 4-node full edgelist) rather than a properly matched dataset. Confirmed via a direct probe (`list, clean` on the merged result, showing both an `A/B/C/D`-labeled block and a separate `n1/n2/n3/n4`-labeled block) before fixing by passing `network(`firstnet')` (the netlist's own first network, whose labels every other network in a coherent netlist must already share) alongside `nodes()`. Directional comparisons (`dist`/`distinv`/`sender`/`receiver`) correctly trigger `nwtoedge`'s own pre-existing "any directed network in the list forces `full`" rule automatically, with no new logic needed - verified directly: a `comparemode(dist)` column on an undirected substantive network still yields all 12 ordered pairs (not 6), with the sign correctly flipping between `(A,C)` and `(C,A)`. Certified via `cscripts/test_nwtoedge.do`'s extended coverage: an exact `same_x` count/sum on a hand-built 4-node network (1 matching pair, hand-verified), the `dist_x` full/sign-flip case, and confirming `comparevars()` coexists with `egovars()`/`altervars()` rather than replacing them. Both modes clean (no `unw_core.do` change - pure `.ado`-level composition of two already-certified commands). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Also fixed, found adjacent to this work: a pre-existing malformed SMCL line in `nwtoedge.ado`'s own doc header (`{synopt:{opt compress}Compress edgelist` missing its closing `{p_end}` and enclosing braces) that would have corrupted rendering of every option listed after it, including the two new ones - a one-line fix, not a broader doc audit. Certified at **L2** (this option's own new tests; built from two already-certified pre-existing commands, `nwtoedge` and `nwexpand`). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 64: `nwcommunity algorithm(labelprop)` - label propagation, Stage 6's first item, with a genuine, empirically-confirmed correctness finding about its own randomization

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `algorithm(louvain\|labelprop)` and `seed(int)` options for `nwcommunity`, plus a new standalone `LabelPropagation()` Mata function and `NWdef::detect_communities_labelprop()` method | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Stage 6 ("Advanced graph functionality") was entirely untouched before this unit. Label propagation (Raghavan, Albert & Kumar 2007) was chosen as the first, most tractable item - the roadmap's own text already framed it as "a cheap addition to the same `NWdef` Louvain infrastructure", which held up: the new `LabelPropagation(W)` function reuses Louvain's own `nw_community_kin()` (sum of a node's edge weight into a given community) and `nw_community_denserelabel()` (remap to dense 1..k ids) helpers directly, needing no new primitives. **A real, empirically-confirmed correctness finding, not merely a style choice**: the first working version mirrored Louvain's own fixed 1..n sweep order and "prefer the lowest-indexed tied community" deterministic tie-break (this session's usual reproducibility-first convention) - and failed on the simplest possible test case, collapsing two triangles joined by a single bridge edge into ONE community instead of splitting cleanly at the bridge. Root-caused directly, not just observed: a fixed check order means whichever community is examined first in a tie (a genuine tie exists at the bridge nodes, each equally connected to its own triangle and, via the bridge, to the other) always wins, and once one bridge node crosses over, the bias cascades until everything merges - the exact systematic failure mode true label propagation's OWN randomization exists specifically to prevent, unlike Louvain's modularity-GAIN-driven search, which has no comparable directional bias. Fixed by genuinely randomizing both sweep order (`unorder(n)`) and tie-breaking (uniform random pick among tied-max candidates via `runiform()`) every sweep, with reproducibility exposed via a new `seed(int)` option (`nwcommunity`'s own version of `nwcug`'s established `seed()` precedent for RNG-dependent commands) rather than false algorithmic determinism.
