Overall picture after this unit: the original 566x R-vs-Stata gap (unit 80) was significantly self-inflicted (an inefficient TNT proposal, an overly conservative convergence test, and now a large-N architectural bug in MPLE construction that would have blocked ANY sufficiently large directed model regardless of the ERGM terms involved) rather than an inherent Mata performance ceiling. With all three now fixed, the remaining gap is concentrated specifically in GWESP/shared-partner-heavy models (40-68x) rather than being uniform across all models (non-GWESP models, even at 10x the original benchmark's node count, are within roughly 3-28x) - a clear, evidence-based signal for where a future C/C++ kernel would actually pay off, per the user's own stated decision rule, rather than a generic "rewrite the sampler in C" conclusion. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 82: incremental shared-partner cache for GWESP - implemented, correct, and honestly NOT adopted by default

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmGraph::enable_sp_cache()`/`sp_adjust()`/`shared_partners()` - incremental shared-partner cache | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Per unit 81's own decision framework ("GWESP-involving models sit at 40-68x; this is the clear next Mata-level lever"), implemented the incremental shared-partner cache the roadmap had anticipated since unit 68: `ErgmGraph` gains an opt-in `sp_counts` asarray (canonical `(a,b)` pair -> current shared-partner count), built from scratch in O(sum_i deg_i^2) via `enable_sp_cache()` (iterating, for each node, every UNORDERED pair of its own neighbors - the corrected, non-double-counting formulation unit 68's own prototype had already worked out, re-derived here since that prototype's code was never adopted into the shipped file), then maintained incrementally by `toggle()` in O(deg_i+deg_j) per toggle (adding tie (i,j) makes j a new shared-partner contributor for every pair (i,x) where x is a neighbor of j, and symmetrically i for pairs (j,x); removing undoes exactly that - captured from `neighbors_out(i)`/`neighbors_out(j)` BEFORE either branch of `toggle()` mutates anything, then applied after). `stat_gwesp()`/`change_gwesp()` now call `shared_partners()` instead of `common_neighbors()` directly - O(1) when the cache is enabled, falling back to the identical `common_neighbors()` computation otherwise, so this is architecturally a pure performance option with zero effect on either function's own return value. Certified two ways in a new permanent test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_spcache.do` (both modes): (1) an independent brute-force cross-check (every cached `shared_partners()` value against `common_neighbors()`, checked for EVERY pair in the graph, not just toggled dyads) across 2000+ toggles spanning 6 networks of varying size and density (including a fully-connected case), zero mismatches; (2) end-to-end equivalence of `stat_gwesp()`/`change_gwesp()`/`ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` with the cache on vs off, byte-identical results. |
| **Wired into `nwergm.ado`, benchmarked, and then deliberately REVERTED based on direct evidence** | ✅ (built and tested) | ✅ | N/A (a disclosed non-adoption, not a shipped default) | ✅ | Initially wired to auto-enable whenever a `gwesp()` term is requested (the obvious integration point). Re-running the two GWESP benchmarks from unit 81 (per the user's own explicit instruction to re-benchmark immediately after this change) gave a genuine surprise: the 100-node GWESP benchmark went from 23.1s to 39.3s - SLOWER, not faster - in a clean, controlled A/B test isolating the cache as the only variable (same `nwergm.ado` build otherwise, same seed, same network, same fitted theta, identical resulting coefficients/iteration count confirming the RNG trajectory was bit-for-bit identical either way). Root-caused via a sequence of narrowing, isolated repros rather than accepted at face value: (1) an exact replication of the real scenario (real 100-node network, real fitted theta `(-3.0787, -0.1183)`, real two-term edges+gwesp model, real MCMC settings) as a standalone Mata script reproduced the same slowdown (3.796s -> 6.496s for a single `ErgmMCMCSample()` call), ruling out any `.ado`-layer confound; (2) decomposing that call into "evaluation-only" (propose + `full_change()`, never toggle) versus "always-toggle" (propose + `full_change()` + unconditionally toggle) isolated the two competing effects directly: evaluation alone is ~1.8x FASTER with the cache (1.062s -> 0.587s per 50,000 steps - confirming the O(1) lookup genuinely is cheaper), while always-toggling is ~1.45x SLOWER (0.990s -> 1.436s - the `toggle()`-time maintenance cost, primarily two `neighbors_out()` calls each allocating a fresh array via `asarray_keys()`, plus the `sp_adjust()` loop, is real and substantial); (3) measuring the ACTUAL Metropolis-Hastings acceptance rate for this real, fitted model found it to be 92.9% - TNT's own acceptance rate on a converged, well-fitted sparse model is very high (not the "mostly rejected" intuition a fresh implementer might reasonably have), meaning `toggle()`'s own maintenance cost fires on nearly every single proposal, not occasionally; (4) a systematic degree sweep (n=200, matched high acceptance rate 83-92% via a similarly strong theta, same seed both ways) found a genuine, quantified crossover: the cache is 1.6-2.0x SLOWER at degree 4-20, and only becomes a net win (1.6x faster) around degree ~30-40+ - both of unit 81's own GWESP benchmark networks (~4 and ~5.9 average degree) sit well below that crossover. **Conclusion, evidence-based rather than assumed**: the "obvious next optimisation" the benchmark data pointed to does NOT pay off for the realistic sparse networks this package's own benchmark suite (deliberately built to reflect common real-world social-network density) represents - it would help genuinely dense networks, which v1's own target scope does not particularly emphasize. The auto-enable wiring was reverted (both call sites - the main `nwergm` program's own `gwesp()` block, and `nwergm_simulate`'s own per-draw graph construction) rather than kept as a regression; the cache machinery itself remains in `unw_ergm.do`, fully certified, available for a future degree-aware auto-enable heuristic or an explicit opt-in control option, neither of which is implemented yet (see `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`). This is the same "implement, measure directly against realistic benchmarks, and report honestly even when the obvious optimization does not pan out" discipline this project used for the batch-means variance estimator (unit 80) - a second data point that this project's own performance intuitions, however reasonable-sounding, are being verified rather than assumed. |

This unit's own real lesson for future performance work on this package: **an optimization's own net effect can depend on properties of the WORKLOAD (here, the Metropolis-Hastings acceptance rate of a well-fitted model, and the network's own degree) that are not obvious from the algorithmic complexity of the operation being optimized alone** - O(1) is not automatically better than O(degree) once the cost of MAINTAINING the O(1) structure is counted, and that maintenance cost's own weight depends on how often it is paid, which depends on the specific model/data being fit, not just the term/network-size combination. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 130 files) after reverting the wiring: same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-81, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (the cache machinery itself; the non-adoption decision is a documented finding, not a certified capability).

## Harmonisation phase, unit 83: native (C) MCMC backend - the Mata-first restriction explicitly relaxed, with dramatic measured results

The user explicitly relaxed this project's own earlier standing preference for Mata over C/C++, based directly on units 80-82's own accumulated evidence (the GWESP gap surviving both the TNT/MPLE fixes and the shared-partner-cache attempt), and authorized moving performance-critical `nwergm` code to a native Stata plugin wherever profiling supports it - explicitly not a mandate to rewrite the whole package, and explicitly requiring the existing Mata implementation to be preserved as a reference/fallback, not replaced.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fresh verification of Statnet's own current C implementation (not relying on this project's earlier Part I study alone, per the user's own explicit instruction) | ✅ | n/a (research) | n/a | ✅ | Direct fetches against the current `statnet/ergm` GitHub source (August 2026) confirm: `src/changestats_spcache.c`'s shared-partner cache is a per-statistic hash table (`StoreStrictDyadMapUInt`, via `kh_init`), updated in its own `U_CHANGESTAT_FN` on every toggle at **O(degree) per toggle** (iterating the affected nodes' own edge sets) - the SAME asymptotic cost as this project's own unit-82 Mata cache, not a smarter algorithm; and only instantiated for statistics that actually need it (`I_CHANGESTAT_FN`/`F_CHANGESTAT_FN` lifecycle), matching `ErgmGraph::sp_cache_enabled`'s own opt-in design. `src/MHproposals.c`'s TNT proposal (`MH_TNT`) confirms the same P=0.5/Q=0.5 tie-vs-dyad branching and `DP=P*ndyads`/`DO=DP/Q` Hastings-ratio construction already implemented since unit 80 (`TNT_LR_E`/`TNT_LR_DE`/`TNT_LR_DN` macros), exactly one toggle proposed per call, via a `DyadGen` random-edge abstraction giving O(1)-ish edge selection - confirming `ergm_propose_tnt()`'s own live-`elist` design is architecturally the same approach, not a different one this project happened to miss. **The conclusion this comparison actually supports**: Statnet's own C code pays the SAME O(degree) toggle-time cost nwergm's Mata code would pay with the cache enabled - the reason Statnet can afford to always maintain it while nwergm's own unit-82 finding said not to is that the same operation costs orders of magnitude less per call in compiled code, not that Statnet uses a fundamentally different algorithm. This is exactly the profile (correct algorithm, expensive per-operation interpreter overhead) where moving to native code helps, and is the direct evidentiary basis for this unit's work below - not merely the user's own prior expectation taken on faith. |
| Mata primitive microbenchmark, isolating where per-step cost actually goes | ✅ | n/a (measurement) | n/a | ✅ | `dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/50_microbench_primitives.do`: at realistic sparse degree (~8-12 after construction, both directed and undirected, n=100/500), `has_edge()`/`change_edges()`/`change_mutual()`/`change_nodematch()` each cost ~0.26-0.38 microseconds/call; `ergm_propose_tnt()` costs ~1.3-5.6 microseconds/call; `G.toggle()` costs ~3.2-3.8 microseconds/call; **`change_gwesp()` costs 17.35-29.34 microseconds/call - a 50-100x multiple of the other terms**, tracking `common_neighbors()`'s own 7.4-11.5 microsecond cost (which `change_gwesp()` calls up to `2*deg(i)+1` times per invocation) and `neighbors_out()`'s own 5.9-7.1 microsecond cost (a fresh array allocation via `asarray_keys()` on every call). At this degree the actual number of shared-partner comparisons `change_gwesp()` performs is small (a handful) - the cost is concentrated in Mata's own per-call/per-hash-lookup/per-allocation overhead compounding over those few operations, not in the algorithm's own complexity. This is the direct, measured confirmation (not an assumption) that the remaining GWESP gap is an interpreter-overhead problem, precisely the case the user's own instructions said native code should target. |
| **Native MCMC kernel**, `native/ergm_mcmc.c` (a genuine Stata C plugin, SPI 3.0, built via `native/Makefile`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Implements the ENTIRE burnin+sampling Metropolis-Hastings loop (propose, evaluate every active term's change statistic, accept/reject, toggle, record) natively, crossing the Mata/Stata/native boundary exactly ONCE per `ErgmMCMCSample()`/`ErgmMCMCSampleDiag()` call (i.e. once per MCMLE iteration), per the user's own explicit "do not call C on every toggle from Mata" instruction. Scope deliberately narrow, matching the four terms the R-vs-Stata benchmark suite already exercises: `edges`, `mutual`, `nodematch`, `gwesp` (TNT and uniform proposals, directed and undirected graphs) - any model using a term outside this set transparently falls back to the unchanged, fully-preserved Mata sampler (see below), decided ONCE per `nwergm` call by `ErgmNativeSetup()`, never inside the MCMC loop itself (term dispatch inside the C loop is a plain `switch()` over a small pre-resolved array, no string parsing or allocation in the hot path). **Graph representation**: a single open-addressing hash table (tombstone deletion, periodic compaction) maps a canonical dyad key to its row index in a dynamic live-edge-list array, giving O(1)-average `has_edge()` AND O(1)-average TNT tie-pick/removal in one structure (the C analogue of `ErgmGraph`'s own `adjout`+`elist`/`edgepos` split, unit 80) - per-node adjacency arrays (needed only for GWESP's shared-partner traversal) are allocated only when a `gwesp` term is present, so directed-only models never pay for them. **No incremental shared-partner cache is implemented** - unit 82's own finding (cache maintenance cost exceeds lookup savings below degree ~30-40) has no reason to flip in compiled code just because both sides of that tradeoff get cheaper together, so the on-demand `common_neighbors()`-style traversal is a direct, correctness-preserving port of unw_ergm.do's own default (cache-disabled) path - a deliberate, evidence-based choice, not an oversight. **Boundary data**: the edge list (the one genuinely dyad-scale object) crosses via Stata dataset variables in a dedicated, isolated frame (`SF_vdata`/`st_store`/`st_addvar`/`st_addobs`, never `st_matrix()` - the same package-wide rule unit 81/`docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md` established); small model-scale data (theta, term codes/decays, the observed statistic) crosses via one space-separated argument string. **RNG**: a self-contained xorshift128+ generator seeded once per call from a value drawn via Mata's own `runiform()` (so it is itself reproducible under `set seed`), iterated independently thereafter inside the plugin - the native and Mata backends do NOT share an RNG stream or produce bit-identical sample paths for the same seed (an explicit, disclosed non-goal - see the cross-certification row below for what IS required and verified instead). `G` is rebuilt from the native run's own final edge list after every call, exactly reproducing `ErgmMCMCSample()`'s existing in-place-mutation contract that `ErgmMCMLE()`'s sequential-MCMLE outer loop depends on. Two real Stata Plugin Interface contract details were confirmed by direct trial rather than assumed from general SPI documentation (both now documented in `ergm_mcmc.c`'s own header and `native/smoke`'s throwaway probe that found them): the plugin's exported C entry point must be named exactly `stata_call` regardless of the Stata-side `program name, plugin` name chosen, and the `plugin call ..., "args"` string arrives as a SINGLE `argv[0]` token, not pre-split on whitespace by Stata - both corrected before writing the real kernel, not discovered by trial-and-error against it. |
| Native/Mata cross-certification | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `cscripts/test_nwergm_native.do` (both modes): (1) `ErgmNativeSetup()` eligibility - accepts edges/mutual/nodematch/gwesp combinations, rejects a `nodecov` model (a term genuinely outside the native scope) - exactly as designed; (2) **statistical equivalence**: the native and Mata backends, started from the IDENTICAL hand-built network (deterministic construction, no RNG) at the SAME theta/burnin/interval/samplesize (2000 draws, interval 5), produce sampled sufficient-statistic means that agree within the Monte Carlo error implied by `ergm_lag1_autocorr()`'s own autocorrelation-inflated standard error (the same inflation `ErgmMCMLE()`'s own final variance step already uses, reused here rather than an invented tolerance) - covered for both a directed dyad-independent model (edges+mutual+nodematch, n=80) and an undirected GWESP model (edges+gwesp, n=80); this is the correct standard per the user's own explicit instruction ("they need not produce identical stochastic trajectories... but distributions of sampled statistics must agree"), not bit-identical trajectories, since the two backends deliberately use independent RNG streams. (3) **Self-consistency**: after a native call, recomputing `M.full_statistic()` from scratch on `G`'s own rebuilt state matches the native run's own last reported statistic row to within 1e-6 (directed case) / 7.5e-12 (undirected case) - confirming the edge list the plugin writes back genuinely IS the network the reported numbers describe, not merely plausible-looking output. |
| **Benchmark suite re-run with the native backend engaged** (`dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/`) | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (measurement) | ✅ | Same five benchmarks, same default settings, only the backend changed (native auto-selected wherever eligible - all five benchmark models are). Wall time: benchmark 1 (30-node dir edges+mutual) 6.6-7.0s → **0.311s** (R: 0.29s, ratio ~1.07x); benchmark 2 (100-node dir edges+mutual+nodematch) 13.746s → **0.495s** (R: 0.484s, ratio ~1.02x); benchmark 3 (100-node undir edges+gwesp) 22.065s → **0.419s** (R: 0.326s, ratio ~1.29x - down from ~68x); benchmark 4 (500-node sparse edges+nodematch+gwesp, still hits the 20-iteration cap either way - convergence-at-scale is a separate, not-yet-investigated item, see `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`) 93.174s → **3.932s** (R: 2.293s, ratio ~1.72x - down from ~40.6x); benchmark 5 (1000-node directed control, no gwesp, also hits the cap) 27.762s → **4.883s**, which is now FASTER than R's own 9.177s (ratio ~0.53x). Coefficients/standard errors agree closely with R on every benchmark, unchanged in character from before this unit (the native backend changes execution speed, not the statistical model being fit). All five benchmarks are now within the user's own stated "excellent" GWESP target (~2-5x) or better, and the two non-GWESP benchmarks are at effective parity with or faster than Statnet - a dramatically stronger result than the "<10x good, ~2-5x excellent" target this unit's own governing instructions set as the bar. |
| Full `cscripts/` ERGM regression sweep, both dev and production mode, after wiring | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All 15 `test_nwergm_*.do` files (14 pre-existing + the new `test_nwergm_native.do`) pass clean in both dev mode (`do unw_ergm.do`) and production mode (against the freshly rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` and `lib/plugins/ergm_mcmc.plugin`, built via `native/Makefile`'s own `macos` target). No regressions relative to the unit-82 baseline. |
| Cross-platform status | ✅ (macOS) / documented-not-built (Windows/Linux) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `native/Makefile` builds a universal (arm64+x86_64) macOS plugin, verified on this development machine (Apple Silicon, Stata 18 BE). Windows (`cl /LD`) and Linux (`gcc -shared`, note the Unix-specific `_unix.plugin` naming Stata's own plugin loader expects) build recipes are documented in the Makefile's own header comment but NOT built or verified - no Windows/Linux toolchain was available in this environment. `ErgmNativeAvailable()` checks `fileexists()` on the expected plugin path and returns 0 (never errors) when absent, so a platform without a compiled plugin transparently and silently uses the unmodified, fully-functional Mata backend - the package's own statistical behavior and Stata-facing syntax are identical regardless of which platform's plugin situation applies, satisfying the user's own explicit cross-platform requirement even though acceleration itself is currently macOS-only. |
| Mata reference implementation preserved unchanged | ✅ | ✅ (all pre-existing tests still pass verbatim) | ✅ | ✅ | `ErgmMCMCSample()`/`ErgmMCMCSampleDiag()` gained a native-branch check at their own top (`if (M.native_enabled) { ...; return }`) but every line below that branch is byte-for-byte what it was before this unit - confirmed by the full pre-existing test suite passing unchanged. `ErgmModel` gained six new fields (`native_enabled`/`native_termcodes`/`native_decays`/`native_attr`/`native_proposal`/`native_lastaccept`) purely to carry this config across calls (Mata class instances exhibit reference semantics across function calls - the same property `ErgmGraph::toggle()` already relies on for sequential MCMLE - avoiding the need for genuine Mata "global" variables, which `set matastrict on` does not support declaring inside function bodies the way an early draft of this unit incorrectly assumed, confirmed by direct trial). The Mata backend remains the reference/fallback/debugging implementation/certification oracle exactly as the user's own instructions required - nothing was deleted or replaced. |
| `nwergm_simulate`'s own per-draw loop deliberately NOT wired to native | ✅ (deliberate exclusion, documented) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | That loop calls `ErgmMCMCSample()` once PER SIMULATED NETWORK with `samplesize=1` - wiring native there would mean one plugin round-trip (frame create/drop, program define, dataset construction) per simulated draw, exactly the "crossing the boundary too often" architecture the user's own instructions warn against for a single-row payload. `__nwergm_last_M.native_enabled` simply stays at its `ErgmModel::init()` default of 0 on this path (nothing needed to be added to keep it Mata-only) - documented in `nwergm.ado`'s own comment at that call site rather than silently left unconsidered. |

**Answering the user's own explicit conditional** ("do not move to C++ unless GWESP remains badly behind after the cache"): unit 82 established that condition WAS satisfied (the cache did not close the gap for realistic sparse networks) - this unit is the direct response, and the measured result (40-68x → 1.02-1.72x across all five benchmarks, two of them now at or beyond parity with Statnet) is a much larger win than the "reduce the gap" framing anticipated. The remaining ~1.3-1.7x GWESP-specific gap and the still-unconverged 500-/1000-node benchmarks are both understood, disclosed, and separately tracked (`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`) rather than treated as solved. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes) after this unit: same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-82, no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 84: MCMLE convergence-at-scale root-caused and fixed - an invalid statistical test, not a slow or stuck optimizer

With native execution making iteration count cheap to investigate directly (per the user's own updated priority: convergence at scale now matters more than further speed), this unit did exactly the iteration-by-iteration Mata-vs-Statnet comparison the user requested on the same 500-node sparse GWESP benchmark network (benchmark 4) that had been hitting the 20-iteration cap.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Fresh, direct iteration-by-iteration comparison against Statnet on the identical network** | ✅ | n/a (research) | n/a | ✅ | `dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/60_verbose_500sparse_r.R` runs `ergm(..., verbose=TRUE)` on the exact `net500.csv`/`attr500.csv` benchmark 4 uses. Statnet's own real trace: converges in 4 iterations, reporting `Test statistic: T^2 = 5.47/7.95/12.01, with 3 free parameter(s) and 84.9/129.4/203.6 degrees of freedom` - **non-integer, far-below-raw-sample-size degrees of freedom that grow across iterations** ("increasing sample size" is printed explicitly when a test fails). `nwergm`'s own `verbose` option (already implemented) run on the SAME network showed theta stabilizing near its final value from iteration 1 onward (matching Statnet's converged estimate closely - confirming the OPTIMIZER was never the problem) while `Fstat` stayed absurdly inflated (20-640) against a FIXED `Fcrit=3.79` computed from the raw `samplesize=3000` for the FULL 20-iteration cap, never once passing. |
| **Root cause: the per-iteration Hotelling T²/F convergence test treated a thinned, autocorrelated MCMC chain as if it were an i.i.d. sample of size `samplesize`** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The T²→F transformation `ErgmMCMLE()` already used (unit 80) is exact only for i.i.d. draws. `samp` is thinned by `interval` (default 50) but not fully decorrelated - at 500-1000 node GWESP-involving models the autocorrelation is severe enough that the raw `samplesize=3000` wildly overstates how much independent information the sample actually carries about `Dbar`, making the test's own implied confidence region far too tight to ever contain a genuinely converged Dbar under ordinary Monte Carlo noise. Statnet's own "confidence" termination method already accounts for this (confirmed directly from its own printed output above, not inferred) via an effective-sample-size-discounted degrees of freedom, not the raw recorded draw count - the piece missing here. |
| **Fix: reuse the existing (already-certified) lag-1 autocorrelation machinery one step earlier - inside the per-iteration test, not only in the final one-off variance-reporting pass** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `ergm_lag1_autocorr(D)` (unit 80, previously called only after the MCMLE loop for final SE reporting) is now also called INSIDE the loop: `V`'s own diagonal is inflated per parameter by `(1+rho)/(1-rho)`, and an effective sample size `neff = samplesize / mean(infl)` (floored at `p+2` to keep the F distribution well-defined) replaces the raw `samplesize` in `T2`/`Fstat`/`Fcrit` - `invF(p, neff-p, confidence)`. The Newton step itself (`delta`/`mahal`/`gamma`/the theta update) deliberately still uses the UNINFLATED `V`/`Vinv` - theta was never the problem, so this fix touches only the statistical TEST deciding when to stop, not the optimizer that was already tracking Statnet's own value correctly. |
| **Result: all five R-vs-Stata benchmarks now converge in 1-2 MCMLE iterations** | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (measurement) | ✅ | Re-running the full suite with the native backend AND this fix, same default settings: benchmark 1 (30-node) 0.311s→**0.278s**, 1 iteration; benchmark 2 (100-node dir) 0.495s→**0.306s**, 1 iteration; benchmark 3 (100-node GWESP) 0.419s→**0.405s**, 1 iteration; benchmark 4 (500-node sparse GWESP, previously hit the 20-iteration cap both before AND after unit 83) 3.932s→**2.785s**, converges in 1 iteration; benchmark 5 (1000-node control, also previously capped) 4.883s→**3.267s**, converges in 1 iteration. Separately, `dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/05_iteration_variability.do` (20 refits of the original 30-node network at different seeds, unit 80's own variability probe) went from niter ranging 2-20 (mean 4.65, sd 4.18, 19/20 converged) to **exactly 2 iterations on all 20 seeds, 20/20 converged** - the remaining uniform 2 (not 1) is explained, not merely observed: the trust-region cap on the first Newton step's own Mahalanobis norm typically damps iteration 1's own step length below 1 (`gamma<1`), and convergence is only ever declared when `Fstat <= Fcrit & gamma==1` - so a genuinely well-converged theta at iteration 1 still correctly waits for an undamped iteration 2 before the test is allowed to pass. |
| **A genuine, honestly-disclosed remaining open question, not silently resolved**: the fixed test's own reported `neff` can be very small (35.7 out of 3000 raw draws on benchmark 4's default settings) - a correct diagnosis of severe autocorrelation, not a bug, but it raises whether `nwergm`'s own DEFAULT `mcmcinterval` (50) provides adequate mixing at 500+ node GWESP-involving scale, independent of the convergence-test fix above | Not yet resolved | Partial (exploratory only) | N/A | ✅ (disclosed as open, not silently shipped) | Two direct experiments, both on benchmark 4: (1) re-running with `mcmcinterval(500) mcmcburnin(5000)` (10x/1.7x defaults) still converged in 1 iteration, cost only 3.293s (native execution keeps a much larger MCMC budget affordable), and moved the gwesp coefficient from 0.0179 to 0.0334 - much closer to Statnet's own 0.0371, suggestive that thin mixing costs real accuracy, not just test validity; (2) a 5-seed sweep at DEFAULT settings (`edges` -4.417 to -4.449, `nodematch` -0.042 to +0.0006, `gwesp` 0.020 to 0.044) found Statnet's own converged values fall comfortably within nwergm's own natural seed-to-seed spread for `edges`/`gwesp` (the earlier single-seed 0.0179 gwesp value that looked concerning was simply an unlucky draw, not a systematic bias) - **but** the `nodematch` coefficient's ~5-seed spread (a range of ~0.043) is roughly 10x its own reported standard error (0.004), a real, if statistically imprecise (n=5), signal that the FINAL reported variance-covariance (which still relies on the same lag-1/AR(1) approximation, now shown to need a very large inflation factor at this scale) may itself still be understating true parameter uncertainty for badly-mixed models. Not fixed in this unit - deliberately left for the user's own planned "B. Inferential parity" phase (a proper spectral/HAC variance estimator, or a default `mcmcinterval` increase, informed by more than a 5-seed anecdote) rather than changing a widely-used default on limited evidence. |
| Full `cscripts/` ERGM regression sweep, both modes, after the fix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All 15 `test_nwergm_*.do` files pass unchanged in both dev and production mode - the fix changes only the internal convergence TEST inside `ErgmMCMLE()`, not any Stata-facing option, default, or return value's own meaning. |

**Answering the user's own explicit question** ("why does Statnet converge while nwergm keeps reaching the iteration cap?"): not step-size adaptation, not sample-size scheduling, not initialization, and not a stuck optimizer - theta was already landing close to the truth from the very first iteration in every case tested. The cause was specifically "Monte Carlo variance handling," exactly one of the user's own listed candidate culprits: an internally inconsistent test that used an autocorrelation-corrected variance for final reporting but a naive, uncorrected one for the per-iteration stopping decision. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 85: adaptive MCMC interval - closing unit 84's own honestly-disclosed loose end before moving on

The user explicitly asked for this loose end to be closed before starting the planned variance-estimator phase, rather than deferring it.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Adaptive `interval` growth in `ErgmMCMLE()`** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Unit 84's own effective-sample-size fix could correctly report "converged" from a genuinely tiny `neff` (35.7 out of 3000 raw draws, measured on the 500-node sparse GWESP benchmark at the then-fixed default `mcmcinterval`) - statistically valid as a test, but leaving the resulting estimate's own precision uncomfortably low. Statnet's own real trace (`60_verbose_500sparse_r.R', unit 84) already showed Statnet does not accept this either - it prints "New interval = 512" then "New interval = 1024" on its own non-converged iterations, i.e. it grows its own MCMC thinning when the achieved effective size is inadequate, rather than accepting a technically-passing but low-precision test. `ErgmMCMLE()` now computes a target `min_neff = max(200, 64*nparam)` once per fit (mirroring Statnet's own documented `MCMLE.effectiveSize=64`-per-parameter control default, `control.ergm.R`, floored at 200 so 1-2 parameter models still get a reasonably precise fit), and declares convergence only when `Fstat <= Fcrit & gamma==1 & neff >= min_neff` - whenever `neff` falls short, `interval` (now a per-iteration LOCAL, distinct from the caller-supplied `interval0` formal parameter - see the pass-by-reference note below) grows by `min(8, ceil(min_neff/neff))` for the next iteration, capped at an absolute `interval_cap=20000`. The Newton step itself is untouched - only the growth trigger and the interval used for the NEXT sampling call. |
| **A genuine Mata pass-by-reference gotcha found and fixed while writing this unit's own certification test** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Mata passes a bare-variable argument by reference: an early version of this unit's own test (`cscripts/test_nwergm_adaptive_interval.do`) passed a local Mata variable directly as `ErgmMCMLE()`'s `interval` argument and observed that variable change value in the CALLER's own scope after the call returned, because `ErgmMCMLE()` reassigns its own copy of that parameter as part of the adaptive-growth mechanism above. This was never a live production bug - `nwergm.ado`, the only real caller, always passes a literal Stata-macro-substituted number (e.g. `` `mcmcinterval' `` expanding to a bare numeral before Mata even parses the line), never a Mata variable reference - but is fixed defensively regardless: the formal parameter is now named `interval0` and left untouched; a private local `interval = interval0` is what the growth logic actually mutates. Documented in `ErgmMCMLE()`'s own header comment as a general Mata gotcha for any future caller. |
| Benchmark suite re-run with the adaptive interval on top of unit 84 | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (measurement) | ✅ | Same five benchmarks, default settings: all five still converge (none re-hit the iteration cap) in 1-3 iterations at well under 4 seconds even for the two largest networks (benchmark 4: 2.785s→3.744s, 1→3 iterations, interval grown 50→300→600; benchmark 5: 3.267s→3.989s, 1→3 iterations). Benchmarks 1 and 3 (the small/well-mixing cases) are UNCHANGED - the mechanism does not fire for them, confirmed both by direct observation and by the dedicated regression test below. Benchmark 4's own fitted `gwesp` coefficient moved from 0.0179 (unit 84, 1 iteration, `neff`=35.7) to 0.0363 - much closer to Statnet's own 0.0371 - direct evidence the extra iterations bought real estimation precision, not merely a slower path to the same number. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_adaptive_interval.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Two cases: (1) `nwergm`'s own actual production defaults (`mcmcinterval=50`, `mcmcsamplesize=3000`, `mcmcburnin=3000`) on a small network sized like the R-vs-Stata suite's own original 30-node benchmark - asserts `final_interval` EXACTLY EQUALS the requested interval (the mechanism must not fire at all for the ordinary case - a genuine no-regression guarantee, not just "still converges"); (2) a deliberately inadequate starting interval (5) on a 150-node undirected GWESP network - asserts `final_interval` strictly exceeds the requested value (growth genuinely triggered), using the native backend (unit 83) to keep the test fast. Both run in seconds. |
| Full `cscripts/` ERGM regression sweep, both modes, after this unit | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All 16 `test_nwergm_*.do` files (15 pre-existing + the new adaptive-interval test) pass clean in both dev and production mode. No regressions relative to unit 84's baseline. |
| `e(mcmc_interval_final)` - new, small diagnostic `nwergm.ado` return | ✅ | ✅ (exercised by every MCMLE fit; not independently asserted beyond the dedicated test above) | ✅ | ✅ | Exposes the interval actually used for the last MCMLE iteration, distinct from `e(mcmc_interval)` (the caller's own REQUESTED starting value, meaning unchanged) - lets a user or a future test see directly whether/how much the adaptive mechanism grew the interval for their own fit, mirroring the existing `e(mcmle_iterations)`/`e(converged)` diagnostic convention. |

Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes) after this unit: same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-84, no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 86: proper (AR/Yule-Walker) variance estimator - phase B, "inferential parity"

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fresh verification of Statnet's current variance methodology | ✅ | n/a (research) | n/a | ✅ | Direct GitHub check of `R/mcmc_se.R` (August 2026): Statnet estimates the long-run Monte Carlo covariance of the sufficient statistics via `spectrum0.mvar()` (coda package) - fitting an AR model to the MCMC series and evaluating its implied spectral density at frequency 0 (Geyer 1992's "initial sequence" method), NOT a single-lag correction. It also computes an explicit `neff = n/infl` - the SAME effective-sample-size concept units 84-85 already added to this project's own convergence test, independently confirming that mechanism's conceptual alignment with Statnet's real approach rather than being invented for this project alone. The sandwich/importance-weighting machinery around this (`H.obs - H`, IS reweighting) solves a DIFFERENT problem than nwergm has - reusing an off-theta sample - since nwergm's own final diagnostics simulation is always drawn fresh AT the exact final theta; that part of Statnet's machinery was correctly judged not applicable here, not overlooked. |
| **AR(p)/Yule-Walker long-run-variance estimator**, `ergm_ar_yw_infl()`/`ergm_spectral0_ar()` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fits AR(p) per parameter dimension via the Yule-Walker equations, selecting order by AIC among candidates 0..pmax (matching R's own `ar(method="yule-walker", aic=TRUE)` default, which `spectrum0.ar`/`spectrum0.mvar` build on) - a per-dimension approximation to Statnet's own genuinely multivariate VAR fit, a disclosed, deliberate simplification. Confirmed to be an exact STRICT GENERALIZATION of the existing lag-1 `(1+rho)/(1-rho)` correction: on a true AR(1) series both formulas agree to 3 significant figures (3.993 vs 3.994 against an analytic 4.000); on a series with negligible lag-1 autocorrelation but real AR(2) persistence, the new estimator correctly reports a materially larger inflation factor (24.4 vs lag-1's 2.0 on the SAME series) - exactly the failure mode (persistent autocorrelation invisible to a single-lag check) this unit exists to fix. |
| **Adopted as the production estimator**, replacing lag-1 in BOTH of `ErgmMCMLE()`'s own usage sites (the per-iteration convergence test's effective-sample-size calculation, unit 84/85, and the final reported variance-covariance) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Validated directly against the ONE case with an exact external ground truth available (the 5-node network `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmle.do` already certifies against real Statnet output, vcov diag edges=0.44468/mutual=2.17162): lag-1 landed at edges=0.3677/mutual=1.7475 (17-20% low, error 0.077/0.424); the new AR(p) estimator lands at edges=0.4066/mutual=2.1778 (9% low / 0.3% off, error 0.038/0.006) - roughly half the error on edges, ~68x smaller error on mutual. This is the same "implement, test against real evidence, adopt only if it wins" discipline already used for the shared-partner cache (unit 82) and the original batch-means-vs-lag-1 comparison (unit 80) - this time genuinely adopting the alternative because it measurably won on the one case where winning could actually be checked against ground truth, not merely argued for theoretically. `ergm_lag1_autocorr()` itself is left in the file, unchanged and still used by the new estimator's own certification test as its comparison baseline - not deleted. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_variance.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Three checks: (1) exact AR(1) reduction to the existing lag-1 formula; (2) AR(2) detection a lag-1-only check would miss; (3) the real-Statnet-reference comparison above, asserting the new estimator's own error is not worse than lag-1's (with generous slack for single-run MCMC noise) - a genuine "not a regression vs. the method being replaced" guarantee, not just a generic tolerance check. |
| `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own "Update (unit 83)" decision-framework note and the earlier "Variance estimator: batch-means... TRIED AND REJECTED" entry both updated to record that the variance question has now moved past lag-1 | ✅ | n/a | n/a | ✅ | See that document's own Performance section. |
| A pre-existing latent bug found and fixed while diagnosing this unit's own certification test failures: `cscripts/test_nwergm_adaptive_interval.do`'s (unit 85) network builder was a deterministic modular-offset construction (fine for pure timing microbenchmarks, where it originated) that produces near-zero mutual/reciprocated ties by construction - a genuinely degenerate case for a `mutual` term, driving an early draft of a follow-on test toward a DIVERGING fit (steplen collapsing to ~0.01, F exploding into the millions) that had nothing to do with either variance estimator or the adaptive-interval mechanism being tested | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fixed by switching to genuine Erdos-Renyi random construction (independent Bernoulli(p) per dyad) in both this file and `dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/71_variance_calibration.do` (a dev script also affected by the same issue while investigating this unit). `test_nwergm_adaptive_interval.do`'s own "growth must occur under adverse conditions" companion case was ultimately DROPPED rather than fixed further: three different model/network attempts (GWESP, edges+mutual, single edges-only, at increasingly extreme starting intervals) either hit genuine ERGM estimation instability unrelated to this mechanism or simply failed to reproduce severe-enough autocorrelation synthetically - real growth is already documented from actual production runs (benchmarks 4/5, units 84-85) with a measured precision payoff, which is stronger evidence than a contrived synthetic case would have provided. The "growth stays bounded on the ordinary case" half of that test was kept and is unaffected. |
| A genuine methodology dead-end, found and abandoned before it could produce a false conclusion: refitting the SAME observed network many times at different MCMC seeds and comparing the ACROSS-SEED spread of theta against the reported SE | ❌ (deliberately not used) | n/a | n/a | ✅ (disclosed as a dead end, not silently dropped) | This conflates two different quantities: MCMLE's own NUMERICAL precision in locating the MLE for one fixed dataset (which shrinks with more MCMC computation and, after units 84-85's own adaptive-interval fix, shrinks a LOT) versus the TRUE statistical sampling variance of the MLE as an estimator (an intrinsic property of the model/data that does NOT shrink just because the numerical fit becomes more precise). An initial attempt at this unit's own validation script produced ratios like 0.01 (reported SE 100x the empirical spread) that looked alarming until this distinction was recognized - the correct ground truth is Statnet's own reported SE on the SAME network (what `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmle.do` already does), not an empirical refit spread. Recorded here so a future contributor does not rediscover this the hard way. |

Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 132+ files) after this unit: 17 ERGM test files, no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 87: cross-platform native builds (phase C)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Toolchain availability check | ✅ (checked, none available) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | No Windows cross-compiler (`mingw-w64`), Linux cross-compiler, Docker, or `zig` was available in this development environment - confirmed by direct probe, not assumed. This ruled out local cross-compilation and pointed at CI-based builds on GitHub's own hosted runners as the only real option, per the user's own explicit "automated GitHub builds" phrasing. |
| **`.github/workflows/build-plugins.yml`** - builds macOS/Windows/Linux plugins on GitHub-hosted runners and commits them back into `lib/plugins/` | ✅ | ⏳ (cannot run until this repo is pushed to GitHub - see the pending migration) | n/a until it runs successfully once | ✅ | Three parallel jobs (`macos-14`, `windows-latest`, `ubuntu-latest` runners) build the plugin via `native/Makefile`'s `macos`/`unix` targets and a direct `cl /LD` invocation for Windows; a fourth job downloads all three artifacts and commits them into `lib/plugins/` on push to the default branch. This is real, runnable CI configuration, not a placeholder - it will execute for the first time once the pending GitHub migration (see below) makes this repository live, at which point its own success/failure becomes part of this project's normal verification surface. |
| **Real cross-platform-coexistence bug found and fixed before it could ship**: a first draft of both `unw_ergm.do`'s `ErgmNativePluginPath()` and the CI workflow assumed macOS's and Windows's own plugin binaries could share the single path `lib/plugins/ergm_mcmc.plugin` (based on Stata's own documented "a Windows .dll may be renamed to .plugin" convention) | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | That convention is about avoiding `using()` for a SINGLE-platform build, not about multiple platforms' binaries coexisting in one shipped repository - two different binaries cannot occupy the same literal path in the same commit. Caught and fixed before any CI run could silently overwrite one platform's binary with another's: `ErgmNativePluginPath()` now resolves to a per-platform subdirectory (`lib/plugins/macos/`, `lib/plugins/windows/`, `lib/plugins/unix/`) via a new `ErgmNativePluginSubdir()` (reading `c(os)` via `st_global("c(os)")`, confirmed by direct trial to return `"MacOSX"/"Windows"/"Unix"`) - since every call site already passes an explicit `using()` path (never relying on Stata's own name-based auto-discovery), the on-disk filename convention no longer needs to double as a discovery mechanism, so subdirectories are a clean, low-risk fix. `lib/plugins/macos/ergm_mcmc.plugin` (the existing, already-verified macOS binary) was moved into the new layout; full ERGM regression sweep re-verified passing in both modes after the move. |
| `native/Makefile` updated for the new layout, plus a `unix` target (documented, not locally runnable without a Linux host/toolchain) | ✅ | n/a (macOS target re-verified; unix target cannot be exercised here) | n/a | ✅ | `make macos`/`make unix` now write to the correct per-platform subdirectory automatically (`mkdir -p` guards included). |

Windows and Linux binaries remain **not yet built** - this unit lays the real infrastructure (workflow, path resolution, directory layout) for CI to build them automatically once the repository exists on GitHub, rather than fabricating binaries or claiming false completion. `ErgmNativeAvailable()` continues to fall back to the fully-functional Mata backend on any platform whose binary is absent, so the package is correct and complete on every platform today - only acceleration is currently macOS-only, exactly as before this unit, with the path now clear (not just documented) to close that gap. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 17 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2** for the path-resolution/layout change; the CI workflow itself is certified only once it has actually run successfully post-migration.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 88: term-expansion wave 1 (phase D) - absdist, nodematch(diff), nodefactor, nodemix, gwdsp

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `absdist(varlist)` - sum over ties of `|x_i - x_j|` on a continuous covariate | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A natural companion to the existing `nodecov` (sum) for continuous covariates where DISSIMILARITY, not combined level, is the hypothesized mechanism. |
| `nodematchdiff(varlist)` - differential homophily (one coefficient per distinct attribute level, not pooled) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A separate option from `nodematch()` rather than a `diff` suboption on it - Stata option syntax does not cleanly support a per-variable-in-a-varlist modifier the way R's `nodematch(attr, diff=TRUE)` argument does, so users choose pooled (`nodematch()`) vs. differential (`nodematchdiff()`) homophily per variable by which option they list it under. `ErgmTermData` gained a `levels` field (distinct values present, populated once via Mata `uniqrows()` before `addterm()`, fixing the level-to-output-column mapping) and a small `_ergm_level_index()` linear-scan helper (fine since the number of distinct levels is always small - an O(nlevels) per-toggle cost, not O(n)). |
| `nodefactor(varlist)` - one coefficient per distinct level, each counting total degree among nodes at that level | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Toggling (i,j) touches exactly the (possibly equal) level-columns for `attr[i]` and `attr[j]` - an O(1) change statistic, same shape as `gwdegree`'s own change function but with an indicator match instead of the geometric kernel. |
| `nodemix(varlist)` - full categorical mixing matrix, one coefficient per distinct unordered level-pair | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `ErgmTermData` gained a `levelpairs` field (all unordered pairs of the distinct levels present, including within-level pairs, built once in `nwergm.ado`) and a matching `_ergm_levelpair_index()` helper. Undirected v1 scope, matching this project's own existing directed/undirected term-family conventions; a directed ordered-pair mixing matrix is a natural, disclosed future extension using the same lookup with an ordered rather than canonicalized key. |
| `gwdsp(real)` - geometrically weighted DYADWISE (not edgewise) shared partners | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Sums `gw_kernel(shared_partners(u,v), decay)` over EVERY dyad, tied or not - unlike GWESP, which sums only over tied dyads. `stat_gwdsp()` genuinely needs an O(n²) full-dyad enumeration from scratch (there is no O(nties) shortcut the way `all_ties()` gives GWESP, since shared partners are defined for untied dyads too) - paid once per `full_statistic()` call (MPLE design matrix, MCMLE's own Dbar centering), never inside the O(1)-target MCMC change-statistic path. The CHANGE statistic itself is structurally SIMPLER than GWESP's own (no "must also be a tie" restriction on the affected neighbor dyads, and no separate "own dyad" term, since shared_partners(i,j) does not depend on whether i-j is itself tied) despite the full statistic being more expensive to compute from scratch - both properties confirmed by direct derivation before implementation, not assumed by analogy to GWESP. Undirected v1 scope, dyad-DEPENDENT (added to the MPLE-eligibility exclusion list alongside `gwesp`/every other geometrically-weighted term). |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion1.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All five terms certified via the same brute-force `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` cross-check every existing term uses - all five passed with max discrepancy at or below floating-point noise (1e-14) on the first attempt, no debugging required, a direct benefit of deriving each change statistic's own correctness ON PAPER (documented in each function's own header comment) before writing the code. |
| End-to-end `.ado`-level wiring smoke test (all five new options parsed, matrices built, MPLE/MCMLE dispatch correctly) | ✅ | ✅ (ad hoc, not a permanent cscript - each term individually confirmed to run to completion without error under `method(mple)` where dyad-independent, or a small `method(mcmle)` budget for `gwdsp`) | n/a | ✅ | A combined 16-parameter single-model smoke test (all five terms plus edges at once on a 40-node network) was also attempted and found genuinely slow (still mid-fit after 3 minutes) - not a bug, but an expected consequence of this term-expansion wave not yet being covered by the native (C) backend (unit 83's own scope remains edges/mutual/nodematch/gwesp only) combined with a deliberately extreme number of simultaneous parameters for such a small network; the per-term smoke tests confirm correctness without needing that combined stress case to finish. |
| Native backend NOT extended to these five terms in this unit | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Per the user's own "decide term by term through profiling" instruction (unit 83) - none of these five terms has yet been shown to be a genuine performance bottleneck the way GWESP was, so extending `native/ergm_mcmc.c` for them now would be speculative optimization, not evidence-based. `nodefactor`/`nodemix`/`absdist`/`nodematchdiff` are all O(1) dyad-independent change statistics already (comparable in cost to `nodecov`, never separately benchmarked as slow); `gwdsp` shares GWESP's own nonlocal shape and would be the natural next candidate if it is ever shown to matter in practice. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table updated to record all five as done and to carry a fresh, prioritized survey of R ergm's remaining term library (excluding two-mode/bipartite terms, deprioritized per the user's own explicit instruction) for future waves. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 18 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 90: term-expansion wave 2 (phase D) - degree family, concurrent, triangle, ctriple, gwnsp, and a nodefactor fix

Fresh verification against R ergm's own current source (`R/InitErgmTerm.R`, `R/InitErgmTerm.dgw_sp.R` on GitHub - not memory or the earlier Part I study alone) directly informed every term below, per the user's own explicit instruction to check R's actual term list rather than assume it.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Fix: `nodefactor()` now omits the base (lowest-sorted) level by default** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fresh source-checking found R ergm's own `nodefactor(attr, base=1)` deliberately omits one reference level - summing ALL levels' own statistics equals `2*edges` for an undirected network, making a full-level parameterization exactly collinear with the already-present `edges` term (one coefficient unidentified). Unit 88's own original `nodefactor` shipped without this omission. `stat_nodefactor()`/`change_nodefactor()` themselves needed NO Mata change - both are already fully generic over whatever `td.levels` holds - this was a pure `nwergm.ado` construction-time fix (drop the first sorted level before building the term). |
| `degree(numlist)`/`odegree(numlist)`/`idegree(numlist)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | One coefficient per requested degree value (R's own `degree(d)` takes `d` as a VECTOR - multiple simultaneous statistics in one term instance, confirmed from source) - reuses the exact multi-parameter `td.levels` machinery unit 88 built for `nodematchdiff`/`nodefactor`. `degree()` is undirected (total degree); `odegree()`/`idegree()` are directed, touching only the relevant single endpoint's own out-/in-degree when a toggle occurs (not both endpoints symmetrically, unlike undirected `degree()`). Fresh source-checking also found R's own `isolates` term appears to have been REMOVED from current ergm in favor of `degree(0)` (absent from the current term-listing file) - no separate `isolates` term was added here since `degree(0)` already subsumes it. |
| `concurrent` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Count of nodes with total degree >= 2 - a single-statistic convenience special case of the same threshold-crossing reasoning `degree()` uses, matching R's own decision to ship it as a named term despite the conceptual overlap with a general `degrange(2,.)` (not itself implemented - `concurrent` is by far the most commonly used instance of that family). |
| `triangle` (undirected) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Uses the identity `triangle_count = (1/3) * sum over TIED dyads of shared_partners(i,j)` (each triangle contributes to exactly 3 edges' own shared-partner counts). The CHANGE statistic turned out dramatically simpler than GWESP's own: toggling (i,j) with p existing shared partners creates/destroys EXACTLY p triangles, with no neighbor-loop/kernel-reweighting term needed at all - unlike GWESP, an unweighted "count" kernel's own marginal contribution per triangle is always exactly 1 regardless of context, so there is nothing analogous to GWESP's own "other affected dyads" adjustment to compute. Derived on paper before coding, not discovered by trial and error. |
| `ctriple` (directed cyclic triples, `(i->j),(j->k),(k->i)`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Same "sum over arcs, divide by 3" structure as `triangle`, but the per-arc "local count" for arc (i,j) is the number of k such that `j->k` AND `k->i` both already hold (direction-sensitive, not a symmetric shared-partner count) - toggling (i,j) creates/destroys exactly that many cyclic triples. |
| `gwnsp(real)` (geometrically weighted nonedgewise/untied shared partners) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Confirmed directly against R ergm's own current source that `gwdsp = gwesp + gwnsp` exactly (every dyad is either tied - counted by gwesp - or untied - counted by gwnsp - partitioning gwdsp's own "every dyad" sum with no overlap or gap) - implemented as a thin composition of the two already-certified terms (`stat_gwnsp = stat_gwdsp - stat_gwesp`, likewise for the change statistic) rather than a third independent shared-partner traversal. A dedicated identity test (`test_gwnsp_identity`) checks `gwdsp == gwesp + gwnsp` directly on a real network, not just each term's own change-statistic correctness in isolation. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion2.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All seven items above certified via the same brute-force `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` cross-check every existing term uses - six of seven passed at exact floating-point equality (0.00e+00) on the first attempt; `gwnsp` at 7.99e-15 (floating-point noise, as expected for a term built from two others each already at that noise floor). |
| End-to-end `.ado`-level wiring smoke test | ✅ | ✅ (ad hoc, not a permanent cscript) | n/a | ✅ | Each new option (`degree()`, `odegree()`+`idegree()` together, `concurrent`, `triangle`, `gwnsp()`, `ctriple`) run to completion via a real `nwergm` call on random 60-node networks, both directed and undirected as appropriate, with no errors. |
| Native backend not extended to these terms | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Same "decide term by term through profiling" discipline as unit 88 - none of these seven terms has been shown to be a performance bottleneck. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table and prioritized R-ergm survey updated to record all seven as done. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 20 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 91: term-expansion wave 3 (phase D) - nodeofactor/nodeifactor, k/o/i-star family, degree-range family; and a real inline-Mata `if`-statement bug found by the new `.ado`-level wiring test

Continuation of unit 90's same fresh-source-checking discipline against R ergm's own current `R/InitErgmTerm.R`.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nodeofactor(varlist)`/`nodeifactor(varlist)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Directed analogues of `nodefactor()`: one coefficient per NON-BASE distinct level, counting out-/in-degree respectively among nodes at that level (same base-level-omission convention as unit 90's `nodefactor` fix, for the same collinearity-with-`edges` reason). Dyad-independent (change stat depends only on the toggled dyad's own endpoint attribute, not other dyads' state) - deliberately excluded from `nwergm.ado`'s `__ergm_dind` dyad-independence check, unlike the rest of this wave. |
| `kstar(numlist)`/`ostar(numlist)`/`istar(numlist)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | General k-star family (one coefficient per requested k), generalizing the existing single-k `istar2` demo term via a new `_ergm_choose(d,k)` general binomial coefficient helper. `kstar()` is undirected (total-degree stars); `ostar()`/`istar()` are directed. Sanity cross-check `test_kstar2_matches_istar2()`: `kstar(2)` on a network matches the independently-coded `istar2` demo term exactly (both count 2-stars, via different code paths) - 45.0000 == 45.0000. |
| `degrange(numlist)`/`degrangeto(numlist)`, `odegrange()`/`odegrangeto()`, `idegrange()`/`idegrangeto()` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Semi-open-interval degree counts `[from,to)`, using `td.levelpairs` (an Mx2 matrix, `to=.` meaning an open-ended upper bound, matching R's own `to=+Inf` default) via a new `_ergm_inrange(d,from,to)` helper. `degrange()` undirected; `odegrange()`/`idegrange()` directed. All six of these plus the star family are dyad-DEPENDENT (degree-based) and are included in `__ergm_dind`. |
| **Bug found and fixed: bare `mata: if (cond) stmt` one-liners are not reliably parseable when invoked from `nwergm.ado`** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The new `.ado`-level wiring smoke test (below) - the first end-to-end test to actually exercise `nodefactor()`'s/`nodeofactor()`'s/`nodeifactor()`'s own base-level-drop logic through `nwergm` itself, rather than only via `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()`'s direct Mata-level construction - immediately hit `"unexpected end of line"`/`"<istmt> incomplete"` (r(3000)) on `mata: if (rows(td.levels) > 1) td.levels = td.levels[2::rows(td.levels)]`. Isolated via a minimal repro down to the simplest possible case, `mata: if (1 > 0) x = x`, which fails identically - confirming this is a genuine Mata interactive-console limitation (a bare `if` fed as a single "mata:"-prefixed Stata command, outside a `mata\...\end` block, is treated as incomplete, apparently because Mata's one-line reader cannot rule out a following `else` the way a full function-body parse can) and not something specific to this term. Wrapping the `if` in `{ }` does not fix it either (`"invalid expression"`, still failing). This was a REAL, previously undetected bug affecting `nodefactor()` too (present since unit 90, latent because no `.ado`-level wiring test had ever exercised it - only Mata-level `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` calls, which construct `ErgmTermData` directly in Mata and never go through this code path). Fixed at all three call sites (`nodefactor`/`nodeofactor`/`nodeifactor` in `nwergm.ado`) by extracting the logic into a new, ordinary Mata function, `_ergm_drop_base_level(real colvector levels)` (`unw_ergm.do`) - safe because it contains no control flow at the Stata-inline call site, only inside its own function body (which compiles completely normally, like every other function in the file); the call site becomes a single assignment, `mata: X.levels = _ergm_drop_base_level(X.levels)`. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion3.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | All six certification functions pass at exact floating-point equality (0.00e+00), via the same brute-force `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` cross-check every existing term uses, plus the `kstar`/`istar2` sanity cross-check above. |
| New permanent `.ado`-level wiring test, appended to `cscripts/test_nwergm_ado.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Exercises all eight new options (plus `nodefactor()`, retroactively) through `nwergm`'s own option parsing on small directed/undirected networks, including the directed/undirected mismatch guards for each. This is the test that caught the `if`-one-liner bug above - a gap in wave 1/2's own certification coverage (neither wave had an `.ado`-level wiring test; both were certified at the Mata level only) closed as part of this unit. |
| Native backend not extended to these terms | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Same "decide term by term through profiling" discipline as units 88/90 - none of these eight terms has been shown to be a performance bottleneck. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table and prioritized R-ergm survey updated to record all eight as done. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 20 files): no regressions - one authoring mistake in the new wiring test itself (`colsof(e(b))` miscounted by one, forgetting to include `edges` in the total) was caught by the sweep and fixed before certifying. **Methodology note, disclosed for future reference**: batching multiple production-mode (`lib/cscripts_prod/`) cscripts together via nested `cap noi do "file.do"` calls inside one wrapper do-file is NOT equivalent to running each as its own top-level `-e do "file.do"` invocation - a `mata:`-block that declares a NEW function taking a library-only class (e.g. `class ErgmGraph`) as a parameter type fails with `"class ErgmGraph undefined"` when nested this way, even though the exact same file runs cleanly standalone (confirmed directly: 19 of 20 production ERGM tests "failed" under a nested-`do` batch wrapper, then all 20 passed when re-run as separate top-level processes). This is a wrinkle in Mata's own mlib class auto-loading depending on `do`-nesting depth, not a defect in any of this session's own code - recorded here so a future full-suite sweep doesn't mistake it for a regression. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 91 (continued): term-expansion wave 4 (phase D) - esp(d)/dsp(d), fixed non-geometric shared-partner-count terms

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `esp(numlist)`/`dsp(numlist)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fresh-checked against R ergm's own current `R/InitErgmTerm.dgw_sp.R`: `InitErgmTerm.desp()`/`InitErgmTerm.ddsp()` both delegate to a shared `.d_sp_impl()` taking `d` as a vector and a `type` argument selecting the shared-partner definition (`UTP` undirected, `OTP`/`ITP`/`RTP`/`OSP`/`ISP` directed, default `OTP`). `nwergm`'s own GWESP/GWDSP only implement the undirected UTP definition (no directed common-neighbor machinery exists yet - see the still-open "Directed GWESP variants" roadmap item), so `esp()`/`dsp()` are scoped identically (undirected/UTP only) rather than building new directed infrastructure as a side effect. Implemented as a kernel substitution on the already-certified GWESP/GWDSP change-statistic shape: `gw_kernel(p, decay)` (a scalar) replaced by the exact-match indicator rowvector `(p :== td.levels')` (one 0/1 entry per requested d) - every other line of reasoning (which dyads are affected by toggling (i,j), the "own dyad" term `esp` needs that `dsp` does not) carries over unchanged. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion4.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Both terms certified via the same brute-force `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` cross-check every existing term uses, PLUS two structural identity checks with no external reference needed: `sum over d of esp(d)` must equal the network's own tie count (every tied dyad has some shared-partner count), and `sum over d of dsp(d)` must equal the total dyad count `C(n,2)` (every dyad, tied or not, has some shared-partner count) - both confirmed exactly on real random networks. |
| **Caveat found and documented (not a code bug): an EXHAUSTIVE `dsp()`/`esp()` d-range is exactly collinear across its own columns** | n/a (disclosed limitation, not fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The new `.ado`-level wiring test's own first attempt, `dsp(0 1 2)` on a small 5-node network, failed with `_rmcoll`/`logit` returning "no observations" (r(2000)) inside the MPLE design-matrix step (`build_mple_data()`, run unconditionally as MCMLE's own starting-value source regardless of requested `method()`). Root-caused by directly inspecting the design matrix: because a single dyad toggle only ever MOVES shared-partner mass between count-bins (never creates or destroys it), the sum of `change_dsp()`'s own contributions across an EXHAUSTIVE set of requested d-values is identically zero for every row - an exact linear dependency among the `dsp()` columns, conceptually the same "sum of all levels is collinear with the intercept" trap `nodefactor()`'s own base-level omission (unit 90) exists to avoid. Unlike `nodefactor()`/`nodeofactor()`/`nodeifactor()`, `esp()`/`dsp()` do NOT auto-drop a value (matching R ergm's own convention - the user picks specific d values, and this collinearity is rare/coincidental at realistic network scale, only reliably reproducible on a small toy network the way this session's own wiring test happened to). Fixed the test itself (switched to a single, non-exhaustive `dsp(1)`) and disclosed the caveat directly in `nwergm.ado`'s/`nwergm.sthlp`'s own `dsp()` synopt line, plus `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`. |
| Native backend not extended to these terms | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Same "decide term by term through profiling" discipline as units 88/90/91 - neither term has been shown to be a performance bottleneck. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table updated to record `esp()`/`dsp()` as done - this completes every item in the roadmap's own "Medium priority" term-family tier except `transitiveties`/`cyclicalties` and the directed GWESP variants (OTP/ITP/OSP/ISP), both of which genuinely need new directed common-neighbor infrastructure rather than being a small addition on top of what already exists. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 21 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 91 (continued): term-expansion wave 5 (phase D) - directed shared partners (OTP) for gwesp/gwdsp/gwnsp/esp/dsp

Fresh-checked against R ergm's own current `R/InitErgmTerm.dgw_sp.R` (term-name dispatch: `esp`/`dsp`/`nsp`/`gwesp`/`gwdsp`/`gwnsp` are the SAME term names for both directed and undirected networks - R does not use separate `d`-prefixed names in current syntax, it just dispatches internally on a `type=` argument, default `"OTP"` for directed) and `src/changestats_spcache.c` (exact combinatorial definitions of each `type`).

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmGraph::shared_partners_otp(i,j)` - directed "outgoing two-path" shared-partner count | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `SP_OTP(i,j) = #{k : i->k and k->j}` - R's own default directed shared-partner definition. NOT symmetric in `(i,j)` (unlike the existing undirected `shared_partners()`), so it is a genuinely separate function, not a generalization of the existing one. No incremental cache (matches this session's own "decide term by term via profiling" standard - unit 82's UTP cache was added only after evidence). Certified via a DIRECT brute-force cross-check: an independent O(n) `has_edge()`-only scan (`brute_otp()` in the new test file), compared against `shared_partners_otp()` on every ordered pair of an 18-node random directed network - exact match, not just "close enough". |
| `gwesp()`/`gwdsp()`/`esp()`/`dsp()` extended to directed networks via OTP; `gwnsp()` needs no change | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New `ErgmTermData.sptype` field ("" = undirected/UTP default, unchanged behavior; `"OTP"` = directed) dispatched at the top of each of the four existing `stat_*`/`change_*` function pairs (`if (td.sptype=="OTP") return(..._otp(...))`, falling through to the completely untouched existing UTP code otherwise - zero risk to the already-certified undirected paths). New `_otp`-suffixed variants derived by hand from `shared_partners_otp()`'s own asymmetry: toggling arc i->j affects SP_OTP(a,j) for every in-neighbor `a` of `i` (i can serve as the connecting node in a->i->j), and SP_OTP(i,b) for every out-neighbor `b` of `j` (j can serve as the connecting node in i->j->b) - replacing the undirected functions' single symmetric neighbor loop with two directionally-distinct ones. GWDSP/DSP's own dyad-space genuinely doubles for directed OTP (all `n*(n-1)` ORDERED pairs, not `n*(n-1)/2` unordered ones, since `SP_OTP(i,j) != SP_OTP(j,i)` in general). `gwnsp()` needed ZERO code changes - it is already a thin composition (`stat_gwdsp(G,td) - stat_gwesp(G,td)`) that inherits the dispatch automatically once `td.sptype` is set, the same "composition, not a new traversal" payoff unit 90 got when first building it. `nwergm.ado` sets `td.sptype = "OTP"` automatically whenever the network is directed (matching R's own default - no new user-facing option needed to get this behavior), in both the main `nwergm` estimation command and the separate `nwergm simulate` command. All five removed "undirected only" `error 198` guards accordingly. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion5.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | 8 certification functions: the brute-force `shared_partners_otp()` cross-check above; `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` on `gwesp`/`gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp` each with `sptype="OTP"` on real random directed networks; and two structural identities with no external reference needed - `sum over d of esp_otp(d)` equals the network's own ARC count (every existing arc has some OTP shared-partner count), and `sum over d of dsp_otp(d)` equals `n*(n-1)` (every ORDERED pair, tied or not, has some OTP count) - both confirmed exactly. |
| Updated `.ado`-level wiring test in `cscripts/test_nwergm_ado.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The old guard-rejection test for `esp()`/`dsp()` on a directed network (from wave 4, since that combination used to error) was replaced with a positive test: `gwesp()`, `gwdsp()`+`gwnsp()`, and `esp()`+`dsp()` each run to completion on real directed networks through `nwergm` itself, not just via direct Mata construction. |
| ITP/OSP/ISP/RTP (R's other four `type=` options) | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Exact definitions now fresh-documented in `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md` (ITP: `k->i` and `j->k`; OSP: `i->k` and `j->k`; ISP: `k->i` and `k->j`; RTP: mutual `i<->k` and mutual `k<->j`) for when a future wave implements them - each needs its own change-statistic derivation (which third-party dyads are affected by toggling differs by type), not a simple parameter swap on the OTP version, so this was deliberately scoped down to OTP (R's own default, covering the most common case) rather than attempting all five in one pass. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table updated: OTP-based directed gwesp/gwdsp/gwnsp/esp/dsp marked DONE; ITP/OSP/ISP/RTP added as an explicit, well-scoped follow-on row (dispatch infrastructure already exists, only the per-type combinatorics remain). Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 22 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 91 (continued): term-expansion wave 6 (phase D) - transitiveties/cyclicalties

A fresh full-repository file listing (`R/InitErgmTerm*.R`) surfaced a dedicated `R/InitErgmTerm.transitiveties.R` file - these two terms are NOT in the main `InitErgmTerm.R` dispatcher list (an earlier fetch of that file alone incorrectly suggested they might have been removed from current ergm entirely, mirroring `isolates`' actual fate in unit 90 - checking the repository's own file listing directly, rather than trusting one file's contents, caught this before it led to a wrong conclusion).

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `transitiveties`/`cyclicalties` (directed only) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | R's own stated definitions: "the number of ties i->j such that there exists a two-path from i to j" (`transitiveties`) / "...from j to i" (`cyclicalties`). Both are threshold/EXISTENCE indicators (not counts) built directly on wave 5's `shared_partners_otp()`: `transitiveties = sum over existing arcs (i,j) of I(SP_OTP(i,j)>=1)`; `cyclicalties = sum over existing arcs (i,j) of I(SP_OTP(j,i)>=1)` (the reversed argument order closes a directed 3-cycle together with the existing arc i->j, hence the name). Change statistic reuses the exact same two affected-shared-partner-count families wave 5 derived (SP_OTP(a,j) for in-neighbors `a` of `i`; SP_OTP(i,b) for out-neighbors `b` of `j`), but now contributes only on a THRESHOLD CROSSING (0->1 or 1->0) and only when the AFFECTED ARC ITSELF exists (both terms sum over existing arcs only) - and, since each term reads a different orientation of `SP_OTP` as its own per-arc statistic, the two terms attribute the SAME underlying shared-partner-count changes to DIFFERENT arcs (`transitiveties` to arc `a->j`/`i->b` directly; `cyclicalties` to the REVERSED arc `j->a`/`b->i`) - a subtlety worked out by hand before coding, not discovered by trial and error. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion6.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Four certification functions: a FULLY INDEPENDENT brute-force statistic recomputation (scans every arc and every candidate k directly via `has_edge()`, zero dependence on `shared_partners_otp()` itself - catches any error the two might otherwise share); `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` on each term individually; and a THIRD combined-model check with both terms in the same model together (confirms no shared mutable state/cross-contamination between their independent `ErgmTermData` instances) - all four at exact floating-point equality (0.00e+00). |
| Permanent `.ado`-level wiring test, appended to `cscripts/test_nwergm_ado.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Both options run to completion together through `nwergm` itself on a real directed network, plus the undirected-network guard-rejection check for both. |
| Native backend not extended to these terms | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Same "decide term by term through profiling" discipline as every prior wave - neither term has been shown to be a performance bottleneck. |

This completes every item in `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own "Medium priority" term-family tier (the entire tier is now DONE except the explicitly-scoped-out ITP/OSP/ISP/RTP directed-variant follow-on). `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md` updated accordingly. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 23 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 91 (continued): term-expansion wave 7 (phase D) - hamming(), sender(), receiver()

Closes out two more items from `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own "Low priority" term-family tier (deliberately still worth doing per the user's own instruction to "go through the list" of R's terms, not merely the highest-priority ones - both turned out cheaper than their own roadmap effort estimate once actually derived).

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sender`/`receiver` (per-node out-/in-degree fixed effects, directed only) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fresh-checked against R ergm's own current `InitErgmTerm.sender()`/`InitErgmTerm.receiver()`: each adds ONE COEFFICIENT PER NODE equal to that node's own out-/in-degree, with the base (first) node omitted by default (same collinearity-with-`edges` reasoning as `nodefactor()`'s own base-level omission, unit 90). This is mathematically IDENTICAL to `nodeofactor()`/`nodeifactor()` with the node's own identity (1..n) as the "attribute" and every node its own distinct level - so `sender`/`receiver` are implemented as a pure `nwergm.ado`-level convenience (`td.attr = (1::nodes)`, `td.levels = (2::nodes)` to drop the base), calling the exact SAME already-certified `stat_nodeofactor()`/`change_nodeofactor()` (for `sender`) and `stat_nodeifactor()`/`change_nodeifactor()` (for `receiver`) with ZERO new Mata statistic/change code - a bigger effort-vs-value win than the roadmap's own original "Low effort" estimate anticipated, since even that estimate assumed new code would be needed. |
| `hamming(netname)` (Hamming distance to a reference network) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fresh-checked against R ergm's own current `InitErgmTerm.hamming()`: the unweighted base case (no `cov=`) is exactly a disagreement count between the network being estimated and a reference network. Genuinely dyad-independent (confirmed by derivation, not assumed): toggling (i,j) always flips its own state, so if the dyad currently AGREES with the reference the toggle necessarily creates a mismatch (+1), and if it currently DISAGREES the toggle necessarily fixes it (-1) - an O(1) change statistic with no third-party/neighbor effects at all, simpler than the roadmap's own "Medium effort" estimate anticipated.
