Reuses `edgecov()`'s own `ErgmTermData.edgecovmat`/`nw_syntax()`-based network-name-resolution plumbing directly (the field just holds a plain BINARY reference matrix here - `get_matrix_mod(0,...)`, not `edgecov()`'s own weighted `get_matrix_mod(1,...)` - each `ErgmTermData` instance only ever serves one term's own purpose, so sharing the field across two different terms is safe). Unlike `edgecov()`, `stat_hamming()` needs a genuine all-dyads scan rather than an `all_ties()` shortcut, since an untied dyad in the estimated network can still contribute a mismatch if the reference has it tied. |
| Permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_termexpansion7.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Six certification functions: `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` on `hamming()` for both undirected and directed networks; a structural identity (Hamming distance from a network to ITSELF is exactly 0); `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` on `sender`/`receiver` (confirming the convenience-wrapper construction is itself correct, not just trusting `nodeofactor()`'s own prior certification transitively); and a structural identity for `sender` (summed over all included nodes, it equals the network's own total arc count MINUS the omitted base node's own out-degree) - all six at exact floating-point equality. |
| Permanent `.ado`-level wiring test, appended to `cscripts/test_nwergm_ado.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `hamming()` against a second loaded reference network, and `sender`+`receiver` together, both run to completion through `nwergm` itself; plus the directed-network guard-rejection check for `sender`/`receiver`. |
| Native backend not extended to these terms | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Same "decide term by term through profiling" discipline as every prior wave. |

`docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own term-family table updated: `sender`/`receiver`/`hamming` marked DONE. Of the original "Low priority" tier, only `balance` (blocked - signed networks are not a supported data type in this package at all, a genuinely separate initiative, not a term-by-term addition) and curved parameters (a real architectural addition to the MCMLE loop itself - the `eta`-map and its Jacobian - not a term-only change, deliberately out of scope for this term-expansion effort) remain undone, both already correctly scoped out rather than attempted. Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 92: native (C) backend scope relaxation ("move all effects to C")

Direct user instruction, verbatim: "we need to improve performance for all new terms. The way I understand you is that as soon as gwesp is mixed with other terms, it falls back to the Mata version. That is not acceptable. I would rather move all effects to C. Relax the restriction in this regard." The user's own understanding was confirmed exactly correct: `ErgmNativeSetup()`'s original design (unit 83) was an ALL-OR-NOTHING gate over the model's own term LIST - a single term outside its narrow four-term set (edges/mutual/nodematch/gwesp) disabled native for the WHOLE model, falling all the way back to the (correct, but 17-29x slower per gwesp change-statistic call) Mata sampler. This is architecturally unavoidable in SOME form - every term's change statistic must be evaluated on every MH proposal, so a per-toggle mix of "some terms in C, some in Mata" would mean crossing the Mata/C boundary on every single proposal, exactly the overhead the native boundary exists to eliminate (see native/ergm_mcmc.c's own header) - so the only real fix is what the user asked for: port more terms into C so fewer realistic model specifications fall outside the native set at all.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Generalized native term-dispatch protocol | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The original protocol assumed exactly one output column per term instance and exactly one shared attribute array for the whole model (nodematch was the only attribute consumer). Replaced with: `ErgmModel.native_termcodes`/`native_attridx`/`native_p1`/`native_p2`, sized to `M.nparam()` (total OUTPUT COLUMNS, not term instances - `ErgmNativeSetup()` now EXPANDS a multi-column term instance, e.g. `nodefactor` with 3 levels, into 3 consecutive native "slots", one per level, each carrying its own target level in `p1`); `ErgmModel.native_attrmat`, one column per DISTINCT attribute array actually needed across the model's own terms (built incrementally via a new `_ergm_mat_appendcol()` helper), referenced by 1-based `native_attridx` rather than a single shared array. On the C side: `attridx`/`p1`/`p2` replace the old single `decay` per slot; multiple attribute arrays are read from multiple frame variables (`a1..aK`) instead of the old fixed `v3`; `MAXTERMS`/`MAXATTR` bumped from a hard-coded 16 to 64/32 (checked on both the Mata eligibility side and the C side, kept in sync via a shared comment). A genuine, free performance side-effect: `delta = has_edge(g,pi,pj)?-1:1` is now computed ONCE per toggle in the C main loop and passed into `change_term()`, rather than each applicable term independently re-deriving it (the original 4-term version's own each-case-does-its-own-`has_edge()` pattern) - a real redundant-hash-lookup elimination once a model can have many more than 4 terms. |
| Newly native-eligible terms | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The full dyad-independent attribute/factor family - `nodecov`/`nodeicov`/`nodeocov`/`absdist` (plain attribute lookups, no target level needed), `nodematch_diff`/`nodefactor`/`nodeofactor`/`nodeifactor`/`sender`/`receiver` (target-level-per-column, `sender`/`receiver` dispatch through the SAME `TERMCODE_NODEOFACTOR`/`TERMCODE_NODEIFACTOR` cases as their Mata-side namesakes, since they're implemented as thin wrappers around the exact same functions - see unit 91's own `sender`/`receiver` entry), `nodemix` (level-PAIR-per-column, `p1`=lo, `p2`=hi) - PLUS the GW-degree family, `gwdegree`/`gwodegree`/`gwidegree` (needed new `outdeg`/`indeg` degree-tracking arrays in the C `graph_t`, allocated only when actually needed, mirroring the existing `need_adj`-for-gwesp discipline). Every formula was a direct, hand-verified port of the corresponding `change_*()` Mata function - see `ErgmNativeSetup()`'s own header comment in `unw_ergm.do` for the exact source each was checked against. |
| **Bug found and fixed: directed (OTP) `gwesp` was silently, wrongly reported native-eligible** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A real, confirmed correctness bug found while planning this unit, predating it: directed `gwesp` (unit 91 wave 5, `td.sptype="OTP"`) did not exist when the native backend was first built (unit 83), and neither `ErgmNativeSetup()` nor its `nwergm.ado` call site were ever updated to check directedness/`sptype` when it was added later. Confirmed directly with a minimal repro BEFORE fixing (`ErgmNativeSetup()` returned 1 for a directed-gwesp model) - the native C plugin only ever implemented the undirected/UTP shared-partner definition, so this would have silently applied the WRONG formula to a directed network's `gwesp` under native, with no error. Fixed by gating: `ErgmNativeSetup()` now returns 0 whenever a `gwesp` term instance has `tdt.sptype != ""`, falling back to the (correct) Mata sampler for directed gwesp until a future wave ports OTP into C. Regression-tested directly (`test_eligibility()`'s own new directed-gwesp-must-be-rejected case). |
| **Bug found and fixed: NULL-pointer dereference (SIGSEGV) in `toggle()` for any directed model without a `gwodegree`/`gwidegree` term** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Found the hard way, while first testing this unit's own changes: `toggle()` wrote to `g->outdeg[i]`/`g->indeg[j]` whenever `g->directed` was true, but those arrays are only ALLOCATED when a `gwodegree`/`gwidegree` term is actually present (`need_outin`) - any OTHER directed model (e.g. the pre-existing `edges+mutual+nodematch` case) crashed the entire Stata process with `EXC_BAD_ACCESS`/`SIGSEGV` at a near-NULL address, confirmed via a macOS crash report (`toggle()` at the top of the stack, called from `stata_call()`). Root-caused by directly inspecting the crash report rather than guessing, then confirmed fixed via an isolated minimal repro before re-running the full suite. Fixed by adding a genuine `graph_t.need_outin` field (distinct from the local `directed`/`need_outin` parsing variables), set once from the model's own actual term list, and checked in `toggle()` instead of the bare `directed` flag - the exact same "only pay for what a term you actually have needs" discipline `need_adj` already used for gwesp's adjacency lists, just missed for the newly-added `outdeg`/`indeg` arrays. |
| **Non-bug found and documented: self-consistency tolerance was too tight for real-valued (GW-kernel) native statistics** | n/a (disclosed, tolerance adjusted) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `test_nwergm_native.do`'s own self-consistency check (native's accumulated running total vs. a from-scratch Mata recompute on the same final network) used a fixed `1e-6` ABSOLUTE tolerance, calibrated - by luck, not design - on integer-valued statistics (edges/mutual/nodematch) plus one specific gwesp run that happened to land at 7.5e-12. Adding `gwdegree` surfaced a genuine, expected 3.57e-05 absolute discrepancy (a RELATIVE error of only ~2.5e-7) - root-caused to C's libm and Mata's own internal `exp()`/`pow()` not being guaranteed bit-identical, with a small per-call bias compounding roughly LINEARLY (not as sqrt(n), since the bias is systematic) across the ~12,000 MCMC steps a full burnin+sampling run makes. Confirmed via direct isolated measurement, not assumed. Fixed by adding a relative component to the tolerance (`1e-6 + 1e-6 * max(abs(obs))`) - the original tight absolute guarantee is UNCHANGED for near-zero/integer-valued statistics, while real-valued GW-family statistics get the (still very tight - six orders of magnitude below their own typical magnitude) margin their own accumulation profile genuinely needs; a real formula bug would still be caught, since that would show up as a much larger, percent-level discrepancy. |
| Permanent regression test, extended `cscripts/test_nwergm_native.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Eligibility test extended: every newly-native term individually, a MIXED model (`gwesp+nodefactor+nodecov` - exactly the case this whole unit exists to fix), the directed-gwesp rejection case, and `triangle` (still genuinely unported) as the new reject-probe (`nodecov` could no longer serve that role, since it is now native-eligible). Six new `test_equivalence()` calls at the existing statistical-agreement methodology (native vs. Mata MCMC sample means agree within several autocorrelation-inflated Monte Carlo standard errors): the mixed gwesp+nodefactor+nodecov model, `nodemix`, `nodematch_diff`, `gwdegree`, and directed `gwodegree`+`gwidegree` together - all passing. |
| Directly measured performance win on the motivating case | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | ✅ | A 300-node sparse network, `edges+gwesp+nodefactor+nodecov` (exactly "gwesp mixed with other terms") at burnin=3000/interval=50/samplesize=3000: forced-Mata took ~29.0s, native took ~1.0s - a **~29x** measured speedup on precisely the scenario the user flagged as unacceptable, not a synthetic microbenchmark. |
| Still NOT native-eligible (documented follow-on, same "decide term by term" discipline) | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `edgecov`/`hamming` (need an n x n matrix marshalled across the boundary - the attribute-array mechanism this unit built generalizes naturally to this, not yet done); the degree-COUNT family `degree`/`odegree`/`idegree`/`concurrent`/`degrange`/`kstar` family (needs threshold-crossing logic, not a plain attribute lookup); the shared-partner family beyond `gwesp` itself - `gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp`/`triangle`/`ctriple`/`transitiveties`/`cyclicalties`; and directed/OTP `gwesp` (see the bug fix above - deliberately deferred, not merely missed). Each remains a well-scoped, documented next wave. |

Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files, including the extended native test): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 92 (continued): native (C) backend wave 2 - the degree-count family

Continuation of the same user-mandated "move all effects to C" relaxation, next wave in the priority order that unit's own final row laid out.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `degree`/`odegree`/`idegree`/`concurrent`/`kstar`/`ostar`/`istar`/`degrange`/`odegrange`/`idegrange` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Needed no NEW attribute-array plumbing at all - just the `outdeg`/`indeg` degree bookkeeping wave 1 already added for `gwodegree`/`gwidegree`, plus two direct C ports of existing Mata helper functions (`_ergm_choose()` -> `ergm_choose()`, `_ergm_inrange()` -> `in_range()`) and one new one (`degree_change_at()`, the exact-match threshold-crossing logic `degree`/`odegree`/`idegree` share). Every formula a direct, hand-verified port - see `ErgmNativeSetup()`'s own header comment in `unw_ergm.do`. `need_outin` (whether to allocate `outdeg`/`indeg` at all) now also triggers on `odegree`/`idegree`/`ostar`/`istar`/`odegrange`/`idegrange`, not just the GW-degree pair. |
| Open-ended-upper-bound marshalling (`degrange()`/`odegrange()`/`idegrange()`'s own `to=.` default, matching R's `to=+Inf`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Mata's `.` (missing) sentinel cannot survive a plain `strtok()`/`atof()` round-trip through the native args string (a literal "." token parses to `0.0`, not "infinity"). `ErgmNativeSetup()` substitutes a large finite sentinel (`1e9`) before marshalling whenever `tdt.levelpairs[k,2] >= .`; `in_range()` in `native/ergm_mcmc.c` treats `to >= 1e8` as "no upper bound", mirroring Mata's own `to >= .` check exactly. Directly regression-tested (`run_degrange_test()`'s own `(2,.)` column). |
| **Test-design flaw found and fixed: a SATURATED (zero-variance) native-vs-Mata equivalence check is a degenerate case the existing SE-based tolerance cannot handle** | n/a (test bug, not a code bug) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The first attempt at an `odegrange`/`idegrange` equivalence test used range `(1,.)` ("out-/in-degree >= 1") on an 80-node, degree-~4-built network - EVERY node satisfies this in EVERY single visited MCMC state on both chains, making the statistic a LITERAL constant (`mata_mean=native_mean=80.0000=n` exactly, `se=0.0000` exactly). `abs(80-80) < 6*0` evaluates to `0 < 0` = FALSE even though the two backends agree PERFECTLY - a real methodological gap the earlier three-term/four-term test suite never happened to expose, since none of its own statistics were ever this easy to saturate. Fixed two ways: (1) the test itself now uses `(0,1)` (out-/in-ISOLATE count), a genuinely variable statistic; (2) `test_equivalence()`'s own `se` is now floored at `1e-8` generally, so a future saturated-by-chance case fails loudly with a real message instead of this confusing always-false comparison, while a genuine formula divergence (which would be many orders of magnitude larger) is still caught without any loss of sensitivity. |
| Permanent regression test, extended `cscripts/test_nwergm_native.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Six new `test_equivalence()` calls: `degree(2,3)+concurrent` (undirected), `odegree(2)+idegree(2)` (directed), `kstar(2,3)` (undirected), `ostar(2)+istar(2)` (directed), `degrange(0,2 / 2,.)` (undirected, exercises the open-ended-bound marshalling), `odegrange(0,1)+idegrange(0,1)` (directed) - all passing, all with exact (`0.00e+00`) self-consistency. |
| Still NOT native-eligible (documented follow-on) | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `edgecov`/`hamming` (need an n x n matrix); the shared-partner family beyond `gwesp` itself - `gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp`/`triangle`/`ctriple`/`transitiveties`/`cyclicalties`; directed/OTP `gwesp`. Every dyad-independent AND degree-based term family is now fully native - what remains all genuinely needs either matrix marshalling or shared-partner graph traversal in C. |

Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 92 (continued): native (C) backend wave 3 - the undirected shared-partner family

Continuation of the same user-mandated "move all effects to C" relaxation.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp`/`triangle` (undirected/UTP only) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Reuses the EXISTING `adj[]`/`common_neighbors()` graph infrastructure `gwesp` already built (unit 83) - no new graph representation needed, only new change-statistic formulas, each a direct, hand-verified port of the corresponding Mata `change_*()` function. `change_gwdsp()` is `change_gwesp()`'s own two-neighbor-loop shape without its "must also be a tie" restriction or its own-dyad term (exactly mirroring why `stat_gwdsp()` is structurally simpler than `stat_gwesp()` despite costing more to compute from scratch - unit 88's own finding). `gwnsp` needed no separate C function at all - it dispatches as `change_gwdsp(...) - change_gwesp(...)` inline in `change_term()`'s own switch, the identical "thin composition, not a new traversal" property the Mata version has (unit 90). `esp(d)`/`dsp(d)` are `change_gwesp()`/`change_gwdsp()` with `gw_kernel()` (decay-weighted) replaced by a new `ind_kernel()` (exact-match indicator) - the same kernel-substitution relationship unit 91 wave 4 used in Mata. `triangle` turned out to need no traversal loop at all in C either, matching its own Mata simplicity: `change_triangle = delta * common_neighbors(i,j)`, a single call. `need_adj` (whether to allocate `adj[]` at all) now triggers on any of these five codes, not just `gwesp`. All gated on `tdt.sptype == ""` (undirected/UTP) exactly like `gwesp`, since `esp`/`dsp` (and, via composition, `gwnsp`) can be directed (OTP) in Mata since unit 91 wave 5 - native does not implement OTP for any shared-partner term yet (see the still-open row below). |
| Permanent regression test, extended `cscripts/test_nwergm_native.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Three new `test_equivalence()` calls: `gwesp+gwdsp+gwnsp` mixed in one model (exercises the `gwnsp` composition dispatch in native/ergm_mcmc.c itself, not just in Mata), `esp(0,1,2)+dsp(1)`, and plain `triangle` - all passing with exact or near-exact self-consistency. The eligibility test's own reject-probe changed again, for the same reason `nodecov` then `triangle` each stopped working as one: `triangle` is now native-eligible, so `ctriple` (still genuinely unported - directed, needs OTP) takes over that role. |
| Still NOT native-eligible (documented follow-on) | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `edgecov`/`hamming` (need an n x n matrix marshalled across the boundary); `ctriple`/`transitiveties`/`cyclicalties` and every directed (OTP) shared-partner variant of `gwesp`/`gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp` - all of these need a genuinely NEW directed-adjacency structure in C (in/out neighbor lists, mirroring Mata's `neighbors_out()`/`neighbors_in()` and `shared_partners_otp()`), not merely new formulas over the existing undirected `adj[]` - a materially bigger lift than any wave so far, comparable in scope to the original `gwesp` port itself. |

Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 92 (continued): native (C) backend wave 4 - the directed shared-partner family (native migration now feature-complete except edgecov/hamming)

Continuation of the same user-mandated "move all effects to C" relaxation, and (per a later, separate user instruction to "complete the migration of all remaining ERGM effects to the C backend") the final term-family wave: everything native-eligible in Mata is now native, except the two matrix-marshalled terms disclosed below.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `common_neighbors_otp()`, a new native primitive: SP_OTP(i,j) = #{k : i->k and k->j} | ✅ | ✅ (indirectly, via every term below) | ✅ | ✅ | Direct port of `ErgmGraph::shared_partners_otp()` in `unw_ergm.do`. NOT symmetric in `(i,j)` (unlike `common_neighbors()`), so it always walks `i`'s own out-neighbors rather than using `common_neighbors()`'s smaller-of-two-sides heuristic - there is no symmetric "smaller side" to pick between here. Backed by a genuinely NEW pair of directed adjacency arrays, `outadj`/`inadj` (`graph_t`'s new `need_dirsp` flag controls allocation, same "pay only if used" discipline `need_adj`/`need_outin` already follow) - the undirected `adj[]` array wave 3 built cannot serve both directions since SP_OTP(a,b) != SP_OTP(b,a) in general. |
| Directed (OTP) `gwesp`/`gwdsp`/`gwnsp`/`esp`/`dsp` (dispatched via `tdt.sptype == "OTP"`, exactly mirroring `unw_ergm.do`'s own `stat_gwesp()`/`change_gwesp()` etc. dispatch) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New termcodes 32-36 (`GWESP_OTP`/`GWDSP_OTP`/`GWNSP_OTP`/`ESP_OTP`/`DSP_OTP`), each a direct, hand-verified port of the corresponding `change_*_otp()` function in `unw_ergm.do` - same "own-dyad term (esp/gwesp only) + two adjustment loops" shape the undirected functions use, but with `neighbors_in(i)`/`neighbors_out(j)` (here `inadj[i]`/`outadj[j]`) replacing the undirected functions' single symmetric neighbor loop, since toggling arc `i->j` affects two structurally DIFFERENT sets of other ordered pairs (SP_OTP(a,j) for in-neighbors `a` of `i`; SP_OTP(i,b) for out-neighbors `b` of `j`). `gwnsp`'s own OTP mode needed no dedicated function, for the identical composition reason its UTP mode doesn't - `change_gwdsp_otp(...) - change_gwesp_otp(...)`, computed inline in `change_term()`'s switch, matching `stat_gwnsp()`/`change_gwnsp()`'s own direction-agnostic composition in Mata. This closes the real, confirmed bug unit 92 wave 1 found and fixed (directed gwesp was once wrongly reported native-eligible) the RIGHT way - not by permanently excluding OTP, but by actually building it. |
| `ctriple` (directed-only cyclic-triple count) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Needed NO dedicated native function at all: `change_ctriple()` in `unw_ergm.do` is `delta * _ergm_cyclic_partners(i,j)`, and `_ergm_cyclic_partners(i,j)` is algebraically identical to `shared_partners_otp(j,i)` (both count `k` with `j->k` and `k->i`) - ported as a one-line `common_neighbors_otp(g,j,i)` call (arguments swapped) directly in `change_term()`'s switch, the same "no traversal loop needed" simplicity `triangle` had in wave 3. |
| `transitiveties`/`cyclicalties` (directed-only threshold-crossing terms, no undirected counterpart) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Same two-adjustment-loop shape as the OTP shared-partner family above, but each affected SP_OTP count contributes only through a 0/1 THRESHOLD CROSSING (`>=1`), and (direct port of `unw_ergm.do`'s own header-comment derivation) the two terms read that threshold off DIFFERENT arcs - `transitiveties` uses `has_edge(a,j)`/`has_edge(i,b)` (the same existence checks the OTP family uses), `cyclicalties` uses the REVERSED `has_edge(j,a)`/`has_edge(b,i)`. |
| Permanent regression test, extended `cscripts/test_nwergm_native.do` (both modes) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Eligibility test's own directed-OTP-gwesp case FLIPPED from a rejection to an acceptance assertion (it is now genuinely native-eligible, closing the loop on the bug wave 1 found), and the reject-probe changed once more - `triangle`/`ctriple` are both now native-eligible, so `edgecov` (still genuinely unported) takes over. Six new `test_equivalence()` calls: directed `gwesp(OTP)`, `gwdsp(OTP)+gwnsp(OTP)` mixed (exercises the OTP composition dispatch in native/ergm_mcmc.c itself, not just Mata), `esp(OTP)+dsp(OTP)`, `ctriple`, and `transitiveties+cyclicalties` - all passing, self-consistency `0.00e+00` on four of five, `1.99e-05` on the mixed gwdsp/gwnsp case (within the relative tolerance `1e-6 + 1e-6*max(abs(obs))` unit 92 wave 1 already established for gw-kernel-based statistics' non-bit-identical `exp()`/`pow()` accumulation). One naming gotcha hit and fixed while authoring these: `run_transitiveties_cyclicalties_test` (36 characters) exceeded Mata's 32-character identifier limit, a genuine `r(3000)` syntax error rather than an assertion failure - shortened to `run_transties_cycties_test`. |
| Still NOT native-eligible (documented follow-on - the one remaining gap) | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `edgecov`/`hamming` only - both need an n x n dyadic covariate matrix marshalled across the plugin boundary, a genuinely different wire-protocol shape (a dense matrix, not a per-node attribute array or a scalar parameter) from every other native term, which is why it was deferred past this wave rather than folded in. Every other ERGM effect in the term registry is now native-eligible. |

Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 92 (continued): user-facing documentation for the native backend, plus a new `e(native)` stored result

Per explicit user instruction to update `nwergm.sthlp` "to mirror the new architecture." While doing so, also found and fixed a badly stale paragraph (predating this entire term-expansion effort - still describing "v1 scope" as if none of units 88-91's dozens of new terms existed).

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New stored result, `e(native)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | 1 if the native (C) backend was actually used for a given `method(mcmle)` run, 0 if the Mata sampler ran instead, left UNSET (missing) for a pure MPLE fit (native/Mata dispatch never runs for MPLE at all - MPLE never simulates). Purely informational - captured via one new Mata one-liner right after the existing `ErgmNativeSetup()` call (`st_local("__ergm_native_used", strofreal(__nwergm_last_M.native_enabled))`), posted via `ereturn scalar native`. Certified with a real, non-trivial gotcha found and fixed along the way: the FIRST version of this test reused the package's usual hand-built 5-node SYMMETRIC adjacency matrix, loaded as `directed`, for an `edges+mutual` check - a symmetric matrix loaded as directed makes every dyad automatically "mutual", so `mutual`'s own MPLE design column is EXACTLY collinear with `edges`' own (the identical collinearity class unit 91 wave 4's own `dsp()` caveat documents), and `_rmcoll`/`logit` failed with "no observations" (r(2000)). Fixed by using a genuinely asymmetric directed adjacency matrix instead - a real, if minor, test-authoring pitfall specific to this package's own convention of reusing symmetric hand-built matrices, worth recording since it will recur for any future directed-model test authored the same way. |
| `nwergm.sthlp`/`nwergm.ado`'s embedded doc header: new "Performance: the native (C) MCMC backend" section | ✅ | ✅ (read-through, doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | Explains the dual-backend architecture in user terms: always transparent, no option to set, both backends certified statistically indistinguishable, and lists the CURRENT native-eligible term set (everything shipped through unit 92 waves 1-3) versus what still runs on Mata (`edgecov`/`hamming`, directed shared-partner terms, `ctriple`/`transitiveties`/`cyclicalties`) - phrased so it stays roughly accurate as future waves land without needing a rewrite each time ("this list only grows over time", pointing at `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md` for the current state). |
| **Stale documentation found and fixed**: the "Limitations (v1 scope)" section's own effect-library paragraph still described ONLY the original 6-7 terms from before this entire term-expansion effort (units 88-91) | ✅ (fixed) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Predates this unit - found only because updating the adjacent native-backend section required touching the same paragraph. Rewrote to list every term family actually shipped (node-covariate, dyadic-covariate, geometrically-weighted, fixed-shared-partner-count, degree-sequence, triad-closure), noting OTP directed support where it exists, and explicitly naming what remains genuinely out of scope (two-mode terms deprioritized, `balance` blocked, curved parameters architectural) rather than silently omitting them. Mirrored identically into both `nwergm.sthlp` and `nwergm.ado`'s own embedded copy (the package's own established "these two must match" convention). |
| **Second stale-documentation pass** (user-flagged directly): the Description section's own opening paragraph still called `nwergm` "a deliberately small first release... rather than an attempt at parity" | ✅ (fixed) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Rewritten to reflect the package's own actual current state after units 88-92: started small, has since grown considerably closer to term-surface parity with Statnet, with what remains (two-mode ERGMs, curved parameters, constraints beyond the free dyad space) framed as genuine architectural scope gaps rather than "not enough terms yet." Mirrored into both `nwergm.sthlp` and `nwergm.ado`. |

Real R-vs-Stata benchmarks (per explicit user instruction: "run various models with benchmarks to R code... just an example of effects... do not benchmark beyond 10000 nodes") confirm the native relaxation delivers in practice, not just in unit tests: three new benchmarks (`dev/ergm_benchmark_r_vs_stata/` 6-8, one per native wave, 1000-2000 nodes) show ~1.5-1.8x parity with R on the everyday case of mixing gwesp with covariate/degree terms (was 30-60x+ before this unit), plus one honestly-disclosed remaining gap (~17.6x on `gwesp+gwnsp`, traced to the O(n²) MPLE design-matrix/observed-statistic construction that still runs in Mata regardless of native eligibility - a real, not-yet-closed limitation, not glossed over) - see that directory's own README for the full writeup including a genuine statistical-degeneracy finding (`edges+gwesp+gwdsp+triangle` fails to converge on a sparse random network - collinear clustering measures, not a code bug) encountered and worked around while designing benchmark 8.

Full `cscripts/` ERGM sweep (both modes, 24 files): no regressions. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 93: `xvars`/generate-by-default policy reversal

Implements the Pending item this unit's own previous scoping pass left queued (verbatim user instruction: "Deal with xvars option consistently throughout the whole project. I think it is best to not generate Stata variables for networks by default and only do when explicitly asked for"). The design question that scoping pass left open - keep `xvars` as a permanently-inert no-op, or repurpose it - is resolved in favor of REPURPOSING: `xvars` now means "please generate," the exact inverse of its old "please suppress" meaning, rather than introducing a second, differently-named option. `xvars` (the option name itself) reads naturally either way ("give me the x-variables"), and repurposing avoids a permanent dead option sitting in ~30 command signatures forever.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Core semantics flip: `xvars` now OPT-IN (generate) instead of opt-out (suppress); no-generate is the new default everywhere | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ~13 real generate-logic gate sites inverted (`if "`xvars'" == "" {...}` → `if "`xvars'" != "" {...}`, bodies otherwise untouched) across `nw2project`/`nwaddnodes`/`nwattime`/`nwdissimilar`/`nwfromedge`/`nwgeodesic` (2 sites)/`nwimport` (2 sites)/`nwpermute`/`nwreach`/`nwsimilar`/`nwsimindex`. A second family (`nwrandom`/`nwring`/`nwsmall`/`nwpref`/`nwlattice`/`nwexpand`/`nwdyadprob`) delegates its own generate-or-not decision entirely to a single trailing `nwload,`xvars'` call - since `nwload`'s OWN `xvars` option correctly KEEPS its original "abort without generating, just resync" meaning (calling `nwload` directly already IS the explicit ask the whole policy is about - it has no bare default to flip), each of these 7 call sites was rewritten to translate between the caller's own now-inverted `xvars` and `nwload`'s own unchanged one (`if "`xvars'" == "" { nwload, xvars } else { nwload }`). ~15 further pass-through files (`nw2fromedge`/`nw2set`/`nwdropnodes`/`nwimport`'s other sites/`nwreplacemat`/etc.) needed NO code change at all - they just forward the caller's own `xvars` value, so the flip propagates automatically once the terminal generate-logic sites above are fixed. |
| Hardcoded `xvars` literals removed from internal/temp helper calls | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | 8 call sites (`_nwevalnetexp`, `nwcloseness`, `nwcug` x2, `nwdropnodes`, `nwimport`, `nwreach`, `nwsimindex`, `nwtoedge`) unconditionally passed the literal word `xvars` to an internal helper call purely to suppress that helper's OWN variable generation, independent of the outer command's own choice - under the new semantics, no-generate is already the default, so the literal is simply dead weight (its removal is a no-op, not a behavior change). One of these (`nwdropnodes.ado`'s own `generate()` option, line 26, `nwduplicate `netname', name(`generate') xvars`) was a genuinely BROKEN latent crash bug, not just dead weight - `nwduplicate.ado`'s own syntax has never accepted an `xvars` option at all (`syntax [anything(name=netname)], [name(string) ]`, no `xvars`, no `*` wildcard), so `nwdropnodes ..., generate(name)` ALWAYS crashed with "option xvars not allowed" (r(198)), confirmed via direct repro before the fix. New regression test added (`cscripts/test_nwdropnodes.do`) since this path had zero prior coverage. `nwgenerate.ado`'s own pure-network-expression path (`nwgen newnet = expr`) had an analogous, even more dangerous latent bug: its own hardcoded trailing `xvars` literal (after already-forwarded `` `options' ``, which already contains the caller's own raw `xvars' text verbatim if given) would have crashed with "option xvars specified twice" for any caller who actually used `xvars` on this path - fixed by removing the redundant literal outright. |
| Dead/vestigial `xvars` options found and removed | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Three genuinely non-functional `xvars` options found while auditing every one of the 33 files against their own body for real usage: `nwsym.ado` (declared in its syntax line, referenced nowhere in the body at all - the command never generates variables in the first place); `nwtranspose.ado` (declared AND passed to `nwduplicate`, but `nwduplicate` doesn't accept it either - the SAME crash-on-non-empty-value bug class as `nwdropnodes` above, except `nwtranspose` never had a test exercising it non-empty); `nwhierarchy.ado` (documented in its own `.sthlp`/`.ado` synopt line, silently absorbed by its own trailing `*` wildcard, never referenced - the command's own final `nwload `netname', labelonly` call is unconditional and was never gated by it). All three removed from syntax lines and doc headers rather than wired up, since none of the three commands actually has a "generate the full network as Stata variables" mode to gate in the first place. A FOURTH, `nwhomophily.ado`, was different - declared, documented ("do not generate Stata variables"), but never forwarded to the inner `nwdyadprob` call that does the actual generating - a genuine wiring bug, fixed by passing `` `xvars' `` through instead of removing it. |
| **Real bug found and fixed: a stray nonzero `_rc` silently leaked out of `nw_datasync.ado`'s own trailing best-effort `capture mata: mata drop` cleanup calls, on every successful run** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `nw_datasync.ado` ends several of its own branches with 2-3 `capture mata: mata drop <tempname>` calls cleaning up Mata objects that are only CONDITIONALLY created (e.g. `mode` only for two-mode networks) - each one's own harmless "object not found" failure was silently becoming the WHOLE PROGRAM's own final reported `_rc`, since neither a bare `exit` nor `exit 0` actually resets `_rc` (confirmed directly via an isolated repro: a tiny test program ending in `capture <fails>; exit 0` still reports the capture's own stale nonzero `_rc` to its caller - `_rc` reflects the most recently EXECUTED command, and control-flow statements like `exit` do not themselves count as a command execution for this purpose). This was NOT newly introduced by this unit - the bug is exactly as old as `nw_datasync.ado`'s own capture-based cleanup - but was invisible before now: `nwload.ado`'s OWN full-generate path happens to run one more, always-succeeding command (`capture order ...`) after its own call into `nw_datasync`, which incidentally reset `_rc` to 0 and papered over the leak on every call that used to take that path (the OLD default, every time). Once no-generate became the default, the vast majority of calls started routing through `nwload`'s OWN separate `xvars`-suppress branch instead (`nw_datasync `netname'; exit` - no further command after it), which has nothing to mask the same pre-existing leak - so what used to be a latent, unexercised bug became a live one, surfacing as `assert _rc == 0` failures immediately after completely ordinary calls to `nwsmall`/`nwpref`/`nwrandom`/anything else routing through this branch. Fixed by running a genuine, always-succeeding, uncaptured command (`nwname`, chosen since it's already used throughout the package to report a network's own current state) immediately before each of `nw_datasync`'s own three exit points, purely for its `_rc`-resetting side effect - itself wrapped in `_return hold`/`_return restore` (the same pattern `nwgeodesic.ado` already uses for an identical need) since `nwname` is r-class and would otherwise clobber whatever r()-results the CALLER had already posted (e.g. a preceding `nwset` call's own `r(networks)`/`r(nets)`) as an unwanted side effect of a fix that was only ever supposed to touch `_rc`. |
| **Real bug found and fixed: `add_node()` (unw_core.do) never extended the `modes` array, only `nodes`/`nodesvar`/`edge`** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Harmless while `modes` was still completely empty (`get_modes()`'s own lazy-init sizes it fresh off the CURRENT node count on first read, whenever that happens to be) - but once `modes` had already been populated by an earlier `nwset()` call, `add_node()` growing `nodes` without growing `modes` left it permanently undersized. Any later `get_modes_labeled_string()` call (`nwsummarize`, `nwsync`, ...) then indexed past its own end and crashed with "subscript invalid" (r(3301)). This bug is exactly as old as `add_node()` itself, not introduced by this unit, but was completely masked in every prior test: `nwaddnodes.ado` (the method's only live caller) used to auto-invoke `nwload` immediately afterward under the old default, and `nwload`'s own chain happened to rebuild `modes` from scratch as a side effect - once `nwaddnodes` stopped auto-loading by default, the latent bug in `add_node()` itself finally surfaced (`nwaddnodes, nodenames(...)` followed by any command reading mode labels now crashed reliably). Fixed at the actual source: `add_node()` now also appends one "1" (the same single-mode default `get_modes()` itself uses) to `modes` whenever it was already non-empty, mirroring exactly how `nodes`/`nodesvar`/`edge` are already extended two lines above. |
| Permanent regression tests updated/added for the new default | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `cscripts/test_nwlattice.do`/`test_nwhomophily.do` (both had an existing "xvars suppresses" case - each split into two: default-no-generate and `xvars`-generates, the latter now actually asserting variables DO appear, which the old test never checked in either direction since suppression was already the thing being tested - `nwhomophily`'s own "generates" check needed a second corrected guess too, from an assumed `_homophily1` naming to the actual `n1` default nwset() itself uses when no `vars()`/labels are given); `test_nwdissimilar.do` similarly split into two cases, with the expected generated-variable names corrected to match `nwdissimilar`'s own actual default naming convention (inherited node labels, e.g. `A`/`B`/`C`/`D`, not a `name()`-derived prefix - found via direct `describe` probe after the first version of this test guessed wrong); `test_nwdropnodes.do` gained new coverage for the `generate()` crash fix above (zero prior coverage existed); `test_nwvalidate.do` had two assertions removed (`r(netlist)`/`r(networks)`, checked after an UNRELATED `nwvalidate` call) that were never part of `nwvalidate`'s own documented "Stored results" (only `r(exists)`/`r(tryname)`/`r(validname)` are) - they were incidentally-surviving leftover r()-state from the PRECEDING `nwrandom` call, an undocumented, non-guaranteed accident of this package's own "poke r() macros directly via Mata rather than through `return`" convention, not a real product contract worth preserving through an increasingly complex internal call chain; `test_nwcollapse.do` gained an explicit `nwload` call after its own `nwrandom` (the first genuine example found of a CONSUMER command - `nwcollapse`, via `nwtoedge`/`nw_datasync` - that has no `xvars` option of its own and simply assumes the network is already materialized as Stata variables, unlike every generator command it follows in this chain) - which then, for the first time, actually let this test run far enough to expose a SECOND, wholly unrelated pre-existing bug: its own first `nwcollapse` call happens to produce a symmetric result that `nwsummarize` correctly reports as UNDIRECTED (`r(edges)`/`r(edges_sum)`), while the test's own hardcoded assertion checked the DIRECTED-network result names (`r(arcs)`/`r(arcs_value)` == 72, actually double-counting each of the 36 real edges) instead - confirmed via `git stash` to already fail identically on the pre-existing baseline, unrelated to this unit's own changes; fixed to check the correct result names with the correct (36) value. |
| `nwset.ado`/`nw2set.ado`/`nwload.ado`/`nw_start.sthlp`/`nwgeodesic`/`nwtranspose`-family doc text rewritten for the new polarity | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ (read-through, doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | Every `{synopt:{opt xvars}}` line across the ~30 affected `.ado`/`.sthlp` files reworded from "do not generate..." to "generate..."; `nwload.ado`/`.sthlp`'s own explanatory section and worked example rewritten to describe the new default directly rather than patching old wording into a self-contradiction; `nwset.ado`/`nw2set.ado`'s own near-identical explanatory paragraph (describing `xvars` as "essentially only produces a network object" - itself describing the OLD suppress meaning) rewritten identically in both files per this package's established "these must match" convention; `nw_start.sthlp`'s own generator-option summary table row updated; `nwgen.sthlp`/`nwgenvar.sthlp`/`nwgenerate.ado`'s own `transpose(netname)` shortcut listing had its stale `[, {opt xvars}]` mention removed (that option no longer exists on `nwtranspose` at all, see the dead-option row above). |
| Full `cscripts/` sweep (dev mode, all 140 files, run as a single batched session) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | Zero regressions beyond this project's own established baseline (dead external URLs/plotting/network-fetch dependencies - unrelated to this unit). Two full-sweep iterations were needed: the first surfaced the `_rc`-leak and `add_node()` bugs above (through `nwsmall`/`nwpref`/`nwrandom`/`nwaddnodes`/a downstream `test_sparse_index.do` failure caused by `nwaddnodes`'s own bug, not that file's own logic), the second - after both were fixed at their actual source, not papered over in the tests - passed clean on every file this unit's own changes could plausibly affect. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 94: feasible-network-size guidance in the general help files

Per explicit user instruction: "update general help files with respect to which sizes of networks are feasible with this software? The user should know what to expect and what they can do." The existing text (`nw_intro.sthlp`'s own "Limitations" section) was a single vague sentence ("optimized for < 1000 nodes... scalability has not been a major concern") with no actionable numbers - replaced with concrete, evidence-based guidance drawn from this project's own real benchmark history rather than invented figures.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nw_intro.sthlp`'s "Limitations" section rewritten as "Limitations and feasible network sizes", split into the four regimes the package actually has, each with its own real tested ceiling | ✅ | ✅ (read-through, doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | (1) Sparse structural analysis (`nwcomponents`/`nwdegree`/`nwclustering`/`nwneighbor`/unweighted `nwgeodesic`) - cites `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`'s own proven 100,000-node/1,000,000-edge benchmark, not a guess. (2) Dense-matrix-dependent commands (weighted distances, `nwevcent`, older unmigrated algorithms) - cites the real `nw_max_dense_nodes` guard (20,000, `unw_defs.ado`) and explains WHY comfortable use is realistically much lower (memory/time both scale with node count squared). (3) Loading a network as Stata variables (`nwload`/`xvars`) - explains the real bottleneck is Stata's own `c(max_k_theory)` variable ceiling, not anything this package imposes, and explicitly connects this to unit 93's own xvars-default flip immediately above: since generators no longer auto-load by default, a large network's own structure never has to touch Stata's variable budget at all unless the user explicitly asks for it. (4) `nwergm` - cites the REAL benchmarked figure (2,000 nodes, ~1.5-1.8x R parity, from unit 92's own R-vs-Stata benchmarks) rather than the user's own separate "do not benchmark beyond 10,000 nodes" INSTRUCTION, which was a ceiling on what to attempt, not a certified result - carefully worded to not overstate what was actually measured. |
| Cross-reference pointers added from the three commands users most naturally reach for when a network gets large | ✅ | ✅ (read-through, doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | `nwload.ado`/`.sthlp`, `nwset.ado`/`.sthlp`, `nwergm.ado`/`.sthlp` (both the embedded `.ado` doc header and standalone `.sthlp` copy, per this package's own "these must match" convention) each gained a `{help nw_intro##limits:feasible network sizes}` link in their own "See also" section. |
| Help rendering smoke-tested | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | `help nw_intro`/`nwload`/`nwset`/`nwergm` all render without error after the changes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 95: native graph-library feasibility investigation and selective migration (betweenness centrality)

Per explicit user instruction to investigate adopting an external native graph library (igraph, NetworKit, SuiteSparse:GraphBLAS/LAGraph, LEMON, Boost Graph Library, SNAP) as an optional performance backend for existing Mata-implemented graph commands, with a strict migration rule (native total time, including conversion overhead, must be at most half of Mata's own), and an explicit instruction not to stop at the feasibility report once evidence identifies a clear winner. Full investigation, licence audit, and evidence trail in `docs/NATIVE_GRAPH_LIBRARIES.md`.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Licence audit of all seven named/implied candidates | ✅ | n/a (research) | n/a | ✅ | Verified directly from each project's own upstream LICENSE file or package-manager metadata, not assumed: igraph is GPL-2.0-or-later (a real distribution constraint - this package has no LICENSE file of its own yet, see item 8's own repository-cleanup work); NetworKit's C++ core is MIT but Python-first-packaged; SuiteSparse:GraphBLAS is Apache-2.0 and LAGraph is BSD, BUT Homebrew's own `suite-sparse` bundle formula mixes in GPL-3.0-only/LGPL components from unrelated SuiteSparse packages - a real trap for anyone vendoring "GraphBLAS" via that path rather than GraphBLAS's own standalone upstream repo; LEMON and Boost Graph Library are both Boost Software License 1.0 (the most permissive surveyed); SNAP is BSD-3-Clause. |
| Recognition that this project's own prior sparse-Mata migration (`docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`) already banks the general-purpose win a big external library would otherwise offer | ✅ | ✅ (cites that migration's own proven 100,000-node/1,000,000-edge benchmark) | n/a | ✅ | This reframed the entire investigation from "should we adopt a graph library" to "which SPECIFIC remaining algorithms are still slow, and why" - the actual question the user's own migration rule was designed to answer. |
| Real microbenchmark evidence (not assumed) of where Mata's own interpreter overhead still hurts post-sparse-migration | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Betweenness centrality (`nwbetween`, Brandes 2001): 0.89s at 500 nodes, 13.2s at 1,000 nodes on a sparse random network - a ~15x slowdown for 2x the nodes, well beyond the algorithm's own `O(V^3)`-at-fixed-density expectation, the same interpreter-overhead signature that originally justified `nwergm`'s own native MCMC backend. A real, previously-undiscovered inefficiency was found alongside this: `calculate_betweenness()` (`unw_core.do`) still pre-allocates a dense N x (N-1) scratch matrix even though it is populated via the sparse `neighbors()` accessor - a leftover the original sparse migration's own scope did not reach. `nwkcore`/`nwcomponents` were also profiled at the same sizes and stayed fast, confirming the slowness is specific to betweenness's own algorithm shape, not a generic "Mata is slow" finding. |
| **Decision: bespoke native C kernel (matching `nwergm`'s own proven pattern), not adoption of a third-party library** | ✅ | n/a (design decision) | n/a | ✅ | Evidence-based, not a default: the sparse migration already banked the general win, igraph's GPL licence is a real constraint, and the specific need (one algorithm) does not justify a new build-system/cross-platform-toolchain investment a full external library would require. Explicitly disclosed as revisitable if a broader profiling pass (community detection, structural-equivalence measures, the remaining dense-matrix-dependent commands) turns up several more genuinely interpreter-overhead-bound algorithms. |
| `native/nwgraph.c` (new): native betweenness centrality (Brandes 2001, unweighted/dichotomized - the default `nwbetween` mode) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A new, SHARED native-graph-kernel plugin (deliberately structured to host additional algorithm codes behind one plugin later, mirroring how `ergm_mcmc.c` hosts many TERM codes behind one plugin rather than one per term) - `ALG_BETWEENNESS` is algorithm code 1. Direct, hand-verified port of `calculate_betweenness()`'s own algorithm (CSR adjacency built from the caller's own already-fully-expanded tie list - undirected ties arrive pre-symmetrized via `edgelist()`, so no reverse edge is ever auto-added in C, avoiding a double-count bug that would otherwise halve incorrectly). Compiles clean (zero warnings) via `native/Makefile`'s own now-multi-plugin `macos`/`unix` targets (per-plugin `macos-nwgraph`/`unix-nwgraph` targets also available individually). |
| `unw_core.do`: `NativeGraphInstallDir()`/`NativeGraphPluginSubdir()`/`NativeGraphPluginPath()`/`NativeGraphAvailable()` dispatcher, `calculate_betweenness_native()` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Deliberately generalized (unlike `nwergm`'s own single-purpose `ErgmNative*` functions in `unw_ergm.do`) so future native graph kernels can share this one dispatcher and the one `nwgraph.plugin`/`nwgraph_unix.plugin` binary. Marshals via a single `plugin call` per invocation (one call, not per-node/per-edge - the same boundary-crossing discipline `nwergm`'s own native backend uses), using the network's own sparse `edgelist()` (ties with weight <= 0 excluded, matching `calculate_betweenness()`'s own `edge_weight(m,n)>0` filter exactly for signed-network parity). |
| `nwbetween.ado` dispatch | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Dispatches to native whenever `NativeGraphAvailable()` is true and the weighted (Dijkstra) mode was not requested (which remains Mata-only, a documented follow-on); every platform without a compiled plugin, and the weighted mode on every platform, transparently keep using the unchanged Mata implementation - the same graceful, never-erroring fallback contract `nwergm`'s own native backend already established. |
| Certification: exact-equality contract (not a statistical tolerance) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Betweenness centrality is a deterministic, exact combinatorial quantity, unlike `nwergm`'s own stochastic MCMC sampler - `cscripts/test_nwgraph_native.do` (new) covers a small hand-built undirected graph, a small hand-built directed graph, an isolate, a disconnected graph, a signed network (negative tie correctly excluded identically by both backends), and `nwbetween.ado` itself with the native plugin file temporarily hidden mid-test to force and directly compare against the Mata fallback path. All pass, max absolute difference ~1e-12 (ordinary floating-point summation-order noise, not a real discrepancy). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 141 files): no regressions beyond this project's own established dead-external-URL baseline. |
| Real measured results against the user's own migration rule (native total time, including marshalling overhead, <= half of Mata's) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | 500 nodes: Mata 0.89s vs native 0.021s (~42x). 1,000 nodes: Mata 13.2s vs native 0.021s (~628x). Native alone additionally measured at 2,000/5,000/10,000 nodes (0.40s/1.46s/6.79s) - Mata was not re-measured at these sizes given how slow it already was at 1,000 nodes; native at 10,000 nodes is already faster in absolute terms than Mata was at 1,000. Clears the `>= 2x` migration bar by two to three orders of magnitude - an unambiguous, evidence-backed "yes, migrate this one," not a marginal call. |
| **Real, disclosed limitation found while benchmarking**: the Mata-side marshalling step itself (not the native kernel) becomes a bottleneck at very large scale | ❌ (deliberate, disclosed scope limit) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | At 20,000 nodes, `calculate_betweenness_native()`'s own `edgelist()` + `select()` marshalling step became slow and memory-hungry enough (multiple minutes, tens of GB resident memory observed) to dominate wall time - the run was deliberately killed rather than risk exhausting the host machine, rather than treated as "native doesn't scale" (the C kernel itself is not the bottleneck at that size). Reading the sparse CSR arrays directly instead of via the `edgelist()` intermediate representation is the well-scoped follow-on fix, not attempted in this pass. |
| Cross-platform CI updated | ✅ | ✅ (macOS build verified locally; Windows/Linux steps added but not run - no toolchain available in this environment, same disclosed limitation `ergm_mcmc.c`'s own CI entry already carries) | n/a | ✅ | `.github/workflows/build-plugins.yml` renamed "Build native ERGM plugins" → "Build native plugins", updated to build/upload/commit both `ergm_mcmc.*` and `nwgraph.*` binaries for all three platforms (added a `cl /LD` invocation for `nwgraph.c` in the Windows job; Linux/macOS jobs needed no changes beyond artifact path updates, since `native/Makefile`'s own `macos`/`unix` targets already build every plugin). |
| Remaining candidates explicitly scoped as follow-on, not silently dropped | ✅ (disclosed) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `docs/NATIVE_GRAPH_LIBRARIES.md`'s own "Remaining candidates and scope" section: the marshalling-overhead fix above, community detection (Louvain - flagged as a likely second candidate but not yet cleanly profiled), k-core (profiled, found already fast, no migration evidence yet), weighted betweenness (not profiled), structural-equivalence/two-mode/remaining dense-matrix-dependent commands (not investigated), and a note that a genuinely broader external-library architecture remains available IF a wider profiling pass eventually turns up several more interpreter-overhead-bound algorithms - revisit the "bespoke kernel vs. library" calculus then, not now. |

Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 141 files): no regressions beyond the established dead-external-URL baseline. Certified at **L2**.

## Harmonisation phase, unit 96: `nwbetween`'s weighted mode - Opsahl citation and a real sign-of-alpha bug found while adding it

User-requested: "Nwbetween needs reference to Opsahl paper on weighted centrality, alpha is from there." While adding the citation, checked that `nwbetween, weighted`'s own `alpha()` actually implements the paper it was being credited to - it did not.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Opsahl, Agneessens and Skvoretz (2010) citation added to `nwbetween.ado`/`.sthlp` | ✅ | n/a (doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | New "References" section (Brandes 2001 for the base algorithm, Opsahl et al. 2010 for the weighted generalization), matching `nwgeodesic.ado`'s own existing citation of the same paper for the same reason. |
| **Real bug found and fixed: `calculate_betweenness_weighted()`'s own edge cost used `weight^alpha` (no negation) - the mathematical INVERSE of Opsahl's own definition** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Opsahl's own weighted-distance definition (already correctly implemented by `calculate_distances_dijkstra()`, which `nwgeodesic`'s own `alpha()` uses) is `(1/weight)^alpha` - a STRONGER tie is a SHORTER effective distance. `calculate_betweenness_weighted()`'s own header comment already (wrongly) claimed "the same alpha-exponent weight-to-distance convention this package already uses for calculate_distances()/nwgeodesic" while its own code computed the opposite (`weight^alpha`, no negation) - confirmed via direct hand-calculation on the existing test network (A-B=1, A-C=4, B-C=2, C-D=1, undirected): under the corrected `1/weight` cost, the true shortest A-B path runs THROUGH C (cost 1/4+1/2=0.75, cheaper than the direct tie's own cost 1/1=1) - a result the old, wrong formula could never produce, since it priced every direct tie at its own raw weight regardless of any stronger indirect route. Fixed to `edge_weight(m,n)^(-alpha)`, matching `calculate_distances_dijkstra()`'s own already-correct formula exactly. `cscripts/test_nwbetween.do`'s own hand-verified expected values updated (A=0,B=2,C=2,D=0, computed under the old bug, to the now-correct A=0,B=0,C=3,D=0 - re-derived and verified directly against the fixed Mata function, not merely accepted from a single run) with the full corrected hand-derivation recorded in the test's own comment. The `alpha(0)` degenerate-case check (weighted reduces to unweighted) was NOT able to catch this bug on its own, since `weight^0 == weight^(-0) == 1` regardless of the sign error - noted explicitly in the test's own comment as a caution for any future alpha-convention change. |
| Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 141 files) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | No regressions beyond the established dead-external-URL baseline. Only `nwbetween.ado` calls `calculate_betweenness_weighted()` (confirmed via grep - no other blast radius). |
| Two stale `Pending` rows found and removed while reviewing this document for the user's own follow-up request to work through everything left over | ✅ (fixed) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | "Distance-family sparse migration \| Stage 0 remainder \| Large" - `docs/ROADMAP.md` already marks this ✅ done (harmonisation unit 49); this row was simply never removed when that work completed. The `nwaddnodes.ado`/`nwcollapse.ado` "uncommitted rewrite crashes with `get_modes_labeled_string(): 3301 subscript invalid`" row - this is the EXACT bug already found and fixed in harmonisation unit 93 (`add_node()`'s own missing `modes` array extension), just never cross-referenced back to this older, differently-worded Pending row when it was fixed. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 97: `nwevcent` sparse migration, a spectral-shift power-iteration fix, and a codebase-wide `syntax` "no"-prefix audit

User-requested follow-up to unit 96: "follow up with all the things left over, including migrating commands to the sparse matrix architecture." `nwevcent` was the last command still documented as a deliberate, permanent dense holdout (`docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`/`docs/ROADMAP.md` both said so) - re-examined on the premise that eigenvector centrality only ever needs the *dominant* eigenpair, not a full eigendecomposition, so the "no sparse eigensolver in Mata" justification for staying dense didn't actually hold.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwevcent` migrated from dense `symeigensystem()` to sparse power iteration | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New `nw_evcentrality_matvec()` (one sparse matrix-vector product via `neighbors()`/`neighbors_in()`/`has_edge()`/`edge_weight()`, handling both `nosym` "as-is directed" and the default max-symmetrized union-of-neighbors case) plus a rewritten `nw_evcentrality()` driving the iteration - O(nnz) per step, no dense `edge` matrix ever built. |
| **Real numerical bug found and fixed: plain power iteration doesn't converge on bipartite/tree-structured graphs** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A graph whose adjacency matrix has a second-largest eigenvalue tied in magnitude with the dominant one but opposite in sign (every tree/bipartite graph has this) makes naive `x <- A*x` oscillate forever - confirmed directly on a 3-node path graph (eigenvalues +sqrt(2)/0/-sqrt(2)), not a theoretical worry. The standard igraph/NetworkX fix (iterate on `A^2` instead) was tried first and found to be its own distinct wrong answer here, not just a slower-converging correct one: squaring merges the +lambda/-lambda eigenspaces into one degenerate eigenspace of `A^2`, and for the symmetric all-ones starting vector this code uses, power iteration on `A^2` converges EXACTLY to a uniform vector (confirmed by hand: `(1,1,1)` is itself an exact eigenvector of `A^2` for this graph) rather than the true dominant eigenvector `(0.5, 0.707, 0.5)` of `A` alone. Fixed instead with a spectral shift - iterate on `A + cI` where `c` is a Gershgorin row-sum bound (computed via one extra call to the same matvec function, on the all-ones vector) - which preserves `A`'s own eigenvectors while separating +lambda from -lambda in *value*, not just magnitude, avoiding the eigenspace-merging failure entirely. Verified against the hand-derived correct answer for the 3-node path graph. |
| Zero-dominant-eigenvalue ("missing") contract preserved for the genuinely-empty/all-isolates case; NOT preserved for a directed-acyclic network under `nosym` | ✅ (deliberate scope decision) | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (matches existing doc caveat) | The prior dense implementation returned all-missing whenever `symeigensystem()`'s own max eigenvalue was exactly 0 - both for a truly empty network AND, incidentally, for any nilpotent/DAG-structured "as-is directed" adjacency matrix (a defective, non-diagonalizable case `symeigensystem()` was never actually a rigorous solver for in the first place, since it requires a symmetric input). The empty-network case is cheaply and exactly preserved (checking the matvec of the starting vector is exactly zero, before any shift is applied). The DAG-under-`nosym` case is NOT specially detected post-migration: a defective matrix's power iteration converges to the dominant-eigenvalue direction only at a slow, polynomial (not geometric) rate, so a post-convergence "is the true eigenvalue ~0" check never actually fires within a reasonable iteration budget - confirmed directly (residual still ~0.004 after 1,000 iterations on a 3-node directed chain, nowhere near a 1e-8 threshold). No existing test asserted the missing-for-DAG behavior, and the command's own help file already documents "only defined for connected networks," a strictly broader caveat that already covers this case along with every other not-strongly-connected input (e.g. a disconnected undirected network, never specially handled either). Not engineered around further given the lack of any regression contract and the existing doc caveat. |
| **Real, unrelated bug found and fixed while testing the above: `build_sparse_index()` crashed on any all-isolates directed network** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (Mata code comment) | `if (isdirect & nnz > 0)` skipped `build_reverse_index()` entirely whenever a directed network had zero ties, leaving `rowptr_in` sized `J(0,1,0)` instead of the correctly-sized `J(n+1,1,1)` that `build_reverse_index()`'s own `nnz==0` branch would have produced - `neighbors_in()`/`degree_in()` then crashed ("subscript invalid") on any such network. The prior dense `nwevcent` implementation never called `neighbors_in()` at all, so this had never surfaced; the new sparse power iteration's default (symmetrized) path does, for the first time. Fixed by removing the `& nnz > 0` condition. |
| **Real, live, previously-undiscovered bug found via a chain of debugging the above: an EARLIER session's own "fix" to `nosym`-style option handling got Stata's `syntax` "no"-prefix convention backwards, in at least 4 places** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Per Stata's own `[P] syntax`: declaring a bare `no<stem>` option makes `syntax` populate the STEM local (e.g. `` `sym' `` for `nosym`, set to the literal string `"nosym"` when passed) - `` `no<stem>' `` itself is never populated at all, confirmed directly against isolated minimal test programs across 8 different stems (`sym`, `current`, `replace`, `dichotomize`, `diag`, `_spec_cols`, `variation`, `net`, `sync` - all but a `not`-prefixed one, which strips differently), not assumed from memory or documentation alone. An earlier session had already found `nwbetween`'s `nosym` doing nothing, but "fixed" it backwards - changing the check FROM the correct `` `sym' `` TO the never-populated `` `nosym' `` - re-breaking it identically, undetected because no test asserted `nosym` actually changes `nwbetween`'s output (it was verified only via `nwbetween`'s own `weighted`/`alpha` test additions in unit 96, which don't exercise `nosym` at all). Found while independently re-deriving this same convention during the `nwevcent` migration (an in-progress attempted "fix" to `nwevcent`'s own, actually-already-correct `nosym` check was caught and reverted before being an actual regression - see `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`'s own corrected account). A full codebase grep for every `` `no<word>' `` local reference, cross-checked against each file's own `syntax` line, found the identical backwards bug live in exactly 3 more places: `nwbetween.ado` (both symmetrize-before and cleanup-after checks), `nwload.ado` (`nocurrent` - "make current" ran unconditionally regardless of the option), and `nwreplacemat.ado` (a recursive self-call forwarding `` `nosync' `` instead of `` `sync' ``, so the option never actually propagated through the resize path). All four fixed to check the correct stem local; `nwqap.ado`'s `novariation`, `nwset.ado`'s `notvalid`, and `nwreplace.ado`'s `nonet` were also flagged by the grep but confirmed NOT instances of this bug (plain manually-assigned locals, unrelated to `syntax`'s no-prefix mechanism, despite the superficially similar name). Verified empirically post-fix with a directed 3-cycle test case for `nwbetween` (symmetrized: betweenness 0/0/0, folded into a triangle; as-is: 1/1/1) where the two modes are provably forced to differ - a stronger check than the simple-chain case used earlier, which cannot distinguish the two modes at all (a chain's shortest paths are identical either way). |
| Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 141 files, after every fix above) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | No regressions beyond the established dead-external-URL baseline (`nwplotmatrix`/`nwimport`/`nwsimmelian`/`nwplot`/`nw_twomode_metadata`/`nwvalue`/`nw2project`/`nwexport`/`nwdegree`/`nwshared`/`nwuse`), identical before and after. `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` rebuilt clean. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 98: `nwuse` example-dataset crash fixed at its real root cause; a systemic `_rc`-staleness bug in `nwsync.ado`

User-requested follow-up: "Go through all known problems and bugs... Fix all known issues" (data-hosting specifically: "we uploaded all data files to github, so we can simply redirect there"). The GitHub data-host redirect itself was already done (unit 88/webnwuse.ado's own comment); this unit is about the `nwuse.ado` crash that redirect alone didn't fix, previously logged in this document's own Pending list as an under-diagnosed "macro-quoting mismatch."

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Real root cause #1: `nwuse.ado` unconditionally forced a `.nwdta` suffix onto every target** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Only `florentine`/`gang`/`glasgow` actually ship a `.nwdta` (a genuine `nwsave()` restoration file); `gang2`/`hpotter`/`institutions`/`klas12b`/`stockholm`/`usstates`/`example1` are plain `.dta` files meant to be inspected/converted manually (`netexample.sthlp` already advertises `webnwuse usstates` etc. as the intended way to load them). `nwuse`/`nwwebuse` could never load any of the latter - it always tried to fetch a nonexistent `<name>.nwdta` and crashed. Fixed to try `.nwdta` first, falling back to plain `.dta` and skipping the network-restoration logic entirely in that case; `webnwuse.ado`'s trailing `nwload, labelonly` call (meaningless with no network) wrapped in `capture` for the same reason. |
| **Real root cause #2: a stray extra backtick in `nwname `n', ... newvalued("``v'") ...`** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Passed the literal text `` `false ``/`` `true `` (backtick included) to `newvalued()` instead of the plain string, which `nwname.ado`'s own option processing silently didn't recognize - the actual cause of the crash on every genuine `.nwdta` restoration (i.e. `florentine`/`gang`/`glasgow` themselves, previously misdiagnosed as a filename-quoting issue). Found via `set trace on`, confirmed directly rather than assumed. |
| **Real, unrelated, higher-impact bug found while root-causing the above: `nwsync.ado` leaves a stale, misleading nonzero `_rc` on a very common path** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `capture confirm variable `r(vars)'` (checking whether a network has ever been `nwload`ed into Stata variables) legitimately fails on the common case where it hasn't - but nothing reset `_rc` afterward, so any caller checking `_rc` right after `nwsync` (or after `nwsym`, which calls `nwsync` internally) saw a spurious failure. This is the exact same `_rc`-staleness bug class already found and fixed elsewhere in this package (`nwbrokerage`/`nwego`/`nwaltergen`/`nwcompressobs`/`nwplot`, per those units' own certified rows) - not previously recognized as also present in `nwsync.ado`, which has much broader blast radius (called throughout the package, directly and via `nwsym`). Confirmed directly: a bare `capture`/`local`/`display`/`qui`/`mata:` statement does NOT reset a stale `_rc` in Stata, and neither does `exit 0` (tried first, verified NOT to work via an isolated test) - only wrapping a trivially-successful command in `capture` (`capture confirm number 1`, matching this package's own established fix idiom) actually does. Fixed identically to the established pattern. |
| Two stray, superseded root-level example-dataset duplicates found and archived | ✅ (fixed) | n/a | n/a | ✅ | `florentine.dta`/`gang.dta` at the repo root are not copies of the active `data/` versions (confirmed via `diff` - a different, older edgelist-plus-metadata legacy format tied to the already-archived `nwuse_old.ado` mechanism). Their presence was actively harmful, not just clutter: fix #1 above's new `.dta` fallback started silently picking up the stray root copy instead of the intended `data/florentine.nwdta`, breaking two tests' own bare `nwuse florentine` calls. Archived to `old/data/` (see `LEGACY_FILES.md`); both affected tests (`test_nwdegree.do`, `test_nwplot.do`) switched to the now-genuinely-working `nwwebuse florentine`, matching every other test file's existing convention. |
| Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 141 files) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | **10 of 11 previously-documented baseline failures now genuinely pass** (`nwplotmatrix`/`nwsimmelian`/`nw_twomode_metadata`/`nwvalue`/`nw2project`/`nwexport`/`nwdegree`/`nwshared`/`nwuse`/`nwplot` - all were downstream of the two `nwuse.ado` bugs above, not independent issues as previously assumed). Only `nwimport` remains failing, and it is a genuinely different, unrelated cause: a third-party academic host (`vlado.fmf.uni-lj.si`, a UCINET-format dataset mirror this project does not control or host a copy of) confirmed completely unreachable (connection timeout, not merely a 404) via direct probe - out of scope to fix without either finding an authoritative alternate copy of that exact file or securing rights to mirror it into this project's own `data/` folder, neither done here. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 99: `_nwdeploy.ado` fixed end to end; `net install` from GitHub now works

User-requested follow-up: "fix all known issues" / "clean up GitHub repo to have a structure so that things can be released publicly." `_nwdeploy.ado` (the `.pkg`/`.sthlp`-index generator) had never run to completion in this codebase's recent history - two latent bugs were already logged in this document's own Pending list, found while investigating `net install` support during unit 89. Root-caused and fixed all the way through, not just past the two previously-known crashes - three more were hiding behind them, only reachable once the earlier ones stopped blocking the run.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bug 1 (already logged): a literal unmatched backtick in a `.sthlp`/`.ado` comment crashes `_nwdeploy`'s own line-scanning helper | ✅ (new instance fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Not the same file as the one already fixed in unit 89 (`nw_programming.sthlp`) - a second instance in `nwergm.ado`/`nwergm.sthlp` (`` `balance` `` used as informal markdown-style emphasis instead of proper SMCL `{cmd:...}` markup). Fixed the same way: replaced with `{cmd:balance}`. |
| Bug 2 (already logged, now root-caused): `switchedtocommand{bf:nw2clustering}."" invalid name` (r198) | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `getcmddesc()`'s own `strpos("`line'", ...)`/`substr("`cmddesc'",...)` used plain double quotes around a macro whose value is an arbitrary `.sthlp` source line - which routinely contains its own literal embedded double quotes (a `di "..."` line from a command's doc header, in this case `nwclustering.ado`'s own two-mode-network redirect message). The embedded quotes prematurely closed the plain `"..."` literal, leaving a bare trailing token Stata tried to interpret as a command name. Fixed with compound quotes (`` `"..."' ``), which handle embedded literal quotes correctly. |
| **New bug found once bugs 1-2 stopped blocking the run: a literal apostrophe in a command's own one-line description crashes a different `_nwdeploy` helper** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `` `=length(`"`cmddesc'"') - 1' `` - nesting an extended macro function (`` `=...' ``, itself closed by a trailing single quote) around a compound-quoted string whose *content* contains a literal apostrophe (e.g. `nwmodularity.sthlp`'s "Score an existing node partition using Newman's modularity") makes Stata misread that apostrophe as the extended function's own closing quote, silently dropping the rest of the expression (`"too few quotes"` / `"too many ')' or ']'"` - confirmed via an isolated repro before touching the real file). Fixed in three places by computing the length as its own separate `local` first, avoiding the nesting hazard entirely. |
| **New bug found once the above three stopped blocking the run: `nw_alphabetical.sthlp`/`nw_topical.sthlp` regenerated with malformed `{p_end{p_end}` tags** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `getcmddesc()` was designed to strip a source line's own trailing `{p_end}` tag before the caller re-appends a fresh one - but it stripped only the last **character** (`-1`), leaving a dangling `{p_end` (6 chars, no closing brace) that then concatenated with the caller's own literal `"{p_end}"` into `"...{p_end{p_end}"` - malformed SMCL, invisible until now because `_nwdeploy` had never gotten this far before. Fixed to strip the full 7-character `"{p_end}"` tag. Verified by rendering both regenerated files via a real `help` call (`_rc == 0` for both) and checking brace-balance directly, not just visual inspection. |
| **New, unrelated issue found in the same pass: `nw_alphabetical.sthlp` was never actually alphabetical** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `` : dir "..." files "*.ado"/"*.sthlp" `` returns filesystem order, not alphabetical - true since this tool was written, just never visible before since the tool never completed a run. Sorted explicitly via Mata (`sort(tokens(...)', 1)`) before iterating. |
| `_nwdeploy.ado`'s own embedded `contact@nwcommands.org`/`contact[at]nwcommands.org` strings and `{browse "http://nwcommands.org"}` template, plus the same dead email in `_nwnodelab.ado`/`nwdropnodes.ado`/`nwmovie.ado`/`nwcloseness.ado`/`nwmoviexy.ado`/`nwvalidvars.ado`'s own file headers, and `nwcommands.sthlp`/`nw_intro.sthlp`/`netexample.sthlp`'s own homepage links/hosting description | ✅ (fixed) | n/a (doc-only) | n/a | ✅ | Updated to `thomas.u.grund@gmail.com` and the project's real GitHub repository URL respectively - part of the same "go through all known problems, fix dead links" pass. The one *citation* mention of the dead URL (`nw_intro.sthlp`'s 2014 bibliographic reference) was deliberately left as-is - a citation reproduces what the cited work itself said, dead link or not. |
| **`net install` from GitHub now actually works**: `nwcommands-hlp.pkg`/`nwcommands-ext.pkg`/`nwcommands-dlg.pkg` generated for the first time (previously blocked entirely by the bugs above; only `nwcommands-ado.pkg` existed), a new `stata.toc` added, `nwinstall.ado`'s three `net from "http://nwcommands.org"` calls rerouted to the GitHub raw path | ✅ | ✅ (every `f`-line in all four `.pkg` files confirmed to reference a file that actually exists) | ✅ | ✅ | Rerouting alone wasn't enough - `_pkg_ado.txt`/`_pkg_hlp.txt` also referenced eight files that no longer exist at the repository root (`nwsort.ado`/`.sthlp` - deprecated, moved to `deprecated/`; `florentine.dta`/`gang.dta` - archived to `old/data/` this same harmonisation phase, unit 98; `glasgow.dta`/`klas12b.dta`/`kapmine.dat`/`madrid.dat`/`wiring.dat` - example data that was never actually bundled with the code package, only ever served from `data/` via `nwwebuse`) - removed from both manifests. |
| **A genuinely new, previously-undiscovered Stata constraint found while first testing a live `net install`: `.pkg` files have a hard, undocumented-in-practice line-count limit** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `net install "nwcommands-ado"` (117 `f`-lines) and `nwcommands-hlp` (119 `f`-lines) both failed with `"package file too long"` (r640) even though every referenced file existed and the `.pkg` syntax itself was valid - never caught before because `_nwdeploy` never ran to completion, so nobody had ever actually attempted `net install` against a *generated* package this large. Confirmed the exact boundary via a local binary search (103 total lines - `v`/`d` header plus `f`-lines - installs; 104 does not). Fixed by chunking: `_nwdeploy.ado` gained a new `nwdeploy_writepkgchunks` helper that splits a manifest into multiple numbered `.pkg` files (`nwcommands-ado1.pkg`, `nwcommands-ado2.pkg`, ... - 80 `f`-lines per chunk, a comfortable margin below the measured limit) and returns the chunk count so `stata.toc` is generated to match exactly; `nwinstall.ado` gained a corresponding install loop (try chunk `i`, stop cleanly on the first one that doesn't exist) for both `ado` (previously not installable via `nwinstall` at all - a gap this fix also closes) and `help`.
