Implemented with a scope refinement to protect the many existing internal callers (21 files, 28 call sites, confirmed via `grep`) that rely on `nwset`'s own auto-numbering for their own *unspecified*-name defaults (e.g. deriving a fresh `_similar`/`_correlate`-style name each call): only an *explicit*, caller-chosen `name()` is now held to the strict create/replace convention (errors on collision, r(6099), matching `nwgenerate`'s own message shape); an unspecified name keeps the old auto-numbering behavior unchanged, since the caller expressed no intent about a specific name there. The identical fix was independently needed in `nwfromedge.ado` (a separate creation path with its own independent `nw_validate`-based collision handling - `nwset`'s own `edgelist` option dispatches to it, so fixing `nwset.ado` alone would not have covered that path). Verified all three of the user's own flagged edge cases directly: replacing a network leaves exactly one copy registered under the requested name with the new content; a separately-named network survives a `replace` untouched; and replacing the *only* registered network correctly rebuilds the shared `nw` registry object first (`nwdrop` itself drops that whole object once the last network in it is gone, confirmed directly - the replace path would otherwise find nothing to register the replacement into). |
| **A fourth, related bug, flagged by the user as "secondary hardening worth doing regardless"**: referencing any nonexistent network (not just one caught by the first bug above) raised a raw Mata "type mismatch: exp.exp: transmorphic found where struct expected" (r(3000)) instead of `nw_syntax`'s own documented, clean "Network X not found" - whenever the `nw` registry object didn't exist at all yet (not merely "0 networks registered") | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Root-caused to a single missing `capture` in `nw_syntax.ado` (used by nearly every command in the package to resolve a network name): its own network-lookup line was the only one of three adjacent lookup statements not wrapped in `capture`, so an uncaptured Mata error aborted before the surrounding "not found" fallback a few lines below could ever run. This is what made the first bug in this unit look like memory corruption rather than a clean not-found error - the never-created network's later reference hit this exact path. Confirmed the fix doesn't mask any other genuine error by re-running the full `cscripts/` suite (`nw_syntax` is used extremely widely) - no regressions. |
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | New/updated cases in `cscripts/test_nwset.do` and `cscripts/test_nwfromedge.do`, including a pre-existing `test_nwset.do` assertion that had certified the *old* auto-numbering-on-explicit-collision behavior as correct - updated to reflect the new, intentional behavior change (that old assertion was testing exactly the bug this unit fixes). Full `cscripts/` suite (144 files) re-run in dev mode after all four fixes: 144/144 pass, zero regressions - including confirming none of the 21 files/28 call sites that call `nwset ... name(...)` internally broke under the new explicit-name collision guard. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 117: `nw2clustering` fixed - completely broken on every bipartite network before this, not merely a corner case

Continuing autonomously past the performance pass and the user's own `nwset` bug reports, per the standing "find other things on the roadmap" instruction: this closes a Pending item flagged (not fixed) during unit 103's benchmark study - `nw2clustering` crashed with a reshape error on any bipartite network at all, including tiny ones.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Root cause found and fixed: redundant duplicate rows from a chain of m:m merges against per-key-unique lookup tables** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | This command walks a growing 4-path (`ego0-alter0-ego1-alter1-ego2-alter2-ego3`) via five successive `merge m:m` steps against tables that are each uniquely keyed by construction (`ego_list`/`alter_list`, built via their own earlier `reshape wide ... i(alter)`/`i(ego)`) - an m:m merge against a uniquely-keyed `using` table broadcasts the same using-row to every master-row sharing that key, so once the master side already has more than one row for a given key (a real possibility this deep into a multi-hop enumeration), the merge produces bit-for-bit identical duplicate rows. The very next `reshape`'s own `i()`-varlist then correctly refuses to accept those as unique observations - the actual crash (previously misdiagnosed in Pending as "a malformed i()-varlist missing a numeric suffix"; the varlist itself was correct, the underlying data genuinely wasn't unique). Confirmed directly that the duplicates were full-row duplicates, not merely same-key-different-value rows (`duplicates report` with no varlist gave identical counts to one restricted to just the reshape's own key columns) - so a plain `duplicates drop` immediately after each of the five merge steps is lossless by construction, not a heuristic that could silently discard a genuinely different path. Fixed at all five merge sites, not just the one specific network structure that happened to crash first (confirmed each is susceptible to the identical pattern). |
| **Correctness, verified by property rather than a from-scratch re-derivation of the underlying formula** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | This fix only removes redundant duplicate rows upstream of the existing Opsahl & Panzarasa (2009) weighted bipartite clustering computation - nothing about that formula's own logic was touched, so no new mathematical certification of the formula itself was performed (none existed before this fix either, since the command could never complete on bipartite input at all). Verified instead that the crash is genuinely gone (the original 4-node repro plus 8 further random bipartite structures of varying size/density, none erroring) and that every reported value is either missing (a node with no possible 4-path to normalize by - the same convention `nwclustering` already uses elsewhere in this package for degree<2 nodes) or within the mathematically required [0,1] bounds for a normalized ratio. |
| **Zero prior test coverage** - the one existing case in `cscripts/test_nw2clustering.do` built a network via `nwfromedge` and never actually called `nw2clustering` at all | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Replaced with a real test: the original crash repro plus the 8-structure sweep above, both now passing. |
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | Full `cscripts/` suite (143 files) re-run in dev mode after this change: 143/143 pass, zero regressions. Performance (this command's own O(N^4)-shaped Stata reshape chain, a separate, already-tracked Pending item) is unaddressed - this unit is a correctness fix only. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 118: `nwmixing` fixed - three independent bugs meant it never worked at all

Continuing autonomously down the roadmap: closes the other Pending item flagged (not fixed) alongside `nw2clustering` during unit 103's benchmark study - `nwmixing` failed a basic precondition check even against a plain, freshly-`nwload`ed attribute variable.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Bug 1: a pure naming mismatch** - `nwmixing.ado` referenced `` `attribute'_nwego ``/`` `attribute'_nwalter ``, but `nwtoedge.ado`'s own `egovars()`/`altervars()` option actually materializes `` `attribute'_ego ``/`` `attribute'_alter `` (confirmed directly from `nwtoedge.ado`'s own rename logic, which concatenates the attribute name with the `ego()`/`alter()` option's default value - `"_ego"`/`"_alter"` per `unw_defs.ado`, never `"_nwego"`/`"_nwalter"`) | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The command could never have found the variables it needed - not a corner case, its own basic documented usage. |
| **Bug 2, found once past the first: `rep_EIvar()` (this file's own permutation-test helper) declared a `real matrix` parameter but was called with `` `netobj'->get_matrix() `` - a POINTER to a matrix, never dereferenced** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Fixed by dereferencing it (`` *`netobj'->get_matrix() ``). Also removed two leftover bare `net1`/`attr` statements inside `rep_EIvar()` itself - harmless without unit 109's own bug class (Mata auto-displays any uncaptured top-level expression), but clearly forgotten debug output, not intentional. |
| **Bug 3, found while writing this unit's own test coverage: a string (categorical) `attribute()` crashed Stata's own `tab ..., matcol() matrow()` call** ("option matcol() not allowed", r198) | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `tab`'s own `matcol()`/`matrow()` options (used to build the mixing-table matrices this command's downstream E-I-index computation needs) are not allowed on a string variable. The command already built a numeric encoded copy of the attribute for its own permutation test (`encode ..., generate(`att')`) but passed the raw, possibly-string attribute to `nwtoedge`'s `egovars()`/`altervars()` regardless - fixed by forwarding the already-encoded numeric copy specifically when `attribute()` is a string variable (confirmed via `confirm string variable`), while numeric attributes keep using the original variable name (preserving this command's own existing `x_ego`/`x_alter`-style column naming, rather than switching to an anonymous temp name unconditionally). The encoded copy's own auto-generated value label is applied to the materialized columns so categorical labels (e.g. "red"/"blue") still display correctly, not just their underlying numeric codes. |
| **Zero prior test coverage** | ✅ (fixed) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New `cscripts/test_nwmixing.do`: undirected/directed/valued numeric-attribute cases plus a string-attribute case (the one that surfaced bug 3), all asserting a clean run and a real `r(EI_index)`. |
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | Full `cscripts/` suite (144 files with the new test) re-run in dev mode after all three fixes: 144/144 pass, zero regressions. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 119: `nwhomophily`'s statistical effect re-verified - the earlier "byte-identical output" finding is not reproducible

Closes a Pending item that had left `nwhomophily`'s own core statistical effect uncertified: a prior investigation reported `homophily(2)` and `homophily(-2)` producing byte-identical output networks under the same seed. No code change was needed - the feature itself was already correct; the earlier finding was a test-methodology artifact.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Re-investigated end to end and found the effect genuinely works correctly - not reproducible** | n/a (no bug found) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Confirmed at every layer of the pipeline: the homophily-weighted tie-probability matrix itself is correctly and oppositely signed for `+2` vs `-2` (`exp(2)`≈7.39 vs `exp(-2)`≈0.135 for same-group pairs, `exp(0)=1` for different-group pairs, verified by directly inspecting `nwdyadprob.ado`'s own intermediate `_tempdyad` weight column); `gsample`'s own weighted sampling genuinely favors higher-weight observations (confirmed via an isolated, `nwhomophily`-independent repro: 10/10 sampled observations came from a 100x-heavier-weighted group); and the two output networks are demonstrably NOT identical (140 of 380 dyads differ between `homophily(2)` and `homophily(-2)` on a 20-node test network). |
| **Root cause of the earlier "identical" finding, most plausibly: this package's own `get_nodenames()`/`nwtomata` row-column order is sorted LEXICOGRAPHICALLY by node label** (`"n1","n10","n11",...,"n19","n2","n20","n3",...`), **not by numeric/creation order** - confirmed directly | ✅ (found, documented) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A same-group/different-group comparison that assumes row `i` of the output matrix corresponds to the `i`-th CREATED node (rather than mapping each row's own label back to its real attribute value first) silently compares the wrong pairs of nodes - this session's own first attempt at re-verifying this fell into the identical trap (producing an apparently INVERTED effect) before the row-order property was noticed and corrected for. A genuinely useful, non-obvious gotcha for testing any command in this package via direct matrix comparison, not specific to `nwhomophily` - worth keeping in mind for any future `nwtomata`-based verification work. |
| **Correctness, verified directly with a strong, unambiguous directional test** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Once nodes are mapped back to their attribute value via their own label (not assumed row position): `homophily(2)` produces far more same-group ties than different-group ties (32 vs 6, on a 20-node/10% density test network); `homophily(-2)` produces the exact opposite (6 vs 32) - a clean, strongly significant, correctly-signed effect in both directions, added as a real regression assertion to `cscripts/test_nwhomophily.do` (previously a documented-but-unasserted "NOT asserted here" comment). |
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | Full `cscripts/` suite (144 files) re-run in dev mode after adding the new assertions: 144/144 pass, zero regressions. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 120: `nwset`'s `mat()` option now accepts a bare Stata matrix name

Closes a Pending item open since unit 76 (the ERGM work that found it, deliberately scoped out at the time as "out of scope for the ERGM-focused unit that found it").

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`mat()` accepts a bare Stata matrix name, not just a literal expression or a bare Mata variable name** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `mat()`'s own argument has always been evaluated as a bare Mata expression - a literal expression (`(0,1\1,0)`) parses directly as an anonymous Mata matrix constant regardless, and a bare Mata variable name already works too (Mata variables are themselves valid bare expressions) - but a name referring to an existing STATA matrix does not auto-import into Mata, which has no such convenience (confirmed directly: `matrix define X = (0,1\1,0)` then `nwset, mat(X)` failed with "X not found", r(3499), before this fix). Fixed by detecting an existing Stata matrix name via `confirm matrix` first, then copying it into Mata explicitly via `st_matrix()` before proceeding - a literal expression or Mata variable name simply fails `confirm matrix` harmlessly (neither is a valid/existing Stata matrix name) and falls through to the original, unchanged code path. |
| **Correctness, verified for all three input forms** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New assertions in `cscripts/test_nwset.do`: a bare Stata matrix name now creates the correct network (content verified via a round-trip `nwtomata` comparison against the original matrix); the pre-existing literal-expression and bare-Mata-variable-name forms both still work exactly as before, unaffected by the new code path. | 
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | Full `cscripts/` suite (144 files) re-run in dev mode after this change: 144/144 pass, zero regressions - notable given `nwset.ado` is this package's single most widely-used foundational command. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 121: `nwassortativity` - new command, closing a confirmed feature gap

Continuing autonomously down the roadmap (`docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own "Area J (similarity/homophily/mixing/assortativity)... needs... a clean confirmation of assortativity's absence"): confirmed via a direct file-listing audit that no assortativity-related command existed anywhere in this package (not to be confused with `nwmixing`'s own categorical E-I index, or `nwcorrelate`'s own neighborhood-profile correlation - genuinely different questions). Newman's (2002) assortativity coefficient is a standard, widely-used SNA measure with no prior implementation here.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **New command `nwassortativity`** (`.ado`/`.sthlp`), plus a new `NWdef::calculate_assortativity_pairs()` Mata method (`unw_core.do`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Computes Newman's `r`: the Pearson correlation, across every tie, between the value of some quantity (default: each node's own degree; `attribute()` for an arbitrary numeric node attribute) at one end of the tie and its value at the other. Built on `connected_neighbors()` (added for `nwtriads`/`nwclique`, units 114/115) - directed input is symmetrized (connected in either direction), the same established convention this package already uses for measures with no natural directed generalization (`nwclustering`/`nwclique`). `calculate_assortativity_pairs()` builds the doubled (attr[i], attr[j]) pair list once per tie (both orderings), so the caller's own `correlation()` call (Mata's existing utility, already used elsewhere in this codebase) gives Newman's `r` directly regardless of traversal order. |
| **Correctness, verified against hand-computable cases** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A star network (every tie connects the degree-3 hub to a degree-1 leaf) gives `r = -1` exactly - a textbook case of perfect disassortativity, with zero room for a sign or scaling error to hide. A complete graph (every node has identical degree, zero variance) correctly returns missing rather than a spurious value, matching this package's own established convention for degree-undefined cases (`nwclustering`). `attribute()` mode verified against a hand-derived value (`r = -0.75`) on the same star network with a simple `1,2,3,4` attribute. A random Erdős-Rényi graph (n=10,000) gives `r ≈ 0.005`, matching the theoretical expectation of no degree correlation in a random graph. |
| **Measured performance** | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | n/a | n=10,000 (50k edges): 0.53 seconds - genuinely sparse from the start (built directly on `connected_neighbors()`, never materializing anything dense), no separate optimization pass needed. |
| Package registration | ✅ | n/a | n/a | ✅ | Added to `_pkg_ado.txt`/`_pkg_hlp.txt`/`nwcommands-ado1.pkg`/`nwcommands-hlp1.pkg` (alphabetically positioned, matching each file's own existing convention) and `nw_alphabetical.sthlp`/`nw_topical.sthlp` (under "Positions, Roles & Equivalence", alongside `nwburt`/`nwconstraint`/`nwcoreperiphery`/`nwsimilar`/`nwdissimilar` - the closest existing analogues). `.sthlp` generated via `nw_helpwriter`, not hand-written. |
| Scoped regression sweep | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a | New `cscripts/test_nwassortativity.do` (star/K4/attribute/directed/string-attribute-error cases). Full `cscripts/` suite (145 files with the new test) re-run in dev mode: 145/145 pass, zero regressions to any existing command (a purely additive change - new Mata method, new `.ado` file, no existing code path touched). |

## Pending (queued for implementation, not yet started)

|---|---|---|

Not attempted in unit 108 itself, given the scale of improvement already banked there (n=10,000: did not complete -> 103.5s) and the correctness stakes of continuing to modify a correctness-critical flow algorithm under time pressure. | Medium (a further, purely allocation-level optimization on top of an already-working, already-bounded command - not correctness-affecting) | Small-Medium (buffer-reuse only, no algorithmic change - lower risk than unit 108's own fixes, but still needs the same cross-validation rigor before trusting it) |

A bespoke sparse rewrite (n independent single-source BFS/Dijkstra passes, each row summed and discarded immediately, O(n) space instead of O(n^2)) would remove both the redundant dense copy and most of `nwgeodesic`'s own unneeded diameter/eccentricity postprocessing - but `nwcloseness` transparently forwards `nwgeodesic`'s full option surface (`alpha()`, `force`, `sym`, `unconnected()`), so a bespoke implementation risks silently diverging from any of that surface unless it replicates all of it faithfully. Deferred rather than attempted under the same time pressure that fixed unit 106's queue-concatenation bug, given the correctness stakes of quietly changing option semantics. | Medium (nwgeodesic/nwcloseness both now complete at n=10,000 per unit 106; this would be a further constant-factor and memory improvement, not a correctness fix) | Medium (needs to replicate nwgeodesic's full alpha/force/sym/unconnected option surface faithfully) |
| **A cluster of commands confirmed dense-matrix-dependent** (`nwcloseness`/`nwqap` via `nwtomata`'s own dense conversion) - found via unit 103's benchmark study. `nwissymmetric` (unit 110), `nwburt` (unit 111), `nwconstraint` (unit 112), `nwsimilar`/`nwdissimilar`/`nwcorrelate_nodes` (unit 113), and `nwtriads` (unit 114, via a genuine algorithm swap rather than vectorization) are done. `nwqap`'s own cost (repeated dense permutation + full-matrix Pearson correlation, `correlate_nets_rep()`) is inherent to the MRQAP permutation-test methodology itself (every permutation needs a fresh O(n^2) reshuffle and a correlation over ALL dyads, not just edges) - not a sparse-reformulation candidate the way the others were; likely an accept-as-is case similar to `nwspectral`, pending a closer look. | Medium (real, measured scaling limit; not correctness-affecting as-is) | Small (`nwcloseness` already scoped above; `nwqap` likely accept-as-is, pending confirmation) |
| **`nwcorrelate_nodes`'s zero-variance fallback uses the OUTGOING sums for its own cmin/cmax check in all three `context()` branches**, even inside the incoming-only and both-directions branches - found while rederiving `correlate_nodes()` from scratch for unit 113's own performance fix. Almost certainly a copy-paste artifact from the outgoing branch, never updated for the other two. Deliberately reproduced as-is (not fixed) in unit 113, since - unlike that same unit's two crash bugs - this branch does not error, so there is a real prior behavior to stay compatible with in a performance-only pass. | Low (only affects the rare zero-variance-node-pair fallback case, not the general formula) | Small (once someone decides what the "right" behavior actually is for the incoming/both cases) |
| ~~`BronKerbosch()`'s R/P/X sets are dense 0/1 indicator vectors over *all* n nodes at every recursion level~~ **`nwclique`'s own case fixed in unit 115** via a new sparse-index-list `BronKerboschSparse()`, scoped to `nwclique.ado`'s direct-tie use (n=10,000: did not complete -> 21.6s). `nwnclique.ado`/`nwnclan.ado`'s n-clique family still uses the original dense `BronKerbosch()` on their own n-step-reachability relation (a genuinely different "neighbor" concept `connected_neighbors()` cannot substitute for) - not yet confirmed to have the same scaling problem in practice (n-step reachability graphs tend to be much denser than the direct-tie graph, which may already force a small candidate-set count for other reasons; unmeasured). | Low-Medium (n-clique family's own scaling behavior at n=10,000 unmeasured, not confirmed broken) | Medium (would need its own sparse "n-step-reachability neighbor" abstraction, analogous to `connected_neighbors()` but for the n-clique family's own relation) |
| **Complete the N=10,000 benchmark tier** for the ~20 commands not reached in unit 103's study (`nwpath`/`nwreach`/`nwbridges`/`nwcommunity`/`nwmodularity`/`nwsimindex`/`nwconcor`/`nwdyads`/`nw2degree`/`nw2project`/`nwcug`/`nwqap`/`nwpermute`/generators/misc) - not confirmed problems, simply not reached given time constraints. `dev/benchmark_suite.do` is ready to extend directly. | Low-Medium (informational completeness for the performance report/journal writeup) | Small (the harness already exists) |
| ~~`nw2clustering.ado` fails with a reshape error (r9) on any bipartite network~~ **Fixed in harmonisation unit 117** - the real root cause was redundant duplicate rows from a chain of m:m merges against uniquely-keyed lookup tables (not a malformed `i()`-varlist as originally suspected - the varlist was correct, the data genuinely wasn't unique), fixed with a lossless `duplicates drop` after each of the five merge steps. The command's own performance (item 4 in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s "Top 10 existing features to improve": an O(N^4)-shaped Stata reshape chain) remains a separate, unaddressed item. | Low (the correctness bug is fixed; only the pre-existing performance concern remains) | Medium (sparse migration of the reshape chain, unrelated to this fix) |
| ~~`nwmixing.ado` fails a precondition check even against a standard setup~~ **Fixed in harmonisation unit 118** - three independent bugs (a naming mismatch against `nwtoedge`'s real `_ego`/`_alter` convention, an undereferenced pointer passed to the permutation-test helper, and a string-attribute crash in the underlying `tab ..., matcol()` call), all now fixed with new test coverage. | Done (unit 118) | Done |

Confirmed directly: `nwset, mat((0,0,0\0,0,0\0,0,0)) undirected name(x)` then `nwtriads x` now returns `_003==1` (correct - one all-null triad) with no error. | Harmonisation follow-on | Done (unit 114) |

A real, strongly-directional effect is now asserted in `cscripts/test_nwhomophily.do`. | Done (unit 119) | Done |

Originally found affecting 8 files, all downstream of the two real `nwuse.ado` bugs unit 98 fixed (the dead `nwcommands.org` host and the `newvalued("``v'")` typo) - now moot for those 8, since the calls those files make no longer fail at all. `test_nwimport.do` is the one file where this genuinely still applies: its UCINET-host fetch is not the file's last statement and remains uncaptured, so anything after it is still unverified by the ordinary sweep - not fixed here, since the underlying host (a third-party academic mirror, not ours) is confirmed unreachable, not merely slow. | Low (narrowed to one file, a `capture`-wrap away once someone chooses to accept that scope reduction) | Small |

`nw_helpwriter.ado`'s line-copy loop remains not robust to certain backtick-quote patterns in a `.ado` file's lines (doc header *or* program body — the loop macro-processes every line unconditionally before deciding whether to write it), and separately can *silently truncate* a `.sthlp` (no error at all) if a macro-mangled line happens to evaluate to the literal string `***/`, prematurely closing the copy. Deliberately not fixed at the mechanism level in this pass (harmonisation unit 16) — correctly escaping arbitrary source lines is a larger, separate, riskier change than the isolation fix that unit made; assessed and declined once already (see unit 16's own certified row). Four known, confirmed-safe files are affected: `nwimport.ado` (genuine program-body content triggers a hard "too few quotes" crash, but its own test independently fails anyway for an unrelated reason - a known baseline failure - so it was never going to get certified regardless); `nwplotjs.ado` (its embedded-JavaScript content eventually exceeds Stata's maximum macro/line length; confirmed via `git diff` to leave the file completely unmodified, not corrupted); `nwplot.ado` (found while modernising `nwplot`, harmonisation unit 33 - its own pre-existing Mata layout-algorithm code trips the same "too few quotes" crash, but only after the doc-header copy has already completed, confirmed via the crash location); and `nwcomponents.ado` (found while adding `nwkcomponents`, harmonisation unit 37 - its own pre-existing Mata body content trips the identical crash, same safe "after the doc header closes" pattern). In every one of these last two cases the `.sthlp` regeneration was verified to be complete and correct despite the crash - only the automatic "last certified" stamp was skipped (added by hand instead, since each file's own test genuinely passes). No audit has been done for whether any *other* file's doc header trips this - the 43-file bulk backfill in unit 16 happened not to hit it, but that doesn't rule it out elsewhere. | Stage 1 follow-on (infra) | Small-Medium |
